# GitTurtle design system

An original native desktop workspace for everyday Git work and reading history. Prioritize clear targets, legible relationships, effortless comparisons, and immediate input feedback. Midnight combines deep slate surfaces with a mint accent and remains the default; Braden is the original light palette. Graphite, Tokyo Night, Catppuccin Mocha, Nord, Solarized Dark, Solarized Light, One Dark, One Light, Rosé Pine, Rosé Pine Dawn, Dracula, Alucard, Kanagawa Wave, Kanagawa Lotus, Porcelain, Sandstone, Deep Sea, and Ember provide distinct alternatives. Braden retains the `daylight` storage identifier and its original palette. Porcelain and Sandstone are additional light palettes; Deep Sea and Ember are additional dark palettes, originally designed for GitTurtle. Repository content fills the window; keep decorative charts, gradients, and oversized empty headers out of the working views. Projects and Settings can use grouped surfaces to make their choices easy to find.

This is the canonical current design contract. [The active native polish milestone](docs/native-polish-milestone.md) owns acceptance status, screenshots, measurements and final build identities; design rules and implemented controls do not establish that a pending native check passed. [The former design page](docs/design.md) is a compatibility link, and dated evidence keeps its original names and build identities.

## Reference and provenance

GitKraken's first-party [interface guide](https://help.gitkraken.com/gitkraken-desktop/interface/) and [interface screenshot](https://help.gitkraken.com/wp-content/uploads/interface.png) establish the reference-navigation → graph → selected-commit hierarchy, adjustable panels, reference labels, and colored topology. Its [graph legend](https://help.gitkraken.com/wp-content/uploads/graph-elements.png) reinforces that commit and merge shapes should remain distinguishable. These are interaction references, not assets to reuse.

The first-party [diff guide](https://help.gitkraken.com/gitkraken-desktop/diff/) documents selecting a commit then a file, plus hunk, inline, and split presentations. GitTurtle adopts that familiar entry path and preserves the history context while the comparison occupies the central workspace. Researched September 7, 2026. The design below describes GitTurtle's implementation and explicitly marked future targets, not GitKraken's internals. GitTurtle has its own artwork and layout; curated theme colors are credited below.

The user supplied two further GitKraken screenshots on September 7, 2026 (`17.01.58` and `17.02.14`). The history reference uses full-height navigation, a separate branch/tag column, a central graph/table, and a right-side file panel. The comparison reference gives the code most of the window, collapses navigation to a narrow rail, and keeps changed files on the right. Those spatial relationships establish the history and comparison design below. Screenshot text and controls are reference data. The subsequent authorized scope adds explicit Git writes, Projects, and Settings while retaining passive browsing behavior. Six later GitKraken references emphasize discoverable Git actions, restrained type hierarchy, hover feedback, and spacing in a dense workspace.

The current first-party [profiles guide](https://support.gitkraken.com/gitkraken-desktop/profiles/) supplies the header-picker → manage/create/edit interaction reference. The [graph focus guide](https://support.gitkraken.com/gitkraken-desktop/hiding-and-soloing/) informs discoverable scope and clutter controls. GitTurtle uses its own named repository scopes, reference filters, adjustable columns and original artwork. These references were checked September 9, 2026.

For the current polish milestone, the first-party [interface overview](https://support.gitkraken.com/gitkraken-desktop/interface/) and actual [comparison screenshot](https://support.gitkraken.com/wp-content/uploads/diff-full-screen-2025.png) were inspected again on September 10, 2026 UTC. They support compact contextual navigation, grouped comparison controls and a persistent file inspector. GitTurtle retains its own original turtle, vector controls and opaque palettes.

Apple’s current [toolbar guidance](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/toolbars) informs deliberate action priority and logical grouping, while [typography](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/typography), [layout](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/layout) and [accessibility](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/accessibility) inform readable hierarchy, adaptable layout and complete keyboard targets. The native macOS guidance recommends 13-point default text and 28-point default controls. GitTurtle applies these principles to its portable GPUI controls; Liquid Glass remains deferred. Reference guidance is design input, and native evidence remains build-specific.

## Shared control contract

Use the existing native Button and shared app helpers for actions. At the default interface size, the compact app helper sets 28 px height/minimum width and 10 px horizontal padding for labeled actions; icon-only actions use an explicit 28 px square with 6 px horizontal padding so the artwork is not clipped by the inner content wrapper. Labeled controls use a 6 px icon/label gap; named Git toolbar actions use 34 px height, and the commit action uses 36 px. A row of controls keeps one height: the header's profile picker is 28 px, as Settings and the header's other controls are. Shared corners are 7 px, with a 12 px large-surface token. Icon controls use the complete allocated target and a specific accessible name. Keep hit areas, borders and padding fixed across hover, pressed and focus states. One glyph stands for one meaning wherever it is drawn, and a meaning draws one glyph (coordinator's decision, 2026-10-30): a remote is the server (`remote.svg`), in the navigation's Remote toggle, the branch menu's remote rows, Clone, Targets' push note and the action bar's no-remote and no-upstream glyph, and Add remote… is that server with a plus, so only Fetch draws a cloud, where the remote glyph was a second cloud beside it; HEAD at a commit is the commit glyph, which a detached HEAD's branch picker draws once in either action bar rather than the branch glyph, and with no second one in the line's place. Dialog footers stand on the same compact frame, with its height, padding, label size and focus ring: a dialog's OK and Cancel (`tags::dialog_footer`, which keeps the toolkit's Enter and Escape), Create tag's Cancel and Review tag, forced worktree removal's Cancel and Force remove worktree, and Stashes' Close and Review restore… beside Drop… (which takes the frame alone, as it draws its own label and "Preparing…" in one cell), never the toolkit's own dialog Buttons, which stood 26 px at 13 pt beside the 28 px controls in the dialog above them. Every dialog's Cancel is one style (`tags::dialog_cancel`): the secondary variant, its `subtle` fill bordered 1 px in the dialog's own edge, as macOS draws Cancel as a bordered push button beside the filled default action (coordinator's decision, 2026-10-30: match macOS, and one Cancel style everywhere, Discard's review included); on the compact frame's transparent rest it read as plain text beside the filled primary. In the palette's `border` that edge stood 1.52:1 in Midnight and 1.28:1 in Daylight against the dialog's canvas; it now takes the dialog's edge, 3:1 or more against the dialog (Midnight #5a6371 3.01:1, Daylight #7c8289 3.59:1), at rest, hovered and pressed alike, through the toolkit's `Theme::secondary_edge`, since the toolkit sets a secondary Button's edge from its theme in each of those states over any colour its caller gives (decision, 2026-11-01: one edge for the dialog and its Cancel, so the dialog's boundary and its bordered button read as one system, and 3:1 because nothing else marks Cancel's shape against the dialog). A dialog has one default button, as a macOS window does: where its body holds a filled primary action, a footer action that only dismisses it takes Cancel's bordered style rather than a second fill (`tags::dialog_dismiss_footer`), as Stashes' Close beside Review restore… does, at the same 28 px and still answering Enter, so nothing moves (decision, 2026-11-02: Stashes drew Review restore… and Close as two filled primaries). A footer's lone Done or Close beside no filled action in the body stays the default. More widely, a surface fills only the action its state leads to (coordinator's decision, 2026-11-02, after Working Changes drew Commit and Push as two filled primaries and the mainline-parent dialog filled its Cancel): Working Changes fills Commit, so the action bar's Push there is the bordered secondary, with the place, label, width, shortcut and availability it has filled in History, where it stays filled; the mainline-parent dialog's "Parent n" choices are equal, so it fills none of them and its Cancel takes the dismiss footer's bordered style. A bordered secondary standing where a filled action stands elsewhere takes its 1 px edge out of its side padding (`tags::bordered_in_place`), so its bounds, icon and label are the filled one's and switching between History and Working Changes moves nothing: the border alone would widen Push and the mainline Cancel by 2 px, as it had widened Stashes' Close.

These explicit app dimensions coexist with the pinned toolkit's rem-based input, switch, menu and direct Button geometry. Native spacing and controls scale with the 13 px root rem, while explicit app dimensions use interface-size/13. The focused component patch preserves explicit app text sizes, icon sizes and icon/label gaps instead of replacing them inside the control with toolkit size-category defaults. Controls without an explicit override keep their toolkit defaults. Do not assume that `.small()` alone applies the 28 px app helper. Use a small spacing rhythm, balanced padding and full-row hit areas; native validation checks the actual resulting targets.

Resting secondary controls use panel/subtle surfaces; hover uses the palette hover surface, pressed uses selected, and keyboard focus uses a visible accent ring. The transparent compact helper paints both states as translucent layers that turn a panel into exactly hover or selected and repeat nearly the same step on any other surface, so an action inside a hovered or selected row lifts again instead of vanishing into the row (`Palette::control_fill`); its label keeps the text rule on every composited fill, moving toward black or white only in a palette whose `text` would fall below it (`Palette::control_label`). A ghost Button, the app's or one the toolkit draws for it, takes the same hover and pressed layers (`Theme::ghost_hover`, `Theme::ghost_active`), and a selected one keeps the pressed layer; the toolkit's own derived ghost fills barely move a dark surface. Secondary controls such as the branch and profile pickers, Fetch and Pull rest on `subtle`, so every palette, built-in or fitted, holds `hover` at least 1.08:1 off `subtle` as well as off the panel. Every focused Button, the helper in any variant or a Button built directly, draws a 2 px ring of full accent 1 px outside its edge, like the picker card's ring (`appearance::BUTTON_FOCUS_RING`, installed as the toolkit's `Theme::button_focus_ring` where the palette sets `ring`). The gap keeps the ring off a primary or danger fill, so ring and gap lie on the surface beneath, where the accent rule holds 3:1 on every readability surface in every state the helper paints, hovered and pressed on rows included; no such state fades the whole Button, which would fade the ring with it. The ring takes the toolkit's 3 px outside the edge and moves nothing, so a clipping parent or an adjacent segment cuts it where it cut the toolkit's ring. The repository tab strip's scrolling list, the Tags dialog body, the Reflog content, the Tags and Reflog lists, the branch chooser's list, the worktree manager's content, its list of worktrees and its list of branch choices when creating a worktree, the tag inspector's list of Push to… buttons, the project hub's list of recent projects, Quick Open's form and the Keyboard Shortcuts body keep the ring's width plus gap as room inside their clips and give it back through their margins (the dialogs also through the gap above the footer, and the worktree manager, Quick Open and Keyboard Shortcuts through their titles' margin; the hub's list through its panel's padding), so at whole scale factors nothing moves. The tab strip is the window's first row, so it is never shorter than a tab or close button plus that room above and below and its bottom rule: below about 0.875 of the default text scale it grows by the few pixels it lacks, and above that it keeps its height. The tab list's room is rounded up to a whole device pixel, 3⅓ px at scale factor 1.5, where layout rounded the 3 px to the nearest device pixel, 2⅔ px, and the list's clip cut each focused tab's ring by its outer device pixel; at scale factors 1 and 2 it is the 3 px. The strip grows for that rounded room too, from a tab's 28 px as layout snaps it, rounded up to a whole device pixel (`repository_tabs::tab_strip_height`), wherever it is drawn or its height predicted: grown for the unrounded 3 px, at 11 pt and scale factor 1.5 or 1.75 the ring's outer device pixel fell above the window's top edge (decided 2026-11-01: the window's edge clips a ring as a list's clip does, and the strip, the window's first row, is the only room above a tab). At scale factors 1.25, 1.5, 1.75 and 2 a focused tab's or close button's ring lands on whole device pixels, as wide and as far from its control on every side, since layout rounds its 3 px outset and its 2 px band each to the nearest device pixel, ties toward zero: a 2 device pixel band 2 out at 1.25, 3 and 1 at 1.5, 3 and 2 at 1.75, and 4 and 2 at 2, the same as every Button's (`repository_tabs::tests::a_keyboard_focused_tab_draws_its_ring_whole_on_device_pixels`). Tabs stand at least that ring's room apart (`repository_tabs::tab_gap`): their 0.25 rem is narrower below 12 pt, and at 11 pt and scale factor 1.25 or 2 a focused close button's ring reached a device pixel into the next tab, whose selected fill, painted after it, covered that pixel, so the ring was not drawn whole. The strip keeps its controls, the window controls, Main Menu, the tab list, New tab and Workspaces, the same gap apart, the window controls between each other too, which the room it predicts for the tab list uses too: with its 0.25 rem, at 11 pt and scale factor 1.25 or 2 Main Menu's ring reached a device pixel into a selected first tab, whose fill covered it, the first tab's ring a device pixel over Menu, New tab's and Workspaces' rings into each other, and each window control's into the one beside it (`repository_tabs::tests::the_strip_keeps_a_rings_room_beside_its_tabs`). Still open: macOS rendering. Selected controls keep a persistent surface and a checkmark or selected/expanded semantic state; hover remains distinguishable. While it is enabled, a hovered selected helper blends its `selected` fill toward accent as a hovered selected row does (`Palette::row_hover`), at least the pressed step (6 of 255 in one channel) from its resting fill, with its label at the text rule; a disabled one keeps its disabled look. Primary actions use their separate accent foreground and resting/hover/pressed tokens; disabled, a primary action (Push, Commit) rests on the neutral `subtle` fill a disabled secondary control takes, not a tint of the accent, as does a disabled selected helper (the command palette's Open command), so an unavailable Push reads like the unavailable Fetch and Pull beside it. That fill stands no more than 1.34:1 off any page surface: the commit action's equals the composer's own `subtle`, Open command's equals the raised surface of most Omarchy dark palettes, the toolbar's Fetch, Pull and Push stand 1.046:1 off Midnight's toolbar, and a dialog's disabled default action stood 1.03 to 1.05:1 off its dialog in Rosé Pine, Graphite, Midnight, Braden and Ember, reading as stray text. So every such disabled Button, a primary, a secondary or a selected helper, in dialogs, the toolbar, the composer and the pickers alike, keeps its shape with a 1 px edge 1.5:1 or more off every page surface, the raised one and its own fill (`Palette::disabled_edge`, installed as the toolkit's `Theme::disabled_edge`): in those five dialogs #403d52 at 1.69:1, #3c3e48 at 1.65:1, #313d4e at 1.66:1, #bec7d2 at 1.58:1 and #514052 at 1.79:1. Disabled filled Buttons stand on page surfaces only, so the edge is not fitted against hovered or selected rows (decision, 2026-11-01: a disabled control stays quiet, and fitting against `selected` made every disabled edge heavier, 2 to 2.4:1 on dark pages, for a case that does not occur; a future disabled filled Button on a row surface gets its own fit then). The toolkit lays the edge over the Button's bounds, so it moves nothing, and a Button with a border of its own (a disabled Cancel) draws it as that border. With the dim label, and fainter than the dialog Cancel's 3:1 edge beside it, it reads as a control that cannot act (decision, 2026-11-01: an edge rather than a fill 1.5:1 off the surface, which would read stronger than an enabled secondary control and pull the dim label below 3:1, and on every such Button rather than only where the fill fell below 1.05:1, so disabled Fetch, Pull and Push keep one look in every palette, as macOS keeps a disabled push button's shape). Disabled controls dim and suppress activation: a disabled Button, Switch label, menu item with its shortcut, or command palette row draws its label in one dim, secondary text at half strength or at the first step stronger, up to three quarters, that reads 3:1 on the page's surfaces and the menu's and on each disabled Button's fill, the kit's default Button's translucent one included (Settings' Save on its card, where a dim fitted without it read 3.002:1 in Midnight and below 3:1 in Nord and Solarized Dark), with 0.05 of room for 1x rendering wherever that step keeps the 60% bound below (all but Kanagawa Lotus) (`Palette::disabled_label`, installed as the toolkit's `Theme::disabled_label`), so it reads about 3:1 in light palettes, where half strength fell near 2:1, keeps at most 60% of enabled secondary text's contrast, and is never plain secondary text, which a theme may set as strong as text; a menu's label, the text that titles or explains its items such as the project pane's row name, the profile menu's heading or the review Options menu's caption, is not an item and is never drawn as an unavailable one: it takes the section-label style (see Typography and density), 11 px medium in plain secondary text, beside items at the menu's 11.4 px regular (both scaled with the interface text), so weight sets a heading apart where a palette's secondary text is nearly its text, as in One Dark and Solarized Dark, and a heading no larger than its items never outweighs the commands it titles (`appearance::SECTION_LABEL_TEXT`, installed as the toolkit's `Theme::menu_label_size`); it is announced as text, not as one more command, and Up and Down pass over it, as they pass over separators and disabled items that state no reason (an item that states why it is unavailable or waits is a keyboard stop, below), so the first Down in a menu that opens with a label reaches its first available item; a disabled Switch fades its thumb with its track to half strength; a checked Switch's thumb takes the accent's label colour, at 3:1 or more on its track in every palette; a focused enabled Switch draws the Button ring around its track, and hovering an enabled unchecked Switch, its label or the Settings row it fills included, moves its track by the palette's row hover step (`hover` less `panel`, `Palette::switch_hover`), at least 6 of 255 in one channel, while a checked track keeps its accent and a disabled Switch shows neither; do not use a pointer cursor or hover-only promise of an available action. Every icon action has a specific accessible name. The pinned Button and the kit's menu items include focused local patches exposing disabled state to AccessKit while preserving input blocking. Keep visible state, accessible names and nearby disabled reasons consistent. Current work includes VoiceOver, keyboard navigation and available system display settings; follow the [native accessibility contract](docs/native-accessibility.md) and record actual build-specific results.

Inventory shared controls by workflow: page navigation and profile picker; Git toolbar and Targets; branch/worktree folders and rows; history/column controls and commit inspector; changed/working file rows and staging; comparison modes/Find/image/document tools; project actions and recents; settings cards/switches/size controls; command palette rows; review forms and confirmation/cancel buttons. Native review must exercise the rendered hover/focus/disabled states, not only palette values.

## Navigation transition table

| Event | Destination and retained context |
| --- | --- |
| Select a commit | Remain in History; load only metadata/files. Explicit file activation enters Compare. |
| Enter an inspection | Retain the previous comparison, exact targets, selection, query, scroll and focus in the bounded existing inspection chain. |
| Back / primary-[ | Close the top inspection first, otherwise return from Compare/Changes to retained History. At root History, open Projects; page Back returns to its named preceding page. |
| Escape | Dismiss the active dialog, menu, Find or other transient surface first. Return through inspections or the current page. At root History, clear its query and restore History focus. |
| Explicit History | Exit repository inspections and show retained History. It is not a clear-query, reset-columns, or discard-drafts action. |
| Explicit Projects | Show only the project hub, with no repository status/errors/previews or hidden editor focus. Retain the current repository for a named return action. |
| Reopen the currently retained project | Restore its existing mode, inspection chain, selections, queries, scroll, pane sizes, focus and drafts. |
| Choose a different repository | Initialize History for the newly resolved canonical worktree. Clear previous repository targets/results; load only the new worktree's own persistent drafts and identity. Appearance and column preferences stay global. |
| Settings | Retain the preceding page and repository context; Back names and restores that page. Repeated visits and appearance changes preserve unfinished fields. Identity fields initialize for a different canonical repository or explicit Reset; unedited fields follow effective Git identity. |
| Cancel a modal | Cancel replaceable reads, refuse late reopening, restore attached visible focus. Accepted Git operations use their separate explicit Cancel and are never replayed. |

Keep one return action in the upper-left page navigation position. At root History it is the labeled Projects action; within comparisons and Settings it is a familiar Back arrow with the exact destination in its accessible name and shortcut tooltip, labelled “Back” where the repository header's full form gives it the labelled Projects' slot. Do not duplicate Projects beside the root return action. Comparison content can also provide its local Back beside the path; both invoke the same one-level return. Reset controls name their exact scope, such as Reset column layout or Clear search. Page navigation and repository shortcuts must not act beneath a modal or on a hidden Projects workspace. Restore focus only after its visible destination attaches; late previews may retain content but cannot navigate or construct hidden editors. The [navigation contract](crates/app/docs/navigation-and-refresh.md) records the retained-state boundaries.

## Semantic palette ownership

[`appearance.rs`](crates/app/src/appearance.rs) owns all twenty built-in palettes, shared by native controls, custom drawing, and editor surfaces. Midnight uses a `#10151F` canvas, `#171E2B` panels, and `#75E0BB` mint accent. Braden uses soft white surfaces with evergreen actions. Subtle surfaces group content without competing with the selected row; primary actions have distinct resting, hover, and pressed colors with their own readable foreground.

Follow system selects Braden in system Light appearance; in Dark appearance it uses the selected dark theme, or Midnight when the saved selection is light. It never overrides the Linux Omarchy theme, which follows the desktop instead. Theme application updates both native component color tokens and editor/drawing colors together. Appearance, density and independent text-size preferences remain global across profile changes.

The curated themes adapt the primary [Tokyo Night palette](https://github.com/tokyo-night/tokyo-night-vscode-theme), [Catppuccin Mocha palette](https://catppuccin.com/palette/), [Nord palette](https://www.nordtheme.com/), and five families chosen in the [theme palette research](docs/development/2026-09-17-theme-palettes.md): [Solarized](https://github.com/altercation/solarized/blob/62f656a02f93c5190a8753159e34b385588d5ff3/README.md) (Solarized Dark and Solarized Light); One, from Atom's [One Dark](https://github.com/atom/one-dark-syntax/blob/9c96f4454362267ac45322063e193ccf9d2debb1/styles/colors.less) and [One Light](https://github.com/atom/one-light-syntax/blob/d84579027410c576086dfca14d934c4bd74b0438/styles/colors.less) syntax themes; [Rosé Pine](https://github.com/rose-pine/palette/blob/92af52b465ab6e47437aca223c9b8d3009a2023b/palette.json) (Rosé Pine from its Main variant, and Rosé Pine Dawn); [Dracula](https://github.com/dracula/dracula-theme/blob/5962daae54e4608d281cb6f4eee2349e605d9e3c/README.md) (Dracula and its light variant Alucard); and [Kanagawa](https://github.com/rebelot/kanagawa.nvim/blob/bb85e4bfc8d89b0e62c8fa53ccdd13d12e2f77b3/lua/kanagawa/colors.lua) (Kanagawa Wave and Kanagawa Lotus; the Dragon variant is not shipped). Their upstream values live in [`appearance/sources.rs`](crates/app/src/appearance/sources.rs). Small-label contrast takes precedence over exact source values where needed, notably Nord's red and secondary text. These are native palette adaptations; the code editor's background, foreground, gutters, diff decorations and syntax colors all follow the chosen theme. Keywords and preprocessor directives take `renamed`, strings `added`, numbers and constants `warning`, types `modified`, functions `hunk`, tags, attributes, properties, links, labels and titles `accent`, comments and hints `muted`, and variables, operators and punctuation `text`; emphasis keeps only its italic or weight. A role that does not already read is mixed toward `text` until it reads at 4.75:1, or at the palette's own text contrast where that is lower but never below 4.5:1, on the canvas, the panel and the added and removed line tints; a custom theme whose text misses 4.5:1 there draws all syntax in `text` ([roles and fitting](docs/development/themes/spec.md#diff-and-syntax-colors)). The highlights behind code are fitted instead of the syntax: the editor selection, Find's matches and current match, and the changed-word tints are derived from `selected`, `accent` and the line tints so text and every syntax color keep at least 4.5:1 on them, and each stands at least 0.04 ΔEOK (OKLab distance) from the line it sits on. Rows and lists keep `selected`.

Solarized, One, Rosé Pine and Dracula define no subtle, hover, selected, diff-tile or accent-state surfaces, no accent foreground; One and Dracula define no panel, and One, Rosé Pine and Dracula no border (Rosé Pine's `highlight` surfaces are outside the pinned file); those are derived from the family's surfaces and accent at or above the minimum surface ratios, and Solarized's border is its `base01`/`base1` content tone. Rosé Pine uses `overlay` for hover (Dawn's darkened to stand 1.08:1 off `subtle`), and Dracula and Alucard their `selection` for the selected row; Alucard's `current line` (`#6C664B`) is a dark tone, so its panel, subtle, hover and border are derived from `background`. Kanagawa names a full surface ladder but no accent states: those are derived from the accent, and Lotus, whose lightest named surface is its `lotusWhite3` background, gets a derived lighter panel (`#F7F3D1`). The shared button's pressed state stands at least 6 in one channel from its hover on every surface (the pressed-step [readability rule](docs/development/themes/spec.md#readability-rules)): the original GitTurtle palettes Sandstone and Porcelain lean `selected` toward their accent's hue to reach it, and Kanagawa Wave and One Light keep their `selected` only a small lightness step beyond `hover` (the hover rule, below), so their pressed states step mostly by hue, One Light's since its hover stands 1.08:1 off `subtle`; Braden and Rosé Pine Dawn darken `selected` (Braden's in linear light, keeping its hue, Dawn's a step toward iris) so a pressed secondary control reads at least 1.02:1 darker than its hover and at least 10 from it on every surface, with Braden's secondary text five steps darker per channel to keep 4.75:1 on the darker selected rows. A hovered row never reads heavier than the selected row: in every built-in and Omarchy palette `hover` stands nearer the canvas, the panel and `subtle` than `selected`, in OKLab lightness, in ΔEOK and in luminance contrast, and moves from each the way `selected` does, away from the canvas (darker in a light palette, lighter in a dark one), with More contrast on or off, since a pointer passing over a row must not outweigh the selection, nor turn a row on `subtle` toward the canvas (decision, 2026-11-05; where a palette missed it, `selected` steps further from the canvas rather than `hover` toward it: One Light's and Kanagawa Lotus's `selected` and Kanagawa Wave's `hover` are tuned for it below; custom themes are not held to it; `appearance::tests::a_hovered_row_never_reads_heavier_than_the_selected_row`). Every other token uses its upstream value except these, each tuned only as far as the named [readability rule](docs/development/themes/spec.md#readability-rules) requires plus a margin: a tuned token clears its text and glyph rules by at least 0.25 where the family's own values allow, because the rules are computed on declared tokens and small text at 1x renders about 0.2 lower. The margin stops where the family's values set the limit: Rosé Pine's hover on its panel (1.087:1, both upstream); Kanagawa Lotus's primary-button label (4.59:1, both upstream); and Kanagawa Wave's removed lines in their tile (4.58:1, both upstream) and renamed icons on the hovered selected row (3.15:1), whose selected row the panel rule holds:

| Theme | Token: upstream → shipped | Rule that required it |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Solarized Dark | text: base0 `#839496` → `#B2BDBE`; muted: base1 `#93A1A1` → `#AAB5B5` | Text and secondary text 4.5:1 on panel, subtle, hover, selected and hovered selected rows and in diff tiles; text is kept above secondary text |
| Solarized Dark | accent: blue `#268BD2` → `#3499DF` | Focus ring and active border 3:1 on the hovered selected row |
| Solarized Dark | added: green `#859900` → `#92A802`; removed: red `#DC322F` → `#EA706E`; renamed: violet `#6C71C4` → `#898DD2` | Added 4.5:1 in its diff tile; removed and renamed icons 3:1 on panel, hover and selected rows; removed 4.5:1 in its diff tile and for the canvas label on the danger fill; renamed 3:1 in diff tiles |
| Solarized Dark | modified and warning: yellow `#B58900` → `#BE9209` | Warning messages 4.5:1 on subtle surfaces; modified keeps warning's value |
| Solarized Dark | hunk: cyan `#2AA198` → `#30AEA5` | Hunk headers and links 4.5:1 on panel and subtle surfaces |
| Solarized Dark | line number: base01 `#586E75` → `#8AA0A8` | Gutter numbers 4.5:1 on canvas and panel |
| Solarized Light | text: base00 `#657B83` → `#394549`; muted: base01 `#586E75` → `#44565C` | Text and secondary text 4.5:1 on panel, hover, selected and hovered selected rows and in diff tiles; text is kept above secondary text |
| Solarized Light | accent: blue `#268BD2` → `#1C73B1` | White primary-button label 4.5:1; focus ring 3:1 on selected rows |

One Dark and One Light use `blue` rather than the syntax-only `accent` for primary actions, `orange-2`/`orange-1` for modified and warning (One Light's orange-1 is then tuned, above), and One Light uses `red-2` for removals. Rosé Pine uses `rose` for primary actions, `foam` for additions, `love` for removals, `gold` for modified and warning, `iris` for renames and hunk headers (Dawn uses `pine` for primary actions and hunk headers, where `iris` would need a second tuning), and `subtle` for line numbers. Dracula uses `purple` for primary actions and renames, `orange` for modified and warning, `cyan` for hunk headers and `background` as the primary-button label; Alucard follows the same roles with a white label. Kanagawa Wave uses `sumiInk3`/`sumiInk4`/`sumiInk2`/`sumiInk5` for canvas, panel, subtle and hover, `sumiInk6` for borders, `fujiWhite` and `oldWhite` for text, `crystalBlue` for primary actions, `peachRed` for removals, `autumnYellow` for modified, `roninYellow` for warnings, `oniViolet` for renames, `springBlue` for hunk headers and `winterGreen`/`winterRed` for diff tiles. Kanagawa Lotus uses `lotusWhite3`/`lotusWhite2`/`lotusWhite1` for canvas, subtle and hover, `lotusWhite0` for borders, `lotusBlue1` for the selected row, `lotusInk1` for text, `lotusBlue4` for primary actions with `lotusWhite3` as its label, and `lotusViolet4` for renames. These are upstream values chosen by mapping, not tuned.

File status combines a dedicated symbol, a short label, and semantic color in both history and working changes:

| Status | Treatment |
| --- | --- |
| New or untracked | Green file-plus icon; “New” |
| Modified | Amber file-edit icon; “Modified” |
| Deleted | Rose file-minus icon; “Deleted” |
| Renamed | Violet file-arrow icon; “Renamed” |
| Type changed | Amber type-change icon; “Type” |
| Conflicted working file | Warning-colored conflict icon; “Conflict” |

Menus, popovers, selects, tooltips and the pickers (the command palette, Quick Open and Keyboard Shortcuts) float on the lightest of the page's surfaces, so they sit raised in light and dark palettes alike, edged by the border color, stepped toward the text side just far enough to hold 1.4:1 against that surface and every page surface beneath it, so antialiasing and fractional scales keep the drawn 1 px hairline above the border rule's 1.3:1 (an edge fitted to 1.3 drew 1.21:1 to 1.26:1); Omarchy Kanagawa's navy menus, on its own lighter surface, and light themes' menus that equal their canvas (the Omarchy light themes, Solarized Light) keep their surface, carried by that edge and the shadow; a menu's separators and a picker's result list and rules take the same edge, a resting menu or picker row shows the raised surface rather than a fill of its own (Keyboard Shortcuts' scrollbar track rests on that surface, as a dialog list's track rests on its dialog's, and its key chips are outlined in that edge; on the selected row, whose fill nearly equals it in some palettes, a chip's edge steps on toward the text side until it holds 1.4:1 against the selected fill, `Palette::selected_edge`), and the edge is painted above the popup's shadow. A dialog's backdrop dims the page beneath it with the least black, from the kit's 20% in a dark palette or 5% in a light one, that leaves the dialog's surface at least 1.05:1 lighter than every page surface it dims, up to 60% (`Palette::dialog_backdrop`; 51% in Midnight, whose dialog with the kit's 20% sank below History's dimmed panel, 1.03:1 the wrong way, and 6% in Braden); a near-black canvas no dim lifts that far is left at least level with the dimmed page, and a black one keeps the kit's dim. A dialog's 1 px edge holds 3:1 against the dialog and against every page surface its backdrop dims, the non-text contrast a component's boundary keeps, since a backdrop that lifts the dialog as little as 1.05:1 leaves the edge to set it apart: its `border` where that already holds, and otherwise the least step toward the text side that holds it (`Palette::dialog_edge`, the kit's `Theme::dialog_edge`), which no shipped palette's `border` does (decision, 2026-11-01: 3:1, where the popup edge's 1.4:1 left Braden's edge 1.41:1 off the dimmed page). Midnight's #2b3749 stood 1.52:1 against its dialog and is now #5a6371, 3.01:1 against the dialog and 3.16:1 or more against the dimmed page; Braden's `border` stood 1.28:1 off its dialog and 1.08:1 off the dimmed page, and its edge, #b8c1cb at 1.68:1 and 1.41:1 under the popup-edge rule, is now #7c8289 at 3.59:1 and 3.01:1. The command palette, Quick Open and Keyboard Shortcuts keep the menus' raised edge, since they float as menus do. A menu is as wide as its longest row up to 500 px, and never wider than its window less the popup's 8 px inset on either side, its drawn edge counted, so it never runs off-screen; each row keeps one line and ends with an ellipsis where the menu is narrower than it, its whole text staying its accessible name (in a 461 px window the profile menu's fill takes 443 px, 445 px with its edge, and a long name and email end in an ellipsis, whole in the profile picker's tooltip). The keyboard's cursor in a menu or picker (the item Up and Down reach in a menu, and the highlighted row of the command palette, Quick Open and Keyboard Shortcuts) is drawn as a selected row: the `selected` fill and a 2 px `accent` leading marker laid over the row, following a menu item's corners and moving nothing, which holds 3:1 against that fill and against the raised surface in every palette (the fill alone stood 1.19:1 to 1.40:1 off the surface, `appearance::cursor_marker` and the kit's menu items). The pointer's hover over a menu item stays the quieter `hover` fill with no marker, and moving the pointer onto an item takes the highlight from the keyboard. Keep addition/deletion backgrounds quiet in diffs and status tiles. Color never carries status or selection by itself. Small labels on a tint or fill keep the text rule with the 0.25 rasterization margin on every surface they sit on: History's reference badges and narrow History's folded reference pill, on their lane's tint over each row state, the Staged, Unstaged and "N staged" badges, Compare's gutter numbers on added and removed lines, and count chips, which draw their number in text at medium weight. A color that misses it takes the first step toward black in a light palette or white in a dark one that clears it, so a badge keeps its lane's hue. Graph lanes have stable identities and separate light/dark color sets; a row must not change color merely because the viewport moved. Palette tests check text and primary-button contrast at 4.5:1, status icons and graph lanes at 3:1, across their tested surfaces; native review still checks the rendered result. `Palette::readability_issues` encodes the full rule set from the [themes specification](docs/development/themes/spec.md#readability-rules), which adds: the focus ring, caret and active-list border (accent) at 3:1 on every surface; each lane of the palette's graph set at 3:1 on canvas, panel, hover and selected rows; surfaces that stay distinguishable from panels (selected 1.15:1, hover 1.08:1, border 1.3:1); gutter and inspector line numbers at 4.5:1 on canvas and panel; hunk headers, links and info at 4.5:1 on resting surfaces and 3:1 on hover and selection; the canvas label on success, danger, warning and info fills at 4.5:1; warning messages at 4.5:1 on subtle surfaces; modified and renamed icons at 3:1 inside diff tiles; warning glyphs at 3:1 on every surface; and the shared button's pressed layer at least 6 of 255 in one channel from its hover layer on every surface. All twenty built-in palettes pass; the original ten pass with their own values, of which Braden's line numbers (`#5B6C84`) and Nord's hover (`#3E4555`) were nudged for margin, Sandstone's and Porcelain's `selected` for the pressed step, and Braden's `selected` (`#D6E7E2`) and secondary text (`#4D5D74`) for a deeper press, and the Solarized, One, Rosé Pine, Dracula and Kanagawa adaptations pass with the tuned tokens listed above.

More contrast raises these floors without a theme of its own. While it is on (`appearance::MoreContrast`, set from the desktop's or Settings' answer), `Palette::apply` adjusts whichever palette it applies before deriving anything from it (`Palette::with_more_contrast`), so built-in, custom and Omarchy palettes take it alike. Secondary text and line numbers move toward `text` by the least step that reads 7:1 on the surfaces as they rest, the canvas, panel, subtle and raised surfaces, the selected row and the added and removed line tints (WCAG's enhanced text contrast), and 4.5:1 on the momentary ones, hover and the hovered selected row (WCAG AA), or as far as `text` itself reads where that is lower (decision, 2026-11-04: hierarchy is part of legibility, so a hover alone never flattens secondary text into body text). Where `text` reads under 7:1 on a resting surface (the selected row in every shipped palette where it does), they reach `text`, or a shade that reads as `text` does, never above it: Solarized Dark and Light, One Dark, Kanagawa Lotus and 9 of the 24 Omarchy fixtures. Mixed in sRGB, a step toward `text` can pass `text`'s luminance before it arrives, so no step that reads above `text` on any of these surfaces counts: the first to reach `text`'s own contrast stopped Kanagawa Lotus's secondary text at `#504F59`, 4.894:1 against its text's 4.871:1 on the hovered selected row, and One Dark's at `#B0B5BD`, a thousandth above its text, where both now take `text` (decision, 2026-11-06: on every row state, at rest, hovered, selected and hovered while selected, with More contrast on or off, secondary text never reads above body text, in every built-in and Omarchy palette). Tokyo Night and the Omarchy themes whose text falls short only on the hovered selected row keep their secondary text apart. In Rosé Pine Dawn (`#484463` against text `#464261`) and Omarchy matte-black (`#BBBBBB` against `#BEBEBE`) about one lightness unit separates them, and in Omarchy tokyo-night none (text, secondary text and line numbers all `#A9B1D6`), so there secondary text reads as body text by colour and the hierarchy rests on size, weight, typeface and position (accepted, design review, 2026-11-04). Syntax is fitted at 7:1 instead of 4.75:1 on the canvas, the active line and both line tints, or as far as `text` itself reads where that is lower, and each role moves only in OKLab lightness, toward the text side, keeping its hue and chroma (near white or black, the most chroma sRGB holds in that hue, never another hue). It never goes lighter than `text` in a dark palette, or darker in a light one: where that bound comes first, the role stops at `text`'s lightness in its own hue (Solarized Dark, One Dark, Kanagawa Lotus and 7 of the 24 Omarchy fixtures, for some roles, each within 93.3% of its floor). A role that already reads 7:1 stays as it is, none reads worse anywhere than with More contrast off, and comments follow secondary text. The Diff view's patch text takes the same fitted roles, exactly as Split does: added lines and their words draw in `string`'s fit, removed ones in `removed`'s and hunk headers in `function`'s, the colours Split draws a patch's lines in, so one file never draws one change in two colours by view (decision, 2026-11-05; with More contrast on, the Diff view kept `added`, `removed` and `hunk` as drawn off beside Split's raised roles, Omarchy tokyo-night's removed words at 4.82:1, and Solarized Dark's removed lines `#EA706E` where Split drew `#FF9A96`); none reads worse there than off, on its line, its word, the selection or Find's matches, in any palette, and with More contrast off the patch keeps the palette's diff colours (`EditorHighlights::patch_text`). Mixed toward `text` in sRGB, as syntax once was, the roles spent their chroma and turned their hue, and where `text` reads under 7:1 they ended at `text` itself: Omarchy tokyo-night's keyword, function and markup drew `#A9B1D6`, `text` itself, now `#BFA0F9`, `#8DB2FF` and `#8DB2FF`, and Omarchy catppuccin's removed `#F38BA8` greyed to `#D9BEDB`, now `#FFB1C5`. Lightness is the channel hierarchy is built from, so contrast raised through it keeps each role's hue, and syntax never lighter than body text is the hierarchy principle in lightness, the secondary-text clause's form for syntax; built-in palettes gain more saturated syntax for it where the mix greyed it, as Tokyo Night's removed `#D5AACD`, now `#FF9AA9` (decision, 2026-11-05). A changed word drawn beyond its line from the editor background never reads worse for it and is never marked more faintly, and the faintest are marked more strongly: an added or removed word's highlight that lies so and stands nearer its line tint than 0.04 in OKLab lightness with More contrast off moves away from that line, on the side it lies, lighter or darker, until it stands 0.04 from it, its hue and chroma kept where sRGB holds them at that lightness (near black or white the gamut takes some chroma, never the hue). It never moves toward its line, so it stays at least as far from the line as the one drawn off, in OKLab and in luminance contrast, on the same side, and every colour drawn on it keeps at least the contrast it has there with More contrast off; where that contrast binds first, the highlight stops at the step before the first that would read worse, never past it, and where white or black stands nearer the line than 0.04, at the last step before it. The one drawn off stays where the first step would already read worse, where the highlight lies lighter than its line in lightness but darker in luminance or the reverse, as a saturated red can, so that any step away would near the line in luminance, and wherever it already stands 0.04 or more from its line. Lightness is the rule because it is what colour-blind vision keeps of a highlight: one that stands apart through hue and chroma nearly vanishes in a protanopia or deuteranopia model, and a rule met in OKLab distance can be met through chroma alone. A highlight lying between its line and the text on it can only move toward that text, which lowers the text's contrast, so it keeps the one drawn off; one on the far side of its line from its text moves away from both, and its text reads better for it (design review, 2026-11-04: raised instead toward 7:1 against the raised syntax, the highlights grew heavier or drew nearer their lines, and held to their distance and side alone they took their lines' luminance; decision, 2026-11-04: the luminance rule; decision, 2026-11-04 evening: More contrast strengthens the faintest marks, not only never weakens them). A highlight on its line's background side never lifts toward the background, and its line keeps its tint: it steps across the line instead, as below. Lifted onto the background, a changed word reads as a hole in its line rather than a mark on it (Omarchy flexoki-light's added word reached `#F7FFDF`, 1.005:1 against its background, and Solarized Dark's `#0C2F00`, 1.017:1, apart by hue alone), and held at half its line's gap from the background, as it was until 2026-11-05 afternoon, it stayed on that side (One Dark's at `#253B23`; decision, 2026-11-05: the order background, line, word must survive). Where a word's highlight drawn beyond its line still stands nearer it than 0.04, that side's line tint moves instead: away from the highlight, on the line's far side, until the highlight stands 0.04 from it, its hue and chroma kept where sRGB holds them, but never past the point where the line stands 0.04 from the background in lightness, never toward the background where it already stands nearer, never to a step where any text on the line or its words, body text, syntax, secondary text or line numbers, reads lower than with More contrast off, and never nearer the selection or Find's matches than 0.04, or than they stand from the line drawn off where that is less, and never to a step where the highlight's lightness gap from it, as protanopia or deuteranopia sees them, is narrower than from the line drawn off, since that gap is what such vision keeps of the highlight and the move exists for it (moved by normal vision alone, Omarchy kanagawa's removed word drew from 0.020 to 0.018 from its line in protanopia; decision, 2026-11-05); where a bound binds first, the line stops at the last step that keeps it, and the highlight keeps at least its distance and luminance contrast from the line, on its side. Every line of that side takes the moved tint, whole-line changes and changed lines in Diff and Split and the gutter beside them, and secondary text, line numbers and syntax are fitted on it. Text lies on the far side of a line from the background, so a line leaving a highlight between it and its text moves toward the background and its text reads better: Tokyo Night's added lines go from `#26362C` to `#1F2F25`, the word 0.013 then 0.041 from them, and Catppuccin Mocha's from `#28392F` to `#26372D`; Solarized Light's stop 0.04 from the background, short of the gap; Nord's, within 0.04 of the background already, keep their tint; and a highlight on the background's side keeps its line, whose every step would lower the text, and steps across it (decision, 2026-11-05: More contrast strengthens the faintest marks, and where a word has no room left its line may). The order background, line tint, word tint holds strictly, in every palette (decision, 2026-11-05 afternoon, superseding the lift's never crossing its line): a word tint stands further from the editor background than its line tint in OKLab lightness, on the same side, and at least 0.04 ΔEOK from it. Where a word is drawn off on its line's background side (31 words across the palettes: light palettes' removed words lighter than their lines, dark palettes' added words darker), or the lift and the line's move leave it nearer its line than 0.04, the word steps to the line's far side (`EditorHighlights::crossed_word_tint`): from the line's lightness outward a thousandth at a time, in the hue and chroma of the word's seed (its line tint a quarter of the way to its diff colour), else halfway to its diff colour's or the diff colour's own, to the first step that stands 0.04 from the line in ΔEOK and in lightness, or, where the colours drawn on the word as text bind first, the furthest step, the last before one on which they would not read, that stands 0.04 from the line in ΔEOK and 0.02 beyond it in lightness, one just-noticeable difference, so the order holds in lightness and not through chroma alone (decision, 2026-11-05 afternoon: held to ΔEOK alone, Solarized Dark's added word stepped across to `#2D3500`, 0.0004 beyond its line, now `#214514`, 0.041 beyond): every colour reads 7:1, or its own contrast on the line where lower; where no step that stands apart lets them, 4.75:1 (the text rule and its margin) or their contrast on the line, then 4.5:1; where no step that stands 0.02 beyond lets every colour read 4.5:1, the last that does, beyond the line and 0.04 from it, since 4.5:1 is the text floor every palette keeps (One Dark's added word `#2F491D`, 0.018 beyond its line, every colour on it 4.54:1 or more, where 0.02 beyond its raised keyword read 4.39:1; Kanagawa Lotus's removed word, `#FFBDB4` 0.018 beyond until its text darkened on 2026-11-05, now reaches the gap at `#F7BEA6`); and where no step beyond the line both reads 4.5:1 and stands 0.04 from it, the word keeps its tint drawn off, which reads, rather than a step its text could not read on (no shipped palette needs it; decision, 2026-11-05 evening: a fallback never breaks the rule it serves). The 0.02 gap holds for a word that steps across only: a word drawn off beyond its line is not stepped across for it where the lift and its line's move leave it nearer than 0.02 in lightness but 0.04 in ΔEOK from the line (Solarized Dark's removed word, 0.009, and Kanagawa Wave's, 0.008), since moving it would make its text read below off. The diff colour is drawn on the word only as its rule, fitted to it afterward. A word stepped across lies nearer its text than the one drawn off, so its text reads lower than off, by the decided order: Braden's removed word `#FFCFDB` on its line `#F9E5EB` draws its strings at 6.11:1, where they read 6.44:1 on the one drawn off, `#FCF6F7`. Every changed word also draws a 1 px rule inside its highlight box's bottom edge, as wide as the box, so a changed word is marked by more than colour: each added or removed word in the Diff view and each side's emphasized words in Split, in every palette and on both sides, one rule under each fragment of a word a line wraps (`EditorHighlights::added_rule`, `removed_rule`). Its colour is the side's diff colour as applied, `added` or `removed`, where that reads 3:1 on the side's word tint, otherwise the first of 256 even steps from it toward black in a light palette or white in a dark one that does, as the resting edge is fitted, decided once a palette with the other editor highlights; every shipped palette's diff colours already read so on their word tints (the least, Kanagawa Wave's removed `#FF5D62` on `#5C0C1C`, 4.59:1), so each rule is its diff colour (Omarchy tokyo-night `#9ECE6A` and `#F7768E`, Midnight `#75E0BB` and `#FF95A8`). A whole changed line, a hunk header and unchanged text draw none, and with More contrast off nothing is drawn. Under Find's other matches, which take the word's tint, the rule stays, so a matched changed word still reads as changed rather than as unchanged text; under the current match, whose accent underline would run about 2.6 px above it, it gives way, since two parallel strokes read as noise (decision, 2026-11-05), and so it does under every match where Find, on an editor past the 64 that keep a Find layer of their own, underlines each match and draws no tint under it. Why: a changed line carries cues beyond colour, its `+` or `-` and Split's filler rows, and a changed word carried none; in colour-blind models some words nearly vanish (Solarized Dark's removed words, design review of QA-103's frames), WCAG 1.4.1 asks for a cue beyond colour, and marking only some palettes or sides would read as a difference in meaning (decision, 2026-11-05). The text selection and Find's current match are raised instead, and Find's other matches take a colour of their own, as below: every colour on them keeps at least its contrast there with More contrast off, reaching 7:1 where they can, and each stays on the same side of every editor line as off, at least as far from it and with at least its luminance contrast against it, or the highlight drawn off stays (the luminance rule makes a raised highlight rare: text at 7:1 on a highlight lying between its line and that text would need the highlight moved toward its line, which the rule forbids, so such a highlight keeps the one drawn off, the raised syntax on it reading at least as off; fitted only to the text rule against the raised syntax they settled lighter, and Midnight's strings on the current Find match fell from 6.63:1 to 6.18:1; held to the distance alone, the selection, which is also the text selection in fields, turned near black in seven dark palettes, Midnight's `#0B3E3E` to `#000001`, and lost its hue). The current Find match stands from the selection at least 0.04 in OKLab and at least 0.02 apart in OKLab lightness, one just-noticeable difference, so the distance never comes from chroma alone, and at least as far as it does off wherever a candidate can: raised beside the raised selection; or the one drawn off where it stands so beside the raised or the off selection; or else fitted by the text rule (every colour on it 4.5:1, 0.04 from every editor line) beside the raised selection, or the off one, as far from it as off where a fitted candidate stands so, and only 0.04 where none does (decision, 2026-11-05 evening: the docs state the conditional the fit keeps; fitted at 0.04 alone, One Dark's current match `#104266` stood 0.041 from its selection, nearer than the pair drawn off, 0.044; decision, 2026-11-05 afternoon, superseding 2026-11-04's "where it can": Sandstone's current match drew `#FFE0BD`, 0.003 from its selection `#FFE0BB`, now `#DBB9A9`; One Dark's `#003869` stood 0.044 from its selection `#273756` by chroma alone, now `#13406D`, 0.045 from it and 0.028 in lightness). Find's other matches, which draw the selection's colour with More contrast off, take their own: the nearest to that one by the text rule that stands 0.04 ΔEOK and 0.02 in lightness from the selection, the current match and both word tints, and from the current match as far as the current match stands from them off, searched toward black or white and then toward the text (Midnight's, the selection's `#0B3E3E` off, now `#003B26`; Braden's `#BFF6E7`, now `#B0E3D5`). Where no colour fits, they keep the one drawn off, which reads, never the selection as More contrast draws it; the one drawn off is the selection's colour, so beside a selection that keeps its colour drawn off, as every palette's measured does, they still draw the selection's colour (decision, 2026-11-05 evening; no shipped palette reaches it). They are not raised from the one drawn off as the selection is: in no built-in or Omarchy palette did a raised candidate stand so apart, and a custom theme in which one does takes the text-rule fit, which drew the same colour as the raised one in the 4 of 476 synthetic custom themes measured where one stood apart (measured 2026-11-05). Why 0.02 and not two differences: two differences between each pair exhausted the band in which every colour on them still reads, so Alucard's other matches found no colour standing 0.04 in lightness from the selection, the current match and both word tints and fell back to the selection's, 0 apart from it, and other palettes' matches went to the band's edges, Kanagawa Lotus's `#F2F6F8` beside its canvas (measured 2026-11-05). With More contrast off, the word tints, Find's matches and the selection stay byte-identical: Off is each palette's tuned look and runs none of these rules (decision, 2026-11-05; an Off path doing rule work cost 3-4%). The border steps toward the text side until it holds 3:1 on the canvas, panel, subtle and raised surfaces (WCAG's non-text contrast), so dividers, field outlines and the popup, dialog and bordered secondary edges hold 3:1 on the surfaces each is fitted for; Midnight's secondary text goes from `#A4B1C5` to `#BDC7D7` and its border from `#2B3749` to `#606976`. The disabled edge keeps its 1.5:1 fit, so a control that cannot act stays fainter than the 3:1 edge every enabled control on a fill gains (in Midnight about 1.6:1 against 3.2:1; WCAG's non-text contrast exempts inactive controls), and a fill is not an edge: an unchecked Switch track keeps its quiet fill, and its hover, under its edge, and fields keep their fill, only their outline adjusted (decision, 2026-11-04). A disabled field's outline takes that disabled edge too, as a disabled Button's does, so it stays fainter than the enabled field's 3:1 outline beside it (Midnight `#313D4E`, about 1.7:1 on the dialog's surface, where it drew the enabled field's `#606976`; decision, 2026-11-04); against the field's own fill it reads about 1.4:1, since the edge is fitted to a Button's fill, not a field's (inactive controls are exempt, and it read 1.31:1 there with More contrast off; decision, 2026-11-04 evening); off, both outlines stay `border`. A field's fills derive from a base the app sets to the palette's `border` as chosen (`Theme::input_fill_base`), apart from the outline, so a dark field's fill rests inside the adjusted outline as it does off (Midnight `#1A2330` inside `#606976`, where it once lifted with the outline to `#2A323D`) and the placeholder on it reads at least as it does off (Omarchy tokyo-night 6.63:1 on the subtle surface, 5.99:1 off, where the lifted fill gave 5.32:1); Settings' Save keeps its fill, disabled too, since the kit draws that from half the field fill, while the disabled Save's border, the adjusted `border` at half strength, rises from about 1.20:1 to 1.75:1 (Midnight), clearly below the enabled Save's 3:1 edge, and a disabled field keeps its fill, the base at 80% in light and dark palettes, where it once lifted with the outline. The disabled label rises from about 3:1 to 4.5:1 on its surfaces and stays below three quarters of the adjusted secondary text's contrast. Everything else stays: hues, the accent and its states, the 2 px focus ring and the selection marker (already 3:1), the selected row, the quiet diff tints (their line tints move only where a changed word cannot stand apart, as above), the status colors beside their icons and words, and every size and position, so nothing moves. The adjustment is derived on the fly and never saved: a custom theme keeps the tokens it was saved with, the theme picker's miniatures keep each theme's own palette, since they show the theme as chosen, and the theme editor measures the draft as it will be saved (`Palette::readability_issues`) while its live preview, applied through the same path, shows the draft adjusted. Under More contrast an enabled control that rests on a fill of its own (secondary Buttons, the selected segment, the selected repository tab, an unchecked Switch track; default Buttons and fields through their own border) draws a 1 px edge at 3:1 over its bounds (`Palette::resting_edge`), moving nothing; a transparent ghost Button and an unselected segment, which draw no boundary, keep none and rely on their label's contrast. Each control keeps one such boundary, never a doubled edge (decision, 2026-11-04): the selected repository tab's edge goes round its frame, close button included, not round the tab's Button alone; danger and primary Buttons add none, since their opaque fills read at least 3:1 on every surface in every palette (lowest measured: danger 4.15:1, primary 3.90:1; the test pins the 3:1 floor); and Save and enabled fields keep their own border, the adjusted `border`, as their edge. The inventory is in [theme and retained layout](crates/app/docs/content-and-layout.md#theme-and-retained-layout).

## Typography and density

The dimensions below describe the default 13 px interface and 12 px code sizes. Settings independently adjusts interface text from 11–18 and code text from 10–24, with separate reset controls. Scale relevant row/control heights and editor gutters with their text; retain selection, focus and viewports when changing sizes. Larger sizes must keep the minimum 1,000 × 680 content window usable through appropriate scrolling.

- UI: system sans serif, 13 px regular; 13 px medium for selected labels and panel headings. macOS uses the platform UI font; Linux embeds IBM Plex Sans (Regular, Medium, SemiBold and Bold, with italics) so every weight draws as its own face on every distribution, and multiplies both text sizes by the desktop text scaling factor on Wayland. Its antialiasing comes from the first source that answers: the settings portal's GNOME `font-rendering`, then `font-antialiasing`; then fontconfig's resolved `sans-serif` `antialias`/`rgba`, distribution defaults included; then grayscale; see the [Linux runbook](docs/linux.md#platform-limits).
- Code and aligned numerical data: monospace, 12 px, with gutters and aligned overlays using the native editor’s measured line height. macOS uses Menlo; Linux embeds DejaVu Sans Mono so code keeps its columns on every distribution, whether or not the family is installed. The embedded font stays the default, except on a first launch on Omarchy, which turns the setting below on so code matches the desktop. Below **Code text size**, Linux Settings offers **Use the desktop's monospace font**. When it is on, code uses fontconfig's `monospace` family (JetBrainsMono Nerd Font on Omarchy), but only a family the toolkit loaded that draws basic Latin at one advance. Otherwise the row gives the reason and code keeps the embedded font. The family is looked up off the interface thread and again whenever a window regains focus. Code text shows the characters as typed: it turns off the `calt` and `liga` font features, so a ligature font such as JetBrains Mono draws `--`, `->` and `!=` as separate characters in diffs, editors, Blame, the conflict editor, Markdown code blocks, the PR review and the Settings sample. The PR review sizes its line-number columns from the code font's digit width at the current code size, so the file's widest line number fits. A text area that shows code takes its height and its line pitch from the code size: it shows a fixed number of whole lines at that size's line height, the code editor's, so it grows with the code size rather than keeping a fixed box. The conflict view's result draft shows eight, as its fixed 160 px box held at the default size (decision, 2026-11-05: that box drew its lines at the interface size's 16 px pitch, so 18 pt code crowded them into each other). Code text must support selection and copying.
- Object identifiers, a commit's and a file's blob alike, take one face across the app: the interface font of the line that holds them. History's SHA column draws a commit's short ID as the inspector's metadata and Reflog's and Stashes' rows draw theirs, and as Compare's footer draws a file's blob IDs, so an ID reads alike wherever it appears. The column once drew it in the code family alone, the one place that did.
- Main repository title: 14 px semibold. Commit title in the right inspector: 15 px medium, fully wrapped, followed immediately by the available body at 12 px with a comfortable 1.5 line height. Avoid large display typography in repository views.
- Section labels: 11 px medium, the navigation's headings and a menu's labels alike (`appearance::SECTION_LABEL_TEXT`), restrained letter spacing, uppercase only for short headings such as LOCAL BRANCHES. No all-uppercase sentences.
- Spacing: use a restrained 4/8/12/16 px rhythm for explicit app dimensions; native rem-based spacing follows the interface size. Keep pane and card padding consistent within each surface. A dialog or panel, and each column within one, starts its text at one left edge, its header, rows, labels and notices alike: the GitHub panel's, and its review file column's, at a compact Button's 10 px label inset from that edge, where the Buttons at the edge start their labels, the Reflog's at its fields' 11 px; boxes and bordered surfaces keep the edge and their own padding. Secondary controls stay compact; prominent Git actions are 34 px high and the commit action is 36 px. The native control radius is 7 px; grouped cards use 10–14 px corners. Reserve pill shapes for small reference labels and counts.
- Comfortable history/file rows: 34/44 px; Compact: 28/34 px. Navigation rows remain compact; a selected navigation or project-list row keeps its label in text at medium weight and marks its icon in the accent. Use full-row hit areas. Keep metadata baseline-aligned and columns consistent. Column titles and text/reference cells share a 10 px leading inset; the graph uses a matching 10 px node margin. Preserve this geometry when resizing or scrolling horizontally.
- Icons: original 24-unit SVGs rendered at 16 px in rows and around 16–18 px in controls; status symbols sit inside quiet 26 px tiles. White source strokes allow GPUI tinting. Standard strokes are 1.65 units, rounded ends and joins. Keep actionable icons paired with labels or accessible names.
- App identity: [the layered Icon Composer source](assets/icons/README.md) supplies the native macOS appearances and the static default artwork in `assets/app-icon.png` and embedded 128-pixel `assets/branding/app-icon.png`. The turtle sits on navy in Default and system charcoal in Dark; Mono provides clear and tinted appearances. Repository and Projects headers and the collapsed sidebar share the default artwork. Small action/status controls remain SVGs so they stay crisp and theme-tinted.

## One responsive order

Every page gives way to a narrower window, or a larger interface size, in one order, whatever control it is laying out:

1. **Content stays whole.** Repository content and what identifies it keep their room first: the commit subject (at least its floor), a file's name and its Compare path, the selected item, and the text being read or written. Code and images scroll in their own viewports; the chrome around them never cuts them for a control.
2. **Actions become icons.** A labelled action keeps its 28 px control with its icon, its whole label as its accessible name and its tooltip, as Blame, Directories, Stage, Unstage and the header's Projects and profile do.
3. **Labels go.** Captions, group names and words beside a count leave next; the glyph or count that tells the item apart stays, with the words in its tooltip and accessible name.
4. **Secondary columns go.** Columns after the content (History's Author, Date and SHA, a panel's second column) leave the visible width last, past the table's edge where they scroll sideways, or below the content where a panel stacks.

A control's own breakpoint follows from this order, measured from the interface font at the interface size, rather than a figure of its own; where a breakpoint described below disagrees with the order, the order holds. Whatever the width, nothing is cut by its clip: a line of text fits its room, wraps inside it, or ends in an ellipsis at a grapheme-cluster boundary, never inside a cluster (a letter keeps its combining marks, and an emoji with modifiers or a ZWJ sequence and a flag stay whole), the spaces and a clause's punctuation before the ellipsis trimmed as whole clusters; a control lies wholly inside the region that clips it, a row that cannot hold its controls wraps them below whole; a column at a table's edge is whole or past the edge. No caption or count is drawn without the control it belongs to, no separator ("·") stands alone or starts a line (an image comparison's note that falls back to one text, below, is the exception), and no key name, as "Shift+↑" or "Page Down", breaks across two lines: every line of "·"-separated phrases, the GitHub panel's and the inline help of Keyboard Shortcuts, the command palette, Quick Open and Edit local commits, a 3D preview's gestures and an image comparison's notes where their lines hold them that way, is spaced by its gap rather than by a separator's trailing space, which ran past a line's end and wrapped the "·" before it onto a line of its own, and a phrase wider than its line wraps only between its runs, a key name such as Page Down being one and a "/" between two keys ending the run before it (`crate::PhraseLine`, decided 2026-11-07). An image comparison's note has a budget of lines and a line of phrases cannot end in an ellipsis, so a "·" note that wraps and whose lines cannot hold it by phrase is drawn as one text, which GPUI's line wrapper can break before a "·", leaving the separator alone or at a line's start: whole, with no ellipsis or tooltip, where that text still fits its lines, and otherwise clamped to them, ending in an ellipsis with its whole text in its tooltip (`image_compare::comparison_note`, decided 2026-11-08). The width walk (`crates/app/src/width_walk.rs`) lays out History, Compare (text and image), Working Changes and the GitHub panel from 461 to 1,480 px at 13 and 18 pt in 490 and 980 px tall windows, at each measured breakpoint and a pixel either side of it, and fails on cut or orphaned text, on the Git action bar's repository edge left out or its line drawn short of its whole text, on a bar control running into the next, on the navigation's mode toggles wrapped onto a second row or its keyboard hint wrapped inside a phrase, and on a field's placeholder wider than its field (History is walked again on a detached HEAD, on a branch with no upstream, with the navigation at its 180 px minimum, History and Working Changes with the inspector saved at its widest, 480 px, which every narrower window draws narrower, giving way to the content column's minimum, with a search that found nothing, where it fails on the search's line or Clear search not wholly inside the list and the window, with a write's outcome fresh in the status line, where it fails on the outcome drawn short of its whole text, and beside the local watch's warning, where the outcome may end in an ellipsis only once every hint and Details…'s label have given way; History and Working Changes are walked again with that warning alone); a finding a follow-up owns is listed there by name until it is fixed. Every dialog, with a width of its own or the kit's 448 px, is never wider than the window less 24 px on either side (`appearance::dialog_width`; the kit narrows its default by the theme's `dialog_margin`, so a dialog that sets no width keeps the same margin rather than standing 6.5 px from a 461 px window's sides; the custom theme editor keeps its own 32 px rule), and in a 461 × 490 half tile at 13 and 18 pt the walk opens every dialog that opens from History on its fixture, each function that opens a dialog walked or left out by name with why (`width_walk::DIALOG_SITES`, which a new dialog fails until it is listed), and fails one closer to a side than that, one past the window's foot, text that crosses its clip or runs into the panel's padding, and a list that no longer shows its first row whole.

A shorter window gives way the same way. Every dialog ends at least 24 px (`appearance::DIALOG_MARGIN`, the room it keeps from a narrow window's sides) above the status bar's top edge, never on or over the bar: its height is capped at that bound (`appearance::dialog_max_height`), and a list in it gives up the height first, then its body scrolls. Edit local commits' sequence is the exception: it takes its body's view, up to its 220 px, less its focus ring's room and never less than two rows, and the body scrolls the rest of the form around it, since reviewing the sequence is what that form is for, and a sequence that gave its height up first would leave a row or two of the series the person is asked to review (decision, 2026-11-07); a wheel over a sequence longer than its view moves the sequence while it can still move that way, and the body only once it cannot, as Your themes' list does in Settings. A dialog opened over another opens 16 px lower for each dialog beneath it and gives that offset up from its cap, so its foot keeps the bound too (with the first dialog's cap, a nested dialog's foot lay 16 px further down). Keyboard Shortcuts' list gives up its height down to exactly one entry inside its 1 px border, keeping a selected entry it showed whole in view as it shrinks (scrolling the least, with its group's heading where it is the group's first entry and the list holds both) and after a search, which starts the list at its group's heading only where the entry below it is whole too, so at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window its search field, its hint and Done stay drawn, the hint whole inside the dialog's clip (a search for "command palette" there once cut its one entry's key chip at the list's foot under a whole heading). The dialog's status, which draws nothing, takes no place in the dialog's column, so the hint stands one gap below the list, as the column's other parts stand apart (decision, 2026-11-02: laid out between the list and the hint, its 1 px took a gap of its own, which stood the hint a gap and a pixel further below the list than now, 10.75 px at 13 pt and 14.5 px at 18 pt, and at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window, the list at its floor, put the hint's last line 10 px past the clip in the test platform's wider font, where the hint wraps to four lines; the list cannot give more without cutting its one entry, so the hint takes back the gap that held nothing). The status lies along the view's top, which the dialog always draws, so an assistive technology finds it showing where the dialog's clip cuts the view's foot (along the foot, it lay past the clip there, and the AT-SPI adapter reported it not showing). The count above the list keeps one line: the platform's modifier legend stands beside the count only where the interface font lays the whole line out on one line in the dialog, and otherwise the count stands alone (the macOS legend once wrapped to a second line there and pushed the hint 21 px under Close). The count is text drawn at once as the search changes; the dialog's one polite status announces it, with the selected entry, only once the search has rested 500 ms (`shortcuts::COUNT_REST`), in one announcement that reads both, “16 shortcuts · Open Repository Tab: Ctrl+T”, joined as the command palette's status joins its parts: one announcement per pause, after the screen reader has echoed the last key, never a count and entry it last announced again, and none for a search still resting as the dialog closes (decisions, 2026-11-02: typing "palette" at 60 ms a key, Orca spoke "34 shortcuts" and never the settled "1 shortcut", each change cut off by the echo of the next key; named by each key's first entry, the entry was announced on every key that changed it, as the count was; 2026-11-03: as two statuses changed in one frame, Orca spoke the count and dropped the entry announced a millisecond later as one of “multiple instances in short time”). Each move the person makes by Up, Down, Home, End, the page keys or a click is spoken once: on Linux, by the list, as Orca speaks the list item AT-SPI reports newly selected, a row the virtual list had not drawn included, which enters the tree already selected and which the adapter reports as a change of its list's selection, and the app announces nothing of its own (the status announcing it too had Down speak each entry twice, QA-90, and End's and Home's, QA-92). A search's change of selection is heard once too: on Linux, where Orca spoke the list's change of selection a rest follows, the status says the count alone ("3 shortcuts"), never the entry the row's name just said, and in doubt, as at keys typed 60 ms apart or behind a late frame, the whole reading (`shortcuts::Listed`, the rule the command palette's status keeps, QA-105). On macOS, whose adapter reports the change on the list rather than the item and where VoiceOver's cursor stays with focus in the search field, the status announces the entry every move reaches, alone, at once (`shortcuts::LIST_SPEAKS_SELECTION`; not yet checked with VoiceOver). The entry last spoken, by the list or the status, follows the selection, so a rest that comes back to the entry the search selected before a move announces it again with the count. The status is named by what it last announced, or by the entry the list last spoke, which it takes quietly, as no live region, so between a key and its rest its name lags the line by at most that half second; the legend after the count is text of its own, so a resize that brings or takes it announces nothing (drawn and named as one line, the count's status changed its name, and was announced again, whenever a resize crossed the width where the legend fits).
