A group's heading row is its name's line and the group gap above it (22 px, about 30 px at 18 pt), not an entry's 42 px, so that search shows the General heading and its entry whole together, about 88 px in the 99 px list (the heading's 58 px row once had its top cut by the list's edge), and Up onto a group's first entry centres the two together where the wheel had cut the heading, as Down past the list's foot does, while a click on it, or a list whose new height would cut it, scrolls the least that shows both (the toolkit's nearest row once brought such an entry back alone to the list's top, its heading cut); where the list holds fewer than two entries, as at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window, where it was about 72 px tall before the hint took back its gap, which gives the list back up to the 14.5 px that gap and pixel took there (in the test platform's wider font, where the hint wraps to four lines, the list went from its 60 px floor, the hint cut, to 63.5 px), it scrolls by whole rows, so each scroll it sets starts it at a row's top: an entry whose heading does not fit with it starts the list with the heading wholly above it, the next row showing at its foot as a list cut at its foot does (decision, 2026-11-02: the General heading stood as a 13 px sliver above the first entry and Up and Down centred an entry between two slivers; whole-row scrolling, rather than a floor sized to the heading and its entry, because that floor needs about 16 px more than the dialog's cap leaves there and would scroll the dialog's body, moving the hint and Done, while the scroll moves nothing the dialog draws; a search's one entry still ends at the list's foot, since the list's end holds it there); the dialog once ended 14 px over the bar at 13 pt, where its hint wraps, and on the bar's top rule at 18 pt. A key chip ends at the rows' trailing inset measured from the scrollbar's gutter, whose track rests over the rows' end, rather than from the list's side, where the track covered the chip's right edge and padding (decision, 2026-11-02). A window resized under the list keeps it where it was, clamped to its rows, where the wheel moved it away from the selection or the new height leaves the selected entry whole, since the person put it there (a resize brought the selection back on every frame, undoing the wheel); a text size chosen with the list open, which changes every row's height under the scroll, still brings the selection into view, whether or not the list's height changes with it (where the dialog's cap held the height, the selection stayed wherever the scaled rows put it). Home and End select its first and last entries and Page Up and Page Down move by as many entries as its height holds whole rows, as a page in a macOS list does, each bringing its entry in by Up's and Down's rules, so Home shows the first group's heading at the list's top and End the last entry whole at its foot, even where the selection is already there and the wheel moved the list away; the list takes the four keys from its search field, which keeps focus and Shift-Home and Shift-End, as macOS's single-line fields leave them to the view beside them (the field took Home and End to move its caret, and Page Up and Page Down did nothing). Its inline help names the four keys beside Up and Down, in the platform's key labels, since the app lists its own keys wherever it lists keys. The status bar's hints keep their earlier “↑↓ Browse · Esc Close” first, Escape kept longest as they give way, to “↑↓ · Esc” and then “Esc” where the room holds one key, and name “PgUp / PgDn Page · Home / End Ends” after them only where room is left once the status line keeps its whole text, so they give way before it: the inline help already names every key, and nothing already drawn should move or be cut for a hint (named with the rest, spelled out as the toolkit spells them, the hints took 358 px at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window, where “↑↓ Browse · Esc Close” takes 175 px, and cut the status line to about 53 px). The theme editor keeps its own 16 px above the bar, and the GitHub panel bounds its own scrolling body to the room `appearance::dialog_max_height` leaves it. About keeps both bounds, its own 550 px narrowed by `appearance::dialog_width`: its details have no height of their own, take what the cap leaves below its title and description, and scroll the rest, cued by their scrollbar, with Done drawn below them; Tab onto Copy bug diagnostics brings it whole into view with its ring, and Enter on it, like Space and a click, copies the report rather than taking Done, as the key goes down and never on a held key's repeats (under X11 a held key arrives as fresh presses), as the toolkit's popover triggers and menus act (decision, 2026-11-05, QA-103: at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window its literal 550 px, scaled with the text, set it 761 px wide from 150 px left of the window, Done past its right side, and its details' literal height, the viewport less 230 px, ended half a pixel into the button, which drew nothing of its label and took no click). `width_walk`'s narrow dialog walk checks every dialog that opens from History on its fixture, About among them, against the bound at 13 and 18 pt; every function that opens a dialog is walked or left out by name, with why (`width_walk::DIALOG_SITES`), so a new dialog fails the walk's guard until it is either, where a list kept by hand left About unchecked.

## One left edge

Each panel, dialog and table column starts its text at one left edge: its header, rows' labels, notices and fields' text and placeholders alike. The edge and the boxes at it come from shared insets in `appearance` (`PANE_INSET`, the kit's 0.75 rem from a pane's side; `FIELD_TEXT_INSET` and `FIELD_TEXT_PADDING`, where a medium field's text starts at every size) and beside the `button` helper (`LABEL_INSET`, a compact Button's label), never a literal of a panel's own. A line may start past the edge only for a reason the grid names: a Button or field standing at the edge starts its label or text its own inset in, a row's leading icon or status tile stands at the edge with its name after the gap, a tree level, a pill, a nested group. A ghost Button, which paints no outline or fill at rest, standing at a text edge aligns its content with that edge, its leading icon, else its label, its frame and hover fill spreading into the padding before it by its label inset; a Button with an outline or fill at rest stands its frame at the edge. The frame of a ghost Button is invisible at rest, so its content is the edge a person reads, as the cards' rows and the GitHub panel's Buttons already show (decided 2026-11-05). So where Your themes' actions wrap onto a line of their own, New theme… stands its "+" icon on the title's edge, its frame the label inset before it at every text size, and Import… follows at its usual gap; beside the text they stay where they were. Its focus ring, 3 px outside its frame, then ends on the inner edge of the card's 1 px border at 13 pt, where the card's 1 rem padding less the label inset leaves exactly that, and a pixel inside it at 18 pt. At 11 pt on a 2x display, in a window 397 px wide or narrower, which only a compositor that ignores the 1,000 × 680 minimum size gives, as a narrow Hyprland tile can, its outer edge lies 0.5 px over that border (about 0.67 px at 1.5x, and more under a desktop text scale below 1): drawn over the border, never cut by it, so the rule stands there too (decided 2026-11-05). Standing its frame on the edge, its icon had started 10 px past the title at 13 pt and 14 px at 18 pt, a third left edge in the card. In Settings' cards the rule holds too. A text size's −, where its controls wrap below its description, stands its label on the title's edge, its frame the small Button's 0.5 rem padding before it. Git identity's Manage named profiles…, as wide as its content, stands its icon on the card's edge, and so do Reset edits where it wraps below Save repository identity and, with no repository open, Choose a project; their rings lie inside the section cards' 1.25 rem padding at every size (Reset edits' and Choose a project's icons had started 10 px past the edge at 13 pt and 14 px at 18 pt, and Manage, stretched across the card, centred its content in it). History columns' Reset column layout, a ghost Button drawn before the rule that History's Columns panel draws too, still stands its frame at the card's edge until it is moved. Where the padding before a ghost Button cannot hold its pull and its 3 px ring, the ring wins and the frame stays at the edge (decided 2026-11-05): the inspector's and the conflict view's 0.75 rem bars hold neither. A dialog's sides hold both at every size: a dialog pads them by the larger of 16 px and the pull with the ring's room (`dialog_ghost_room`, which the kit's dialog asks for as it draws), 16 px at 13 pt, where the two take 13 px, and 17 px at 18 pt, where they take 14 and 3, rounded up to the device pixel where that falls between two (17.33 px at scale 1.5), so a ghost Button's ring at the dialog's text edge ends on the inner edge of its 1 px border at 18 pt and 3 px inside it at 13 pt (decided 2026-11-05; the kit's fixed 16 px held the 13 px but not the 17). So in a dialog the rule holds at every size: Git profiles' New profile… stands its "+" on the dialog's text edge, and so do the profile form's Use current repository signing settings, Activity's actions, Compare local revisions' choices, the rewritten-series review's actions and Back to series, Download an LFS preview's remotes and Read configured source, Worktree created's actions, Add remote's Fetch rules… and About's Copy bug diagnostics, each its icon, else its label, and in a row that wraps the first Button of each line; a Button that stood stretched across the dialog, centring its content, is now as wide as its content. A ghost Button never runs past the dialog's text column (decided 2026-11-05): one with a label and no icon whose label is wider than the column, as Use current repository signing settings is at 18 pt in a 461 px window (about 389 px of label in a 377 px column), shrinks to the column, its frame ending on the column's right side, and wraps its label onto lines that each start on the edge, 1.25 times its text size apart, its frame growing by each line; on one line it keeps the 28 px Button. One with an icon keeps a short label that fits beside its icon in the narrowest column, at 18 pt in a 461 px window, and does not wrap: New profile…, Read configured source, Back to series and Copy bug diagnostics. Stretched across the form, it had ended its label in an ellipsis; as wide as its label, it would have run 26 px past the column and 9 px past the dialog's inner edge. A scrolling body inside such a dialog keeps the pull with the ring's room on its left, and the ring's room on its right, given back through its margins, so its clip cuts neither the hover fill nor the ring: in the profiles list, Activity and the Git LFS download form the body had stood its first ghost Button at its own left edge and cut its ring. A dialog body that can be taller than what the dialog's cap leaves it lies in the dialog's content and scrolls in a view of its own, cued as the profile form is, by its scrollbar while it overflows or by fades where it is too short for one (decided 2026-11-06): it gives way to the cap, the control Tab moves onto scrolls into view with its ring's room, and the body keeps that room around its controls and below its last, the footer's gap giving it back, so no Tab stop is left below a fold or cut at a foot. With no reveal of their own, or in the dialog's own scrolling body, which reveals nothing, a 461 × 490 window left Compare local revisions' choices and the rewritten-series review's actions and publication fields below their bodies' folds or cut, and Add remote's Fetch rules… and Worktree created's Open in editor cut at the foot; at 18 pt the review's series list, Activity's Browse reflog…, Add remote's Use fetch URLs for push and Download an LFS preview's Read configured source too, and Edit local commits' planned commits, which Load commits focuses, and its I understand these commits may be shared, focused below the fold and drawn nowhere (now the body shows the sequence whole, at its final height, from the first frame that draws it, however Load commits was pressed); and in a 1,480 × 800 window Worktree created's actions, Add remote's Fetch rules… and Compare local revisions' last choice, ending their bodies, had their rings' foot cut, as at 18 pt did the Reflog's selected entry's message, its recovery branch name and Review recovery branch…, its content sized to the window less 240 px. Worktree created, whose body did not scroll, scrolls the same way, and no body asks for more height than the window gives its dialog: the Git LFS download form's 450 px stops at the dialog's cap, and the rewritten-series review's body takes what the dialog's cap leaves it below its title and above Back to repository. A Button in a bordered box inside a dialog, as a saved profile's Apply…, stands at the box's edge, whose 0.75 rem padding holds neither, so its frame stays there; a row holding a Button filled at rest, as Compare local revisions' mode toggles, the chosen one filled, and the rewritten-series review's text modes, keeps its frames at the edge, and the worktree manager's rows still stand theirs there until they are moved. The Reflog starts its text, its title's among it, at a field's text inset past its padding, where its fields' text starts, so its ghost Buttons stand their content on that edge (decided 2026-11-06): Copy commit ID its hash glyph and Review recovery branch… its label where the recovery branch's name and its field's text start, each as wide as its content, its frame its pull before that edge, 1 px past the dialog's padding at 13 pt and 3 px into it at 18 pt; both had stood stretched across the column, centring their content. Copy commit ID fits on one line in the narrowest column the rule names, and in a narrower one, as a 260 px tile's at 18 pt, shrinks to the column and wraps its label beside its glyph, each line starting where its first does, rather than end it in an ellipsis in a frame cut to the column; its glyph stands beside the first line, where the label reads from, not centred on its lines, and the check and Copied take the glyph's and that line's place (decided 2026-11-06). Its scrolling content keeps those 3 px with the ring's room on its left, given back through its margin, so its clip cuts neither the hover fill nor the ring. Interactive rebase's Load commits stands beside the base field, past the text edge, as wide as its content at every size, the field giving way, so it never wraps onto a line of its own and the edge rule never reaches it. Interactive rebase's I understand these commits may be shared, a toggle that is transparent until pressed and filled once it is, keeps its frame at the form's text edge in both states, as a filled Button does, rather than take the ghost rule while unpressed and move its frame by the pull as a press fills it (decided 2026-11-06). Where a panel's text starts at a compact Button's label inset, as the GitHub panel's does, its Buttons, transparent at rest, stand their frames at the panel's side so their labels start at the text's edge, and a field stands its box with their frames and keeps its own padding, so its text starts 1 px past the panel's text at 13 pt and 3 px before it at 18 pt, where the label inset has scaled and the field's padding has not; the grid measures such a field by its box. A bordered pane there, the Conversations pane, the reply and inline comment composers, the confirmation and the connection pane alike (`github_view::bordered_pane`), stands its frame with the Buttons' frames and pads its sides by the label inset less its 1 px border, so its text starts at the panel's text edge at 13 and 18 pt, the confirmation standing below a reply's composer as its sibling: 0.75 rem inside its border, the Conversations pane's heading and conversations started 0.65 px past the edge at 18 pt, which the grid first read at 18 pt in a 1,480 px window once the draft status stopped wrapping below the section tabs and the pane rose into view, and the reply composer under it, which kept that padding, started 0.75 px past it at 13 pt (0.65 at 18), so the two stood apart (decided 2026-11-01). A Button whose icon leads its label there, as Pull requests, Refresh PRs and New pull request do, stands its icon at the text's edge and starts its label after the icon and its gap. Both keep what is drawn where it is (the owner's rule of 2026-10-30, move nothing already drawn): moving the field's box to the text's edge would have broken the column its box shares with the Buttons' frames, and moving its text to the edge would have set its box apart from them by those pixels. A leading icon or tile stands at its panel's edge, with the header's icon and the frames of the Buttons there, and its text starts after it and its gap: the 15 px icon of History's navigation rows, the inspector's Changed files header, Working's Staged and Unstaged headings and the project pane's projects; the 17 px Changes icon over Working's file list; a changed file's 26 px status tile, in the inspector and in Working Changes alike; the composer's 13 px branch glyph; the project hub action card's 42 px tile and its features' 16 px icons; and a Button whose 16 px icon leads its label, as the inspector's Copy message does, which starts the label after its label inset, the icon and its gap. So a list's rows take their header's icon column (decided 2026-11-01, the owner's rule of one left edge): the inspector's file tiles stood 3 px past its edge, the list's 1 px edge and the row's 2 px marker added to the pane's inset; in Working Changes the file tiles stand in the list's column with the Staged and Unstaged icons, the rows' leading inset inside the list's 1 px margin and border, which keeps each row's focus ring inside the list, where behind a fixed 10 px inset they stood 2.25 px past the headings' icons at 13 pt; the Changes header above keeps the inset it shares with the filter, the selection controls and the composer, so its icon stands those 2 px before the list's column (decided 2026-11-01: a column the header and the headings share would break one of those two earlier rules); the project pane's icons 2.25 px past its heading at 13 pt and 1.5 px before it at 18. Each row now takes the pane's inset less what stands before it, its marker and, in the inspector, the list's edge, so its icon stays in the column at every text size. The hub's recent projects are a bordered box, their rows a group of their own with their names at one edge after the 42 px folder tile, inside its border and padding. Settings' sections are bordered cards whose 32 px tile stands at the card's edge, its title and description after the tile's gap, and whose controls start at that edge; inside them a theme card, the Your themes card, the repository's box and each text size's sample are groups of their own, their text at their own padding (a sample's 0.75 rem, and the stacked List density control's first label, lie within half a pixel of a compact Button's label inset, which the grid names for both); List density's segmented control, stacked in a narrow card, starts its labels at its Buttons' padding (a text size's controls, wrapped below its description, did too until 2026-11-05, above), and Save repository identity, the kit's medium Button, after its padding, its 14 px icon and its gap (decided 2026-11-01, the owner's rule of 2026-10-30, move nothing already drawn: the grid read Settings for the first time, a scroll view at a time, and each line it found past a card's edge was one of these, named in `layout_grid`). Narrow History's compact graph, which draws no title, keeps no title insets in its header: at 18 pt their 23.5 px widened the 20 px cell past the rows', and the Subject title stood 3.5 px right of its rows. The layout grid (`crates/app/src/layout_grid.rs`) lays out History and its inspector, Compare, Working Changes and the composer, the project hub and pane, the Reflog, interactive rebase, the command palette, Quick Open, the GitHub panel, its bordered panes among them, and Settings, read half a scroll view at a time so that a line one view cuts at its foot lies whole in the next (a view at a time, it never read Your themes' wrapped actions), at 13 and 18 pt in a 1,480 and a 461 px window, measures where each panel's lines start and fails on a second edge, taking a panel whose every line leads with an icon, as Working's file list, at the edge where its icons stand; a finding a follow-up owns is listed there by name until it is fixed.

## Workspace modes and hierarchy

Projects, Repository, and Settings are separate application pages. The repository has History, Compare, and Working Changes modes. History and Compare share the context and persistent right inspector below; Working Changes reuses the comparison area with a staged/unstaged inspector. At a typical 1440 × 900 window, the repository uses a 48 px page header, a 46 px Git action bar with collapsed-by-default Targets, and a 26 px status strip. The workspace uses all remaining height; a window too short for the full header and action bar to leave History three rows takes their compact rows instead (see the shared repository header below). Browsing transitions retain the user's place; an explicit write refreshes the affected state.

The repository header groups History and Changes within one quiet navigation surface; Pull requests is a named adjacent entry into native review, and a press is never silent: it opens the panel at once, over a read of the panel's own that closing cancelled and that is still stopping, or says why while a write runs: on the button itself in its tooltip, which stays through the press, and in the status line, spoken politely, as “Pull requests: wait for …”; through a write it stays pressable and a Tab stop for the whole spell, announced unavailable, its tooltip giving the reason and its label dimming with the other refused controls, never drawn disabled. Repository name and path remain distinct, with the full path available on hover and a readable repository identity node. Selected commit details expose the subject and a separate author/date/captured-identity description without a repeating live region. Profile and Settings actions sit at the trailing edge. Long content truncates within its allocated region; the header can wrap groups at enlarged interface sizes without clipping essential actions. The tab strip reserves space for the open repositories. Content comes before decoration there, in the [one responsive order](#one-responsive-order): its labelled actions, Menu (on Linux) and Workspaces, keep their labels only where every tab's whole name, to the tab's scaled 220 px, fits the list beside them; elsewhere they become 28 px icons (Menu's three lines, Workspaces' worktree glyph without its caret), named “Main Menu” and “Workspaces and tab actions” with tooltips that lead with those names, before any tab's name is cut, as at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 half tile, where “compact” once read “com…” beside a 100 px Menu and a 173 px Workspaces. They give way one at a time, a label going only where the tabs need its room, and each once as the window narrows, in a fixed order: Workspaces' label, the wider, first, then Menu's, so Menu, the primary control, keeps its label longest. In that half tile Menu keeps its label beside Workspaces' icon, where both once became icons together and left about 175 px of the list empty. The order is fixed so each label changes once and nothing already drawn moves back: ranked by the room each form labels, Menu's label went off, on and off again as the window narrowed, moving the tab list's left edge about 60 px each time, and a tab's label growing by “· working” could swap which label showed. The cost is at most the labels' difference more of the list standing empty, about 84 px at 18 pt. The choice is made before layout from the interface font, as the compact chrome's is, from the tabs' labels, the controls' measured labels, padding, icon and caret, and the window controls, so no frame shows one form and then the other. Where the icons still leave the list too narrow, a tab cuts its name, with an ellipsis at a grapheme-cluster boundary down to the name's first character (a floor rounded up to a whole device pixel, beside its insets as layout snaps them, so at scale factor 1.5 layout no longer lays a tab out a third of a pixel short of it), before its close button, which keeps its 28 px and its whole focus ring, where a short name once pushed the button past the list's edge; only where every tab is down to that first character does the list scroll, and a window wide enough for every tab, such as 1,480 px, shows them as before. Its Workspaces menu exposes the complete tab list, pinning, keyboard-friendly reorder actions and saved-workspace management; these occasional actions no longer occupy permanent separate buttons.

Projects and Settings temporarily hide the repository workspace. Returning restores the retained editor or list focus, including an open editor Find field, selection, and viewport. Dragged navigation and inspector widths survive those visits and History/Compare transitions. File-history inspection and comparison modes remain available after returning. Theme changes refresh retained editor decorations without replacing the editors or their search state.

### Projects

Give recent projects a searchable list with repository names and readable folder paths. Keep Open, Clone, and Create together in a clear action panel. Open uses the native folder picker; Clone has a repository URL/local path, parent folder, and new folder name; Create adds the initial branch. Suggest a clone folder name without overwriting the user's edit. Use real destinations, field validation, nearby errors, and a busy state that prevents duplicate submission. Folder-picker cancellation leaves the form intact.

Provide Back to repository when a project is already open. At narrow widths, stack the action panel and recents; preserve scrolling and keyboard access. Larger headings and generous spacing belong here, while the repository view stays dense.

Search shows the matching count against all recents and offers Clear search when no projects match. Clone/Create show the resulting repository folder before submission; truncated destinations retain a full-path tooltip.

### Project list pane

**Show the project list** in Settings, **View → Toggle Project List**, its shortcut (Command-Shift-B, or Ctrl+Shift+B on Linux, listed in Keyboard Shortcuts beside Toggle Sidebar) and the palette command add an optional pane on the far left of Repository and Settings; the hub keeps the whole window. It lists every project the user has opened, in named groups nested up to five levels, and resizes from 180–360 px with the same 1 px division as the navigator; a window narrower than the saved width plus 800 px shrinks the pane toward 180 px without changing the saved width. Nothing is laid out past the window's edge (decided 2026-11-07): beside the pane the page keeps its 520 px floor, the pane drawn no wider than that floor leaves it, and a window whose body cannot hold the pane's 180 px beside the page's 520 px, below 700 px, holds the pane back. Its setting stays on, the page takes the body's whole width, as with the pane closed, and the pane returns as soon as the window gives both room; the choice is made from the window's size before layout, as the page's width is (`GitTurtle::project_pane_drawn`), so the first frame at a new width lays out the form it settles in. In a 461 px half tile the page was laid out at its floor from the pane's edge and a paused merge's banner drew its actions at 559–690 px, past the window. View → Toggle Project List and the palette command still turn the setting on and off there; turned on while the pane is held back, the status bar says “Project list hidden: widen the window”, as the narrow rail's “… unavailable: widen the window” does, whole at 13 and 18 pt in a 461 px half tile, and turned off there it says “Project list off”, each fresh for 1.5 s, as a write's outcome is, and announced politely, since nothing else on screen changes. The Projects hub never draws the pane, so the key, the menu and the palette command there say “Project list on: hidden on this page” or “Project list off” at any window width. A read's error or a write's fresh outcome keeps the status line, as a running write's status keeps the bar, and the note is still announced beside them: it answers the person's own action. Settings' Show the project list switch, on while the pane is held back, reads “Hidden: widen the window to show the list beside the page” in its description's place, its row keeping the taller description's height so nothing moves as one replaces the other, and turned on there it is announced politely while the status line keeps the page's status, since the row is the feedback in place; the palette command's description says “Hidden: widen the window” there, as Toggle Sidebar's says why it waits, and its row's accessible name says it too, without “Unavailable”, since the command stays available. Focus on a control in the pane as it leaves, held back or closed, moves to the page's first stop, its ring whole, rather than staying on a control no longer drawn. The page keeps its controls as the pane comes and goes: focus on one of them stays there, its ring drawn, as the window crosses 700 px either way or the pane is turned on or off, where the page's controls once took new identities, so focus jumped to the page's first stop or stayed on a control no frame drew. Rows share the navigator's geometry: 30 px flat rows, a 12 px indent per level, a 14 px folder for a group and a 15 px turtle for a project so the two never look alike, subdued project counts on groups, the hover surface on hover, and the selected surface with accent text on the open project. Groups come before projects and each level sorts by display name, so a growing list stays predictable; a project whose display name repeats shows its parent folder beside it, and every row keeps the full path in its tooltip and accessible name.

The pane is one tab stop and a `Tree`: Up/Down/Home/End move a 2 px accent cursor marker, Right expands or enters a group, Left collapses or returns to its parent, Enter or Space opens a project or toggles a group, and Shift-F10, or on Linux the Menu key, opens the row's actions beside it. The same menu opens from a right-click or the quiet actions control that appears on hover and on the cursor row; it never enters the Tab order. A row's menu open as the pane leaves, held back or closed, closes with it and stays closed as the pane returns, rather than coming back beside its row with focus elsewhere. Menus are built when opened: rename, nest, move to a breadcrumbed destination with the current place checked and, like impossible destinations, disabled (dimmed and announced unavailable, since a row cannot move to where it already is), and removal that keeps the group's projects. Naming a group uses the Rename project dialog's layout, and a refused edit or failed save appears inside the pane with Retry and Dismiss rather than in the repository banner. Selecting the open project from Settings returns to it; selecting it on Repository does nothing.

### Settings

Group appearance, independent interface/code text sizes, history columns, startup/default branch, the project list pane, and repository Git identity. The twenty built-in themes appear in Light and Dark groups, followed by a **Your themes** group of the saved custom themes whenever there is one, all in one grid of 132 px cards: four columns at and above the scaled 1,060 px breakpoint, three in the stacked layout below it and two below the scaled 720 px breakpoint, so the twenty built-ins take five rows at 1,440 × 900. Each breakpoint is the page's own width as it is drawn, not the window's, so the project list, where it shows, narrows it: a 1,200 px window beside the 240 px list leaves the page 960 px, stacked with three columns (the grid once laid four out there for the window's width, in room the page did not have). Each card has a native miniature workspace preview (a 70 px workspace over a 54 px caption that names the theme in 12 px medium), swatches, a short description and an active checkmark; a custom card's description names its base, a custom card whose palette has readability findings shows the warning glyph with their count beside its name, and a partial row keeps its cards on the full rows' columns. The checkmark's badge is as tall as the name's line at every interface text size, so the selected card's caption starts level with its row, and the badge and the warning glyph are drawn in the applied palette inside a 1 px ring of its canvas, which keeps them whole on a caption whose surface is close to the applied accent or warning. A card that holds keyboard focus draws a 2 px accent ring 1 px outside its border, inside the grid's 10 px column and 6 px row gaps, so focus stays plain on the selected card, whose border is already the accent. Every card is named "‹theme› theme" for assistive technology, and a card is selected by clicking it, built-in or custom alike. On Linux, when the desktop runs Omarchy (its current theme's `colors.toml` is a regular file), a one-card **Desktop** group sits above the light and dark palettes. Its **Omarchy** card is captioned "Follows ‹Theme›" with the desktop theme's name, its miniature draws the palette mapped from that theme, and it is named "Omarchy theme" for assistive technology. Its caption, when too long for one line, takes a second before it ends in an ellipsis; the card keeps its size and place, and its miniature gives up the room, drawing only the rows it holds whole (three instead of four at the default size). It stays in that group whether the desktop theme is light or dark. A first launch on an Omarchy desktop, before any preferences are saved, selects it, so the first window already wears the desktop's theme. Choosing it reads the desktop's theme first and applies what that read finds, so it does not flash the palette the card last showed. A read that takes longer than 100 ms, as one waiting out a switch still in progress, lets that last palette apply meanwhile, which can briefly be the theme the desktop is leaving. The card then follows every Omarchy theme switch. While Settings is open the card stays current with the desktop even when another theme is selected, and applies nothing until chosen. While it is selected, **Follow system appearance** shows off and disabled, described "Omarchy follows your desktop theme", and choosing any other card releases it. When the theme cannot be read, the card says "Unavailable" beside its name and names the palette still in use as its description ("Keeping ‹Theme›", "Keeping the last colors" when no name was read, or "Using Midnight"), a muted line under the Desktop group gives the reason in its own words, wrapped to at most two lines and then ended with an ellipsis, the tooltip and accessible name carry the whole reason and the palette in use, and a selected card stays visible. The [themes specification](docs/development/themes/spec.md#omarchy-theme) owns the mapping. Density offers Comfortable and Compact. Directly below List density, **Reduce motion** and **Increase contrast** are rows built as its row is: a top border above each, the title and description at the row's left edge, and three segments at its trailing edge in the same shared-helper look, focus ring and keep-in-view, stacked below the title in the narrow page as List density's are. Every row's trailing control beside its text, these segments, a text size's or the graph lane spacing's steppers, Your themes' actions and a switch's track alike, is centred on the title's first line, so the control's own text shares the title's baseline at every interface text size and stays there as the window's width rewraps the description; centred on the row, the segments sat lower than List density's beside a description of two lines and drifted down with every line it wrapped to, and with their tops on the title's line a segment group sat 4 px below the title's baseline at 18 pt and a switch 3.5 px above its cap centre. A control taller than that line reaches above the title, but its focus ring never past the room the row keeps clear above it (its padding below a separator or inside a card, or the gap to what is above), so it never reaches the separator or the row above (a focused segment's ring stays 10 px below its separator at 13 pt and 15 px at 18 pt): one that would is lowered just enough. A switch fills its row, which the page reveals with only a ring's room around it, so its track never rises above the row's top: at 13 pt the 20 px track, taller than the title's line (19.5 px on a 2x display, 19 px at 1x), keeps its top there rather than rising 0.25 px (0.5 px at 1x), which let the page's edge cut its ring. A control stacked below its text keeps the stacked layout. Your themes' header and the two text sizes' rows wrap rather than stack: their text keeps at least 180 px (scaled with the interface text) beside the control, and where the row cannot hold the control beside that, the control, Your themes' New theme… and Import… among them, starts a line of its own at the title's left edge, the row's 0.75 rem gap below the description, with nothing reserved above it, and the text takes the row's whole width; at 461 px and 18 pt the header, which never wrapped, kept its actions beside a text column 66 px wide, its title on two lines and its description a word a line. The segments are **Follow the desktop** (on macOS **Follow the system**), **On** and **Off**; following is the default for both because Omarchy has no switch for either. Following, the description says what the desktop asks: "Your desktop asks for reduced motion." or "Your desktop does not ask for reduced motion.", and "…more contrast." for Increase contrast (on macOS "macOS asks…" and "macOS does not ask…"). On and Off say what they do whatever the desktop asks: Reduce motion On, "Transitions finish at once and GIFs and model clips stay paused, whatever the desktop asks."; Off, "GitTurtle's short transitions play, whatever the desktop asks."; Increase contrast On, "Secondary text, dividers and control edges are drawn with more contrast, in every theme."; Off, "Each theme's own contrast, whatever the desktop asks." Each row keeps the height of its tallest description, so a choice moves nothing. Each segment is named by its setting and its choice ("Reduce motion: Follow the desktop") with the selected one toggled, and a press applies the choice at once (Increase contrast applies the adjusted palette in place) and saves it. Apply changes across controls, previews, gutters, and selected rows, then persist app preferences outside repositories. Show failed saves clearly.

Column controls affect visibility and widths, preserve the commit-message column, and offer a layout reset. Git identity is a separate, explicit save for the displayed repository, its Name and Email labels in 12 px medium, as the Projects setting titles beside them are, each also its field's accessible name, never the placeholder; show the current identity and signing state without implying that appearance settings alter Git configuration. Retain unsaved default-branch and identity text through page visits and repeated Settings commands. An unedited identity follows external effective changes; a changed canonical worktree initializes its own identity. Reset edits explicitly restores the current repository value. Named profile management is linked here and in the header.

Below 1060 px of the page's width (the window's, less the project list where it shows), stack the settings groups into one scrollable column. Keyboard focus stays in view on the page: when Tab or Shift-Tab moves focus onto a control the page has scrolled away, a card, switch, button, checkbox or field alike, the page scrolls by the least that shows it whole with the focus ring's 3 px of room from its edge, in the frame that first draws the focus, and a control taller than the page's view shows its top edge with that room, from above or below, and stays there; a click on a partly visible control scrolls nothing. Such a tall control counts as shown while its top edge is in view with that room on either side of it, so a resize after which it would fit whole but is cut shows it whole, one that cuts its top edge shows that edge again, and any other leaves the page where it is; counted as cut because it never fitted whole, it once stayed cut when a resize made room for it. A segment of **List density**, **Reduce motion** or **Increase contrast** shows its whole row that way, its title, its description at its reserved height and its segments, so the description a choice changes is on screen beside it; a row taller than the page's view shows the focused segment with its ring first. Revealed alone, the segments left Increase contrast's description below the fold of a 1,480 × 973 window. A switch's row is its switch and names its setting once, at every width and text size: its title, in 12 px medium at the row's left edge, and its description below it are drawn inside the switch before its track, which ends the row, so a press anywhere on the row toggles it and hovering the row lifts the track; for assistive technology the switch is named by its title and described by its description, which nothing else on the row reads; it draws no label of its own, and its title takes the disabled label's dim while it is disabled. **Follow system appearance** once also drew its words beside its track, so at 18 pt in a 461 px window its title wrapped to three lines beside them; every switch's row read its title before its description, apart from the switch, and only the 36 × 20 px track answered the pointer. The Default branch field is named by its title, as the External editor field is, and its row's text reads only its description. A row whose description changes with its control, as **Use the desktop's monospace font** does between off, looking up and its answer, keeps the height of the tallest of those descriptions, so toggling a switch moves neither it nor the rows below. Theme cards expose hover feedback in their caption and mark the selected choice explicitly. Save/Reset reflect edited values, Return submits the focused form, and a failed preference write offers Retry save. A text setting's save action, the external editor's **Save editor** as the default branch's **Save**, sits beside its field at the card's trailing edge, as wide as its content, in the toolkit's default Button, whose fill keeps its shape while there is nothing to save; a transparent action stretched to the card's width drew only a centred dim label there.

### Custom theme editor

**Your themes** sits directly under the theme picker as a grouped card on the subtle surface: the title, a short description, **New theme…** (the shared 28 px `button` helper, disabled at the 32-theme bound with the reason and the remedy in its tooltip: "Up to 32 custom themes can be saved. Delete one before adding another."), and the saved themes as 30 px rows whose content aligns with the card title (the hover surface bleeds into the card padding), or the muted line "No custom themes yet." when there is none. The row list is `min(rows, 8) × 30 px` tall and virtualized (`uniform_list`, as History): with up to eight themes it is exactly the stacked rows; with more it scrolls inside the card with the toolkit scrollbar on its right edge, which reserves its own track so the thumb never covers **Delete…**, only the rows in view are drawn, Tab and Shift-Tab still walk every row's **Edit…**, **Export…** and **Delete…** in order across the viewport boundary, and the row holding focus is scrolled into view, within the Settings page too, with the ring's 3 px of room from the page's edge. The list keeps the shared focus ring's 3 px of room above its first row and below its last inside the card's existing gaps, and every row's fill, hover and import highlight alike, is painted beneath all the rows, so a focused row action's ring is whole in every slot and the rows and card do not move; and a wheel step over the list moves the list while it can still move that way, and the Settings page only once it cannot, as nested scrolling chains natively. A row shows a five-token swatch strip (canvas, panel, accent, added, removed), the name in primary text, the base in muted text, a checkmark when it is the active theme, a warning glyph with a count when `readability_issues` is non-empty, and compact **Edit…**, **Export…** and **Delete…** actions whose accessible names name the theme ("Edit Sunset theme"); **Export…** says what it writes in its tooltip. **Import…** sits beside **New theme…** in the card header, without an icon, disabled at the same bound with the same reason and remedy in its tooltip ("… Delete one before importing another."). The two actions stand beside the title and description, centred on the title's first line, while the text keeps at least 180 px (scaled with the interface text) beside them; narrower, as at 18 pt in a 461 px window, they take a line of their own at the title's left edge, the header's 0.75 rem gap below the description, and the description takes the card's whole width, as the text sizes' rows wrap their controls. **New theme…** keeps its visible label as its accessible name, and **Import…** is announced as "Import a theme file". A delete, export or import leaves one line at the foot of the card and never two: a failure in the warning color, or a finished export or import in muted text naming the written path or the imported theme. That message wraps to at most two lines and, when it is a single paragraph, ends in an ellipsis when it would run past them (a native dialog's failure is instead its guidance, which on Linux names xdg-desktop-portal and its FileChooser backend, then the service's own error in muted text on a line of its own, shortened where the message is built, so what the bound can cut is the quoted error; the repository picker's alert and the Projects error draw that line muted too), and the path and any text quoted from a theme file are shortened where the message is built, so no message can grow the card and push the settings below it away. The row a just-finished import added is highlighted on the selected surface until the next card action — hovering it is not a card action and keeps the highlight — and importing adds a theme without applying it, so the checkmark still marks the theme the window uses.

The editor is a modal dialog titled **New theme** or **Edit theme**, 1,000 px wide at 13 pt when the window is at least the scaled 1,060 px and 648 px otherwise. Beyond its fixed 16 px panel padding and 16 px scrollbar track the width scales with the interface text size, as the rows do, so the longest description (Accent's) ends at least 12 px before the warning slot at every size and is never truncated at 18 pt; it is never wider than the window less 32 px:

- Header: **Name** input (28 px, as tall as the Base button), then the 200 px **Base** dropdown listing the twenty built-ins in Light and Dark groups with the current base checked; its menu is as wide as the button and as tall as the window below the header allows before it scrolls. New theme starts from the active theme's base with a free "My ‹base›" name. Choosing another base replaces an unedited draft at once; after edits an inline question asks **Replace colors** or **Keep colors**, and both adopt the new base.
- Body: token groups Surfaces, Text, Accent, Status and Diff in `TokenKind::ALL` order, each under a muted 12 px medium label, with 30 px rows of `label · muted description · warning slot · 16 px swatch · monospace hex Input · ColorPicker`; rows and labels keep their height inside the scrolling column. Where a narrow window at a large text size leaves a row too little room for all of that, as 18 pt in 461 × 490 does, the description gives way and then the label, which ends in an ellipsis with its accessible name whole and a tooltip giving the name and description, so the picker stays clear of the scrollbar's track and its focus ring is whole, on arrival after Escape closes the picker too (decided 2026-11-09): the row's fixed columns once ran 21 px past the column, under the track and past the panel's clip, which cut the ring on three sides. The hex field is seven monospace characters, the caret and padding (78 px, so a full value never scrolls its leading `#` out of view); the picker trigger sits in a 1 px border-color edge so it stays visible on its own color. The warning slot shows the glyph on every row whose token a warning's measure reads, so an edit to any marked row can move or clear that warning: each token the warning names; Selected and Accent for a pair on the hovered selected row, which blends them; Canvas and Text for a graph lane, whose set their brightness chooses; and for a pressed-step warning, which names Selected and Hover, also Canvas, Panel, Subtle surface and Accent, since its measure composites both fills over canvas, panel, subtle, hover, selected and the hovered selected row. In the wide layout the rows fill the left and a 300 px column on the right holds the settings-card miniature (`settings::theme_preview` on the draft, captioned with the name and base) above the **Readability** list; below the breakpoint the column stacks under the rows. The form, which holds the footer as its last row, caps its height so the panel's lower edge stays 16 px above the status bar's top edge (a window-capped modal panel, as the status strip below describes): the window less its client-decoration frame on Linux (the toolkit's window paddings, inside which the alert lays itself out, and the frame's 1 px border), the scaled status bar, the 16 px above it, and the alert's fixed chrome (a tenth of the padded window above the panel, the panel's 1 px border and 16 px padding at top and bottom, and the title and the half-rem gap under it). The body takes what the header, the base question and the footer leave by layout, so a footer that grows, such as a save error wrapped to two lines, takes its room from the scrolling body and never moves the panel's edge, and the token column and the side column scroll independently in the wide layout and the whole body scrolls when stacked. The token column (the whole body when stacked) is a bounded scrolling area and shows an always-visible vertical scrollbar in a 16 px track reserved at its right edge, so the thumb never covers the pickers; the wide layout's side column shows the same scrollbar only while it overflows (past about seventeen warnings at 1,440 × 900), in a 16 px track added at its right edge that the token column gives up, so the miniature and the list keep their 300 px and a column that fits draws as it would without one; the footer stays reachable at large text sizes and the panel ends 16 px above the status bar when the rows overflow, at every interface text size. Focusing a row's hex field or picker scrolls the row into view in the same frame that first draws the focus, and the Readability list is a focusable region (a 1 px accent outline when focused) that the keyboard brings into view the same way, in the side column when wide and in the stacked body otherwise.
- Footer: **Reset to base** (secondary) on the left; the save error, a muted "Saving…" while a save is in flight, **Cancel** (secondary) and **Save** (primary) on the right. As the last row of the alert's clipped body it keeps the focus ring's 3 px of room under its buttons inside the clip, which the alert's gap above its empty footer slot gives back, so a focused footer button's ring is whole and the buttons keep the panel's 16 px padding under them.
- Live preview: a valid edit updates the draft, and the whole window, dialog included, shows it through `Palette::apply`. The first edit of a frame applies in its handler, so the render the keystroke itself triggers already shows the draft; later edits in the same frame (a picker drag) coalesce to one further application at the next frame callback. The draft replaces the saved selection for every re-application while the editor is open (system appearance, text size) and nothing reaches the store before Save.
- Warnings: recomputed on each valid edit; each line names both tokens, the ratio rounded down and the minimum ("Muted text on Selected 3.9:1, needs 4.5:1"), or for the pressed-step rule the step ("Selected against Hover differs by 3 of 255 in one channel, needs 6"), beside a warning glyph; with none, the list reads "Every readability rule is met.", which covers the pressed step as well as the contrast minimums. When the list has warnings, the 12 px **Readability** heading shows their count beside it in muted regular text ("1 warning", "3 warnings"), a line under them says "Warnings do not prevent saving.", and warnings never block Save. The list's accessible name carries the count in every state ("Readability, 3 warnings"; "Readability, 0 warnings" with none).
- Validation: hex fields accept `#rrggbb` or `rrggbb` in either case. Anything else outlines the field in the removed color, also while it has focus, replaces the row's warning slot with a square glyph on the removed fill holding the control icons' stroke-drawn cross (`close.svg` at the slot's 14 px, whose ink reaches 4.5:1 about as often as the readability `!`'s), a different shape from the round readability glyph, so the cue is not color alone, adds "invalid" to its accessible name, keeps the last valid draft and disables Save; Return in a valid field rewrites it as lowercase `#rrggbb` and saves nothing. A name `validate_theme_name` refuses shows its message under the field, outlined the same way (also while focused), and disables Save.
- Keyboard: focus opens in Name and stays inside the dialog; Tab order is Name, Base, Keep colors and Replace colors while the base question is shown, then each row's hex field and color picker in group order, the Readability list, then Reset to base, Cancel, Save. The hex field is the primary control; the toolkit picker is an additional popover and shows a 2 px accent ring when focused, its swatches walked with the arrow keys ([Keyboard contract](#keyboard-contract)). Return is routed by focus: in Name it saves when Save is enabled, in a hex field it commits the value, and on a button it activates that button, so Return on Cancel cancels. Answering the base question returns focus to Base.
- Escape, Cancel and closing the dialog discard the draft and re-apply the saved selection; while a save is in flight they are refused so the result has a visible owner. Save keeps the dialog open with the error beside Save, the draft and the preview if the store refuses or fails; on success the dialog closes and the saved theme becomes the selection (Follow system turns off, as choosing any theme does), so the window keeps what the editor showed.
- **Delete…** asks in an alert naming the theme, with **Cancel** (secondary) and a danger **Delete theme** action, both the shared 28 px buttons; when the theme is active the message names its base, and deletion selects and applies that base. The alert opens with Cancel focused, and Tab stays inside the alert; it routes Return by focus: on Cancel it closes, on Delete theme it deletes, anywhere else it does nothing; Escape closes without deleting. After a delete, focus moves to **New theme…** in the first frame that shows the card without the row, however long the store takes to answer, unless the user has focused another control meanwhile.

### History and Compare

| Region | History mode | Compare mode |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Left | 220 px repository navigation | 44 px compact navigation rail |
| Center | Full-height commit graph and table | Full-height code or image comparison |
| Right | 320 px selected-commit details and changed files | Same 320 px details and changed-file list |
| Mode entry | Opening a repository or Back to history | Clicking a changed file or opening it with Return |

The earlier graph-above-inspector split is superseded. Vertical space belongs to the current task, and the right panel supplies context throughout. Use shared 1 px pane divisions and flat surfaces. Navigation resizes from 180–360 px, and the right inspector from 280–480 px, never wider than the room the center's minimum leaves it, so the window holds it whole; keep the center flexible. Use the native pane resize handles and visible column dividers. Preserve chosen navigation/inspector widths across page visits and restart; retain horizontal column scrolling and give the flexible center the remaining space.

### Shared repository header

Show the turtle mark, repository name and path, Projects, History, a Changes tab with its file count, Git identity, and Settings. Refresh remains available through the primary-modifier shortcut and the working-changes control. Distinguish the checked-out branch from the history scope. Keep write actions named and tied to the visible repository/remote target; do not label the whole application read-only. Place commit search in History's scope toolbar rather than allowing a large global search box to compete with code-space controls. Header groups align with pane edges; use 12–16 px horizontal padding and 8 px control gaps. The full header's return action keeps one slot in every repository mode, as wide as the labelled Projects where that shows, which Back, labelled “Back”, fills with its arrow and label at the slot's leading edge where Projects' folder and label stand, so its hit area, hover and pressed fill and focus ring take the whole slot and no empty room before the turtle reads as a missing control, and History's root keeps an empty slot where Projects stands among the trailing controls in the other modes, wherever that slot adds no row, so the turtle, the name, History, Changes and Pull requests keep one place in History, Compare and Changes and a click where Changes was never lands on Pull requests. Where the trailing controls wrap onto a row of their own, as at the default size in a 461 × 490 window, the profile and Settings lead that row from the header's left edge, under the return action, in every mode, and Projects follows Settings where it shows, so no mode opens the row with an empty slot and the profile and Settings keep one place across modes. A running write's progress label and Cancel appear and go without moving anything already drawn: no control, nor the header's height, nor the page below it, so a click aimed at Settings or the profile never lands on Cancel. They form one group, the label and Cancel after it, which gives way in the [one responsive order](#one-responsive-order), taking the richest form its room holds as the write starts: Cancel labelled (“Cancel operation”, drawn as wide as the wider of its labels, so pressing it, which turns it to “Cancelling…”, moves nothing) beside the label with the write's repository (“Creating commit… · GitTurtle”); then Cancel as the shared 28 px control with its close icon, named “Cancel the running Git operation” as the labelled Cancel is, its tooltip leading with its label (while a request of the GitHub panel runs, both forms are named after what they cancel and their tooltips say what stops, from the request's kind: “Cancel connecting your GitHub account”, “Cancel the pull request comparison”, where they once named the Git operation and said they stopped Git and its child processes); then the label without its repository, which stays in its tooltip and accessible name. Within its form the label takes the room there is up to its 180 px limit, a later, longer progress ending in an ellipsis. The group stands in the room the spacer leaves before the trailing controls, at its trailing end, where the spacer, laid out as if empty, holds it, so neither a line break nor the name's share of the row changes; or after the trailing controls where nothing flexible shares their line, on a row of their own or where Settings wrapped onto a line of its own; of the two it takes the place that holds the richer form, the spacer first. Where neither holds even the barest form, as in the unit tests' text system with “Working…” at 13 pt in an 800 px window and at 18 pt in a 900 px one, the status bar draws the group in its status line's place: the label with its repository, ending in an ellipsis where the line is short, and Cancel after it as the bar's 20 px icon control, named and tooltipped as in the header. The bar keeps Cancel's room beside its line, whether or not a write runs, wherever a write's status can come there, decided from the window's width and the text size alone, as if the header held its widest contents: where the widest busy label's barest form, the label at its 180 px limit, which a GitHub request's label reaches, beside Cancel's icon, would have no room in the header's rows with any of the contents a header can have at that width and size (Projects, History's slot or neither, the profile's label up to its 230 px limit, a Changes count of up to six digits), and in every compact row, whose repository name can take all its room. So its hints and the local-refresh warning keep their form and place when the group comes, and the idle hints change form only with a resize or a new text size, never as a branch, a repository's name, a count, the profile or the mode changes (the owner's decision of 2026-10-30: the room once followed the header's labels, so at a boundary a tab with a shorter name changed the idle hints). Where every header holds every write's status the hints and Details… keep that room; kept on every repository frame, it made them give way about 28 px early with no write running. The widest contents are those that leave the status the least room, not simply the longest labels: a profile too wide for the spacer's line wraps with Settings onto a line of their own, which holds the status, where a narrower one fills the spacer's line and leaves it too little. A row reserved in the header for the group was the alternative, and is not taken: the write's label is known only once it starts, so a row reserved before it would have to hold the longest label, at 18 pt nearly every width, and would stand empty whenever no write runs; the bar is where the write's outcome lands when it ends, and the compact row has no room to reserve at all. Where the group goes is decided before layout from the measured widths, and the trailing controls' form never depends on whether a write runs (the label and Cancel were drawn ahead of the trailing controls, so on the trailing controls' own row without the spacer the profile and Settings moved right by the group's width when a write started; then a row of the group's own below the header moved the page down by a row while a write ran, at 13 pt from 1,000 px down).

In a window too short for the full header and action bar to leave History its scope toolbar, search row, column header and three whole rows, or Compare its toolbar rows, review options, footer and three whole diff lines at the code text size, both take a compact form of one row each, in every mode alike. The choice is made before layout from the interface font, so no frame shows one form and then the other: the full forms' heights are predicted for the window's width from each control's measured label, its padding, icon and gaps, the rows the header and the bar wrap to and each row's tallest control, and the full forms stay wherever they fit, as at 1,000 × 680 at 18 pt or 461 × 490 at the default size. In the unit tests' text system a 461 px window keeps them from 457 px tall at the default sizes, from 469 with 24 pt code and from 685 at 18 pt, and a 640 px one from 625 at 18 pt; each is 8 px lower (11 at 18 pt) than while the header's profile picker was a 36 px tile, which made the header's wrapped row of trailing controls that much taller. Where the header keeps one row, as from 640 px wide at the default size (394 px tall, 409 with 24 pt code) or 1,000 px at 18 pt (537), the breakpoint is unchanged. The compact header holds the return action; the turtle, only where the name still keeps its scaled 100 px beside it; the repository name alone at its own width, its path in the tooltip and accessible name; right after it History and Changes as icon toggles (the commit and changes artwork) in their quiet group, the Changes toggle carrying its file count as a small accent badge on its icon's corner (“99+” above 99), ringed with a scaled 1 px of the fill it sits on (the toggle group's, or the toggle's own while it is selected, hovered or pressed) so the ring is a true cut-out, not a halo, and the glyph's strokes stop short of it in every palette; the cut-out is the outermost shape, a disc of that fill with the pill inside it, so no antialiased edge of the pill shows past it (drawn as the pill's border, the ring once let a half-pixel fringe of the accent through, 1.23:1 in Braden); Pull requests as the short label “PRs”; then, at the trailing edge, Projects, the profile and Settings. The name's room counts the Projects slot that History's root leaves empty, so the turtle, the name and the toggles keep one place in History, Compare and Changes and a click where Changes was never lands on another control. The profile there is one of its neighbours: a 28 px transparent control with their scaled icon, not the full header's filled picker. The compact action bar holds the branch picker and the sync state, which truncate before anything wraps (the tooltip keeps the counts; a repository at an edge shows its line there, and its action as an icon after Push, as [Selection, focus, and feedback](#selection-focus-and-feedback) lists), then Fetch, Pull, Push (still primary, and the bordered secondary in Working Changes, as in the full bar) and Targets as icons. Every control is 28 px. A collapsed control keeps its action, its accessible name (“History”, “Changes · 3”, “Pull requests”, “Fetch: Fetch from origin”, “Targets”) and a tooltip that leads with that name, and Tab reaches the controls in the full forms' order. The two rows share the panel surface with no rule between them and keep a focus ring's 3 px above the header's controls, between the rows and below the bar's, so at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 half tile they take 88 px where the full forms would take 285 (296 with the 36 px picker), and History keeps three whole rows and Compare several diff lines. Notices, banners and an expanded Targets take their room from the content in either form and never switch it; a running write's status takes room from neither. The compact row draws a running write's progress label and Cancel in the same group as the full header's, Cancel always its 28 px icon as every control on the row is, before Projects where the name at its whole width leaves the group room for its barest form, and in the status bar elsewhere, as at 18 pt in a 461 × 330 window, so the name stays whole and nothing on the row moves (the trailing controls once wrapped onto a second row when a write started, as at 461 × 400, and the group later took a row of its own below the row).

The action bar is always visible in repository modes. Its current-branch menu, ahead/behind counts, and named Fetch, Pull, and Push buttons make routine work discoverable; Push uses the primary treatment, except in Working Changes, where Commit is the filled action and Push is the bordered secondary in the same bounds (see the buttons paragraph under [Shared control contract](#shared-control-contract)). The counts are never cut: the full bar draws each arrow with its count and word (“↑ 1 ahead”); the compact bar, one row, draws the richest form whole beside the branch picker's whole label, the words, then each count's digits beside its arrow (“↑ 1”), then the arrows alone, and where even the arrows would not fit the picker gives up their room, its name ending in an ellipsis (`GitTurtle::sync_fit`, measured from the interface font at the interface size). The state's accessible name is always the counts (“1 ahead, 0 behind”), and the compact bar's tooltip leads with them; at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 half tile the counts read “↑ 0…”, or “↑ …” beside a longer branch name. Targets are expanded by default, showing a branch field with Switch/Create, the remote, the remote branch, and the push destination. Collapsing Targets leaves the main actions visible. The branch menu lists the current branch and up to 40 other matching local branches; its find/create entry focuses the branch field for larger repositories. Menus are built when opened.

The branch picker also opens contextual branch settings, a searchable branch chooser, tracking-branch creation, merge/rebase preparation, and remote configuration. Branch settings expose rename, safe deletion, and upstream choices with the selected branch and worktree occupancy visible. Right-clicking a navigator branch opens the same management context; its ordinary click remains browse-only. Remote forms distinguish fetch and push URLs and do not contact a remote when saved. The branch chooser and the remote manager open, once their list is read, with focus on their search field and its ring whole in the frame the dialog appears; Tab stays inside the dialog, and closing it returns focus to the control that had it. Show destination and integration consequences in a review dialog before writing; keep these occasional actions out of the permanent action bar.

### Named Git profiles

The secondary header picker combines an identity label, user icon and dropdown caret, with balanced native padding and a full-control hover/focus target. It is 28 px tall, scaled with the interface size, as every control in the header's row is (coordinator's decision, 2026-10-30: one height per row kind; it was a 36 px tile, 50 px at 18 pt, beside the 28 px Settings, 39 px at 18 pt). Labelled, it keeps its secondary fill as the branch picker does; as its icon, in a narrow header, it is a 28 px square. The shorter picker only gives room back: where the header wraps the profile and Settings onto a row of their own, as at the default size in a 461 × 490 window, that row is the controls' 28 px, so the page below starts 8 px higher (at y 199 there, not 207; 11 px higher at 18 pt), and the compact chrome's breakpoint moves down by as much (see the shared repository header); in a narrow header the controls beside the icon close up by the 8 px (11 at 18 pt) its square no longer takes, and a repository name with room to grow takes half of that. Truncate long labels within the 230 px control limit and retain effective name/email and configuration scope in its tooltip. An assigned profile that differs from effective Git settings is visibly marked **differs**.

The keyboard menu says what each of its rows is: the identity Git authors with now, as "Current: ‹name› <‹email›>" above a separator; the saved profiles under the heading "Saved Git profiles", or with none saved the one line "No saved profiles"; then **New profile…**, which opens the profile form, and **Manage profiles…**. Headings and notes are labels, not commands, and none ends in an ellipsis. Management supports named definition creation, editing and deletion; saving a definition changes app storage. Applying a profile is a separate review of the displayed canonical worktree and exact configuration scope. Disclose linked-worktree sharing, preserve global/inherited settings and existing signing requirements, and show errors without replaying an accepted write. Cancellation preserves recoverable form text. Deleting a definition leaves repository configuration, work and signing keys intact. Profiles contain author identity and optional public signing references; hosted-provider login and private-key storage remain separate. The [profile contract](docs/profiles.md) specifies persistence, scope and signing behavior.

### History mode

The left column contains All history, Local branches, Remote branches grouped by remote, and Worktrees. Its Local, Remote and Worktrees toggles, labelled or as their glyphs, and the header's History and Changes in either form, mark the selected one with one mark: its selected fill and a 2 px accent bar under its label or glyph, 3:1 or more against that fill (7.48:1 in Midnight, 4.41:1 in Daylight), since the fill alone stood 1.40:1 and 1.28:1 off the panel (`views::mark_selected_mode`). Decided by the coordinator: one selection mark for both forms, so a toggle that becomes its glyph as the room narrows keeps the mark it had, and the selection never rests on the 1.40:1 fill alone, as labelled modes and the header's toggles once did after the glyphs had gained the bar. Sections collapse and show subdued item counts. Branch prefixes can form folder groups to make thousands of references browsable. A compact filter narrows this hierarchy without losing the active selection. The navigation gives way in the [one responsive order](#one-responsive-order) at the width it is drawn at, decided before layout from the interface font: its Local, Remote and Worktrees toggles keep their labels where all three fit one row, and otherwise become their 28 px branch, remote and worktree glyphs, each named after its mode, with the name in its tooltip, before one wraps under the others, as "Worktrees" did at 18 pt and in the 180 px minimum at 13 pt; the filter, named “Filter branches and worktrees” whatever it shows, fits its placeholder in whole words, “Filter branches & worktrees”, else “Filter branches…”, else “Filter”, where it once read “Filter branches & worktre” cut at its edge at 18 pt; and the keyboard hint at its foot shows only where each of its two lines fits whole, and otherwise gives way whole, the tree's accessible name keeping its keys, rather than wrap inside a phrase. The width walk lays History out with the navigation at that minimum too. Mark the checked-out branch with a small HEAD indicator; selecting a different branch only browses it. Worktree rows show folder name and branch, with full path and detached/locked/prunable state available in details or a tooltip.

The center begins with a 40 px scope toolbar, a separate compact search row, and a 34 px column header. The search field is named “Search repository history” for assistive technology whatever its placeholder shows, and the placeholder fits the field in whole phrases: “Search repository history, author, hash…”, else “Search history, author, hash…”, else “Search history…”, else “Search”, which alone is cut before an ellipsis in a field too narrow for it, so at 18 pt in a 640 px window it reads “Search history…” where it once ended “…history, a” at the field's edge. Below it, the virtualized table fills the remaining height. Use these columns in this order:

| Column | Starting width | Treatment |
| --- | --- | --- |
| References | 140 px | Separate label column with compact reference badges, HEAD's branch first, then the other branches and the tags, and "+N" for the others inside the badge; full names on hover and in the row's accessible name; folds into the subject's pill in narrow History |
| Graph | 112 px | Thin, stable colored topology with a shared lane coordinate system |
| Commit message | 320 px, expands into spare room | Primary 13 px summary text; no inline ref chips beside it except narrow History's one folded pill |
| Author | 136 px | Muted 11–12 px text |
| Date | 92 px | Muted compact date; exact timestamp in inspector |
| SHA | 84 px, never narrower than seven characters | The interface font, as every object identifier; Git's default short form, seven hex characters, whole at every text size and density: the column is at least seven of the font's widest hex character at the current size, whatever width was saved (at 18 pt the default 84 px showed five); also available with copy in inspector |

Visibility and widths are user choices. Escape closes the Columns panel and gives focus back to its Columns button, its ring whole, wherever focus had gone, whether a key or the pointer opened the panel, and a press on the button that closes the panel with focus inside it does the same: focus left on a control inside the panel went with it, and no key reached anything until a click (decided 2026-11-09). A text field that holds focus keeps it, its own Escape being the field's. Where History is not drawn, as in Compare, the panel closes unseen and the same Escape goes on to Back. Keep the commit-message column visible, preserve selected columns at narrow sizes, and provide horizontal scrolling when they exceed the viewport. Where they exceed it but leave the subject its floor, no column is cut at the table's edge before the table scrolls: the subject ends at the edge, or takes the room short of the first column after it that the edge would cut, which then starts at the edge, as the subject does between 721 and 1,001 px at the default size in the unit tests' text system, where it once ran past the edge cut mid-glyph and Author, Date or SHA showed a sliver of its title. The columns past the edge keep their widths and scroll sideways, and the saved widths are untouched. Narrow History keeps the subject. Where the chosen columns would leave the subject less than its floor at the table's start, as the 177 px table of a 461 px window or the 316 px one of a 640 px window does at the default size, the subject takes what remains after the essentials. The floor is on the subject's own text, after the cell's insets and beside any pill: the width its header's whole title, "Commit message", takes at the header's 11 pt medium, measured from the interface font. It has no fixed figure, since it follows the font: with the bundled IBM Plex Sans on Linux (Asahi Omarchy) the title measures about 85.5 px at the default size and 117.5 px at 18 pt, and the unit tests' text system measures about 93 and 128 px. References fold into the start of the subject cell as one pill in the reference badge's lane tint and fitted label. Where half the cell's text room holds the floor, the pill is the badge itself, HEAD's branch where the row has it and otherwise its first reference, then "+N" for the others inside the chip; its name truncates, its count stays whole, and it takes at most that half. Narrower, a name cut to three to five letters says little, so the pill is compact: the first reference's glyph, a branch's or a tag's, and the number of references, tags included, about 24 px at the default size and 34 px at 18 pt. The pill's own tooltip lists every reference, so none covers the subjects being read, and the row's accessible name carries them after the subject ("… · References: main, release, origin/main · …"), as every row with references does beside the References column's badge. The graph takes its compact lane pitch, below, within the room the floor and the compact pill leave it, in whole lanes and never less than one: it keeps every lane whose node and half a pitch past it fit in that room, and no more width than those lanes take with their full margin, so the room short of the next lane is the subject's. How many lanes that is follows the font, as the floor does: with IBM Plex Sans on Linux (Asahi Omarchy) a 461 px window at the default size leaves the graph about 30 px, two lanes, and a 640 px window at 18 pt about 47 px, three, the third's node 37.7 px in with half its 13.8 px pitch after it; the unit tests' text system leaves about 24 and 40 px, one lane and two.
