The subject takes the rest of the viewport, never less than its floor beside the pill, ending at its edge so no sliver of the next column shows there, and truncates with an ellipsis; Author, Date and SHA keep their widths after it and scroll sideways. There the graph's and the subject's widths are not the user's, so their dividers stay drawn but do not drag; the compact graph's title shows only where it fits whole, and its lane buttons give way, as the selection reveals its lane and the command palette keeps Show earlier and later graph lanes. Like narrow History's rail, which leaves the saved navigation choice untouched, the fold leaves the saved columns untouched, so widening the window restores the chosen columns exactly. It is decided once per frame from widths measured once per font and size, the compact pill's count measured once for the most references a loaded commit has, and no name is measured, so a steady scroll asks the text system nothing. Not chosen: scrolling the table to the subject, or hiding columns. Header and rows use the same computed layout and horizontal offset; divider drags persist widths after release. A wider graph must not squeeze many branches into indistinguishable overlapping nodes. Keep one parent/child relationship per graph edge, with stable lane colors across scrolling and paging. Use 1.6 px graph strokes, a 3.8 px ordinary node radius, a 4.3 px merge node, and a 5 px selected node. Merge nodes have hollow centers and a distinct ring. Reference badges can carry a restrained tint from their associated lane, while the selected row uses the active theme's selection treatment. Tags are references too: a commit's tags follow its branches, by name, and count in "+N" and the compact pill. A badge or pill whose first reference is a tag, on a commit with no branch, such as a release tag on an older commit, is the same chip in the same lane tint and label colour, led by the 10 px tag glyph (a tag outline with its hole, `assets/icons/tag.svg`) a scaled 2 px before the name, where a branch's badge has no glyph; the compact pill shows the tag glyph in place of the branch glyph. The glyph, not colour alone, tells a tag from a branch, and the tooltip and the row's accessible name say "tag v1.0", so a tag reads apart from a branch of its name. Avoid saturated full-row branch bands and avatar-heavy topology.

Settings exposes graph lane spacing from 12–32 px, default 20 px, scaled with interface text. The graph column expands to the lane geometry's required width even when its saved minimum is smaller; the table scrolls horizontally and the saved column preference remains intact. Narrow History draws the lanes at a compact pitch, half the scaled spacing and never under 8 px, since nodes keep their radii: 10 px at the default size, so the graph keeps its shape in about half the width, and every row of a frame shares that pitch and lane offset, so selected and hovered rows stay aligned. A shared 10 px node margin and the same loaded-graph lane coordinates keep headers, rows and crossings aligned. A graph cell holds a lane whole where the lane's node and half a pitch past it fit inside the cell, never less than the selected node's 5 px radius nor more than the 10 px margin every graph keeps after its last lane; it draws nodes only on the lanes it holds whole, and its strokes end half a pitch past them, so a lane beyond the column, or before its first shown lane, shows no node or line cut at the column's edge; an edge toward it leaves toward it. Branch identity and topology are prepared on the worker and lists remain virtualized. Discontinuous search results show isolated nodes; an explicit bounded fallback must not invent edges through hidden or unavailable history.

Clicking a commit immediately selects its row and updates the right panel. It does not enter Compare mode. The right panel presents the complete wrapped title, available body, then quiet author/date/hash metadata and parent/base selection above the changed-file list. The metadata takes the inspector's width, never its content's, and the author is one line, whole or ending in an ellipsis inside its box, with the whole name in its tooltip and, as a label's value, its accessible name: at 18 pt in a narrow inspector the metadata once laid out wider than the inspector, whose edge cut "GitTurtle QA". Show the message immediately, with no disclosure or empty-body filler. Full-message and full-hash copy controls and the selected commit's Actions stay visible above the scrolling content. The changed-file heading and its count remain visible while the list scrolls. A file row has the semantic status icon and label described above, a filename, and a muted parent path, each on one line that is whole or ends in an ellipsis inside the row (`views::ellipsis_end`), as a Working row's are: GPUI ends a line only once its width, rounded down, exceeds its room, so with a plain truncation a name or path less than a pixel wider than its box was drawn whole and cut by the row's side. In an inspector where the name would keep less than the widest status word's width beside the word, as the 156 px one at 18 pt in a 461 px window, measured from the interface font, the rows leave the word out and give its width to the name: the status icon's distinct glyph and colour carry the status, here in its tile (at that size a Working Changes row, below, keeps only the tile's bare glyph, so its name stays whole), and the row's accessible name keeps the word, so neither the name nor its path shows only an ellipsis. There are no staging checkboxes. A highlighted file may be remembered, but comparison begins only on the user's file action.

Commit search reads beyond the loaded history prefix. Show its selected branch/worktree scope or All local refs, progress, and explicit Cancel/Continue controls. Search matches commit messages, author names, and hashes; it pins local reference tips so additional pages belong to the same search snapshot. A scan, time, byte, or retained-result bound must say that searching paused, rather than claiming no matching commit exists. Selecting a result pauses background search and opens its metadata through the usual selection path. Clearing search restores the retained ordinary history; Back from Compare retains the search and its results. Completed writes keep that scope and query in place. Restart explicitly includes new local commits and moved tips without silently broadening the search to All local refs.

File history is a contextual inspector for one path and anchor revision. Present its first-parent lineage and rename-following behavior explicitly, with Newer/Older revision controls and paged navigation. Each revision retains its actual old/new path and absent sides. Selecting a revision displays its comparison without discarding the surrounding repository history or working selection; Back restores that context. Rename detection and lineage traversal remain bounded background work, with useful errors when a limit prevents reading deeper history.

### Compare mode

Clicking a changed file replaces the central history table with its comparison and collapses the left column to a 44 px rail. Keep the right inspector and changed-file list in the same location, with the active file visible. Switching files replaces the central content immediately without leaving Compare mode. Use a visible Back to history control with a left arrow at the top of the comparison; the compact rail also provides a labeled history-return action.

The commit inspector reserves at least half its height for changed files. Its message and metadata scroll within a bounded area with a visible scrollbar where they do not fit, and draw none where they do, as a one-line commit's do at 18 pt in 1,480 × 800 and 1,000 × 680 windows and at the default size in 961 and 1,240 px wide tiles 520 px tall, the metadata ending at its last row with no padding below it. Like the scrolling lists below, an area shorter than the toolkit's shortest track, an unscaled 56 px, draws no scrollbar and keeps the cue as a soft fade at each edge the message continues past, in the inspector's panel colour, as File History's area does at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window, where it holds the message's first line, shorter than 56 px; a sliver shorter than one fade draws neither, as in an inspector whose 45% cap lies just past the copy controls' reserved row, about 112 px tall at 18 pt, where the track drew a 16 × 2 px strip on the panel's bottom border (decision, 2026-11-05: no scrollbar draws outside its own scroll view, and one with nothing to scroll draws no track). The area is as tall as the message and metadata, measured at the inspector's width, and one inset after the last parent as wide as the metadata's inset above its first line, up to 45% of the inspector, so Changed files follows P1 at the inspector's rhythm; that inset gives way before the details scroll, and a message too long for 45% stops there and scrolls (`commit_message::message_extent`). (Decided 2026-11-01: the area kept a fixed 45%, which left about 60 px blank between P1 and Changed files at 13 pt.) File History's message area follows the same rule with the revision's details after the message: as tall as the message and those details, one inset after the revision's paths, up to 45% of the room between File History's header and footer, so its revisions follow the paths at that rhythm (decided 2026-11-01: the fixed 45% left 93 px between a one-line commit's paths and the revisions at 13 pt in a 1,000 × 980 window), and never shorter than its toolbar and the message's first line whole, with one revision row whole below it: File History's header and footer, which scroll their own rows, give way down to their first row first, a failed page's notice gives way down to a line of its message and Retry page, its rows scrolling, and the row stays whole where the message takes its whole share, and a panel shorter than all of them scrolls (the shares left the message a 15 px viewport, drawn empty, and the revisions 55 px of a 94 px row at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window, and a failed page's notice left them about 31 px at 461 × 420). While it scrolls, the panel keeps the overflow cue's gutter at its right with the scrollbar in it, its toolbar, message and top row laid out in the room the gutter leaves, and Tab and Shift+Tab reveal each of its stops whole with its ring's room: a control in the header's, a failed page's or the footer's own rows in those rows first, then in the panel, and Copy message and Hash with the whole message panel in view, as is the revision list where the keyboard places focus on it (at 18 pt in a 461 × 420 window Tab moved onto the anchor path's copy control hidden in the header's rows and, after a failed page, onto Older page below the panel's fold). File History's top row gives way in the responsive order: Close, labelled Back, becomes its icon with its label in its tooltip, then the title "File history" goes, so Close stays whole inside the header with its ring's room; the anchor's line wraps inside the header. Full-message and full-hash copy controls and the commit's Actions remain at the top. In an inspector narrower than their labelled row, as in a narrow window at a large interface size, the message area reserves the rows they wrap to, so they never cover Changed files, and where Copy message does not fit even on a row of its own it keeps only its icon as a 28 px compact control, with its accessible name and a tooltip that leads with its name. Hash then keeps only the same copy icon, a 28 px compact control named "Copy full commit hash" whose tooltip leads with "Hash", so the two share one row, as in an 18 pt half tile; copied, it shows the check with the tooltip "Copied" and is announced, as every icon-only copy button is. Each parent's control reads “P1 · ‹hash›”; in an inspector narrower than the widest such label, measured from the interface font, as the 156 px one of an 18 pt half tile, it reads “P1”, its tooltip and accessible name keeping the parent's full hash, so the metadata and its date line stay inside the inspector. Tab and Shift+Tab meet every parent's control in its place after the message's text, under a long message too, whose metadata lies below what the area draws: the key brings the control into the area, and the first frame that shows the focus shows it whole, with its ring's room, the area keeping that room below the last parent where it scrolls (decided 2026-11-09: under a long message Tab passed from the message to the files, and the parents could be reached only by scrolling to them). A click reveals nothing, and scrolling the message by hand is not undone, the control keeping focus for the keyboard to go on from. The area's fades stop short of the ring of the parent's control holding focus, as the scrolling lists' do, and come back whole as focus leaves it. The Changed files header keeps its count whole and its label whole or not at all: in an inspector too narrow for the whole label beside the count, such as the 156 px one of an 18 pt half tile, measured from the interface font, the label leaves rather than reading “Change…”, its icon and count staying, the count named “Changed files · 12” in its tooltip and accessible name; in an inspector the window pins narrower still the count leaves too, so neither is ever clipped. Title/body text is virtualized in bounded pieces, including exceptionally long titles and unbroken paragraphs. It keeps clear of the message's scrollbar by the bar's own 16 px, the same at every interface size, and a scaled 4 px; a reserve that scaled whole with the interface size gave the bar 11.7 px more at 18 pt and broke the title “Add polyglot.png” before the file name's last letter in a 461 × 490 window. The title, body and the author/date/hash lines read as one selectable text: an I-beam over the text, a drag that crosses pieces and scrolls past the area's edge, a double-click word, and the theme's fitted text-selection color behind the selected glyphs, on which text reads at 4.5:1 or more (as does the muted metadata in every built-in theme). Copy takes exactly the selected bytes of the loaded message, and Select All in the focused message equals Copy message. The selected commit's recovery Actions stands at the trailing edge of the copy controls' last row, outside the scrolling message and metadata, so it stays in view however far they scroll and they keep its row for their own lines (as the metadata's last row it sat below the fold at 18 pt in a 1,000 × 680 window, and its row drew a scrollbar at 18 pt in 1,480 × 800). Where its label and caret fit on that row at the width the inspector is drawn at, measured from the interface font, it reads “Actions” with its caret, in the same 28 px compact control as the copy controls and the Changes header's labelled Actions, as wide as its label and caret, with the palette's hover and pressed layers; otherwise it keeps only its more-actions icon (three dots) as a 28 px control named “Selected commit actions”, its tooltip leading with “Actions”, as in the 280 px inspector of a 1,000 × 680 window and the 388 px one at 18 pt. That is the width the window draws, which can be narrower than the inspector's saved width: the pane keeps the size a drag gave it and scales it with the window, so where a tiling window manager narrows the window after it opens the inspector is drawn narrower than it was saved, and its controls still share one row. Where not even the icon fits beside the labelled copy controls, as in the 240 px inspector of a 461 px half tile at the default size, Copy message and Hash keep only their icons too, so the three share one row rather than Actions taking one of the message's. File History uses the same reading order and copy controls while preserving revision navigation and the exact before/after path context; a revision's details take the commit metadata's inset and, as it does, end at their last row with no padding below it, so details that fit draw no scrollbar. Window, pane, density and interface-size changes reveal the selected commit and changed file with the minimum necessary scroll; ordinary scrolling and initial restored viewports remain undisturbed.

The comparison header has a 42 px minimum height and keeps the path, copy action and history/blame actions together. The path uses 12 px medium text with its full value available on hover. Comparison modes form a separate quiet group; at narrow widths or enlarged text the group wraps onto another line, preserving a useful path allotment (220 px, or the whole row in a column narrower than that) and reachable actions, and in a column narrower than the labelled group, measured from the interface font, the modes keep one row as a single control that reads the current mode with a caret (“Diff”), named “Comparison mode: Diff”, whose menu lists the modes with the current one checked, as the review options keep one row with their Options menu; on a row of its own below the path, that transparent control gives its 6 px inset back to the toolbar's padding, so “Diff” starts at the path's left edge; the path keeps its room before its actions keep theirs. Where the whole path does not fit beside a labelled Blame, Blame keeps only its icon (the blame artwork, `blame.svg`: a margin column of two segments, one per commit, ruled off from the four lines they wrote, as Blame's own view sets its attribution margin beside the text; coordinator's decision, 2026-10-30, a glyph that reads as attribution: the commits it once drew as two dots on a rail read as a bulleted list, and a person beside the lines would repeat the profile's person, one glyph for two meanings) as a 28 px control named “Blame”, its tooltip leading with that name, and gives its label's width to the path. Where it does not fit beside the actions' icons either, as “README.md” at 18 pt and “assets/turtle.png” at 13 pt in a 461 px window, Copy file path, File history and Blame fold into one 28 px File actions control showing the overflow chevrons (“»”, macOS's toolbar overflow, which this fold is), named “File actions”, its tooltip leading with that name and listing them, and its menu offering them for the file it was built for; Copy file path chosen there confirms on that control, the check taking the chevrons' place and its tooltip reading Copied. The more-actions dots stay with the inspector's Actions, which shares this row in a narrow window, so a row shows one control with the dots. (Decided 2026-11-01: File actions showed the dots beside the Working inspector's and the commit's compact Actions, two identical “…” in one row that read as one control drawn twice.) Where not even that leaves the path its room, it is elided in its middle, as macOS elides a path, so its top directory and its file name both stay: the top directory and the name around the ellipsis with as many of the nearest whole directories between them as fit (“crates/…/src/views.rs”, “crates/…/views.rs”), else as much of the top directory's start as fits before the ellipsis (“ass…/turtle.png”), down to “…/” and the name, its full value still on hover and as its accessible name (`views::path_tail`). Where not even “…/” and the name fit beside File actions, the name alone stands beside the control, and a name too long for its room, there or wider than the column itself, is elided in its middle, keeping its start, the larger half, and its extension (“Reposito…Sidebar.tsx”, `views::name_middle`), the whole path still its tooltip and accessible name. (Decided 2026-11-01: the path elided from its start, so a long one kept its name and its nearest directories but never its top directory, and a name too long for its room lost its start; macOS keeps both ends of each.) The header's repository identity elides the same way in the room the header leaves it, so nothing moves: its path keeps its top directory, an absolute path's root with it, and its last component ("/tmp/…/compact"), and its name keeps its start, the larger half, and its end ("sysp…mod"), in the full header and the compact row alike, the whole name and path staying the heading's accessible name and its tooltip (`GitTurtle::shown_identity`; decision, 2026-11-02: both ended in an ellipsis, "/tmp/gitturtle-evi…" and "sysprev…" for "sysprev-mod", cutting the part that tells two tabs apart). Unlike Compare's path, the header's path line never drops its directory, since the name line above it already shows the name: where "…/" and its last component no longer fit, the top directory stays whole and the last component is elided in its middle in the room it leaves ("/tmp/gitt…qa87", or "/home/…/GitT…urtle" past directories between), down to "…/" and the elided component, and only where not even that fits the component alone (`views::location_tail`; decision, 2026-11-02, QA-87 #14: the line once kept the component alone, elided in its middle, so it read as a second elided name beneath the name and lost "/tmp"). In every case the path's row keeps one line for every file, so the toolbar keeps one height and Compare's minimum (`compare_minimum`) counts every row it draws. (Decided 2026-11-01: File actions once wrapped onto a row of its own below a name whose “…/” form fit the column but not beside the control, as in a 461 px window, which moved the comparison down as the selection changed between files and was a row the minimum left out; one line moves nothing already drawn.) The actions wrap below the path only where they alone overflow the column. Text uses Diff / Split / Before / After; images use Fit and zoom controls. Give the remaining central width and height to content. A small secondary line can show object identity, file mode, or image dimensions when useful; narrower than its line, it drops the object IDs first and then truncates with an ellipsis, the whole line in its tooltip, so no character is clipped at the column's edge. Keep implementation details out of the main reading area.

Blame always shows its source lines. They keep at least three rows wherever the window can hold them with the header, whose lineage note takes one line, its whole text in its tooltip and accessible name, and in a pane too short for it with three rows and the footer's least, decided before layout from that room, the header draws compact and gives that line up to the source line, whose tooltip and name say it and whose press, the keyboard's way to it, shows it beneath, its accessible state saying whether the note is open, while Reload keeps its icon alone in a narrow pane, its tooltip leading with its name, and the heading keeps the file name, its path giving way from its middle (`views::path_tail`), and “Blame · ” giving way where the room beside it cannot hold even the name's least form, the whole path after “Blame · ” in its tooltip and name; and the footer below them, the selected line's summary, actions and line history, gives up its height first: down to one control with its ring's room, it scrolls within itself, cued by its scrollbar or, below a 56 px track, its edge fade, its row of actions wrapping rather than running past the pane, and an action wider than a narrow pane giving way to an ellipsis, its ring whole; compact where its full form could not show its first row of actions whole with its ring, decided before layout from the pane alone, the header with a shallow repository's boundary line, three rows and that row, so arrowing through lines never changes its form, the full form's summary and detail taking two lines each at most and their whole text in their tooltips, it takes the header's padding and one line of summary, its detail in the tooltip and name, so the first action starts in view; where it has its full height, 260 px at 13 pt and scaling with the text, and one row of actions, a line's history keeps its own list, capped at 130 px and scaling likewise, so the summary and actions stay where they are, the list giving way inside the footer where the text above it wraps past that height, and only a shorter pane, or one too narrow for that row, merges the rows into the footer's scroll, where History of line's own label reads "Tracing…" while they are read, in the bounds its name gives it so the row of actions does not move, nothing scrolls away from it, and the outcome is announced politely once; a pane still shorter than those parts scrolls as a whole, as File history's does; and each control is revealed with its whole ring as it takes focus (decided 2026-11-08: at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window the footer left the lines 2 px and lay past the pane's foot, its Buttons' rings clipped away). In a window with room for everything nothing moves.

Back to history restores the existing graph, selected commit, reference scope, search query, loaded pages, column widths, and scroll position. Restore keyboard focus to the selected history row without jumping to the top. Keep the right panel's selected file and scroll position. Preserve the selected file path across adjacent commits when it is changed in both; otherwise select the first file in the right list without forcing a mode transition.

At narrower windows, History can collapse its navigation to the same rail while its chosen columns scroll horizontally, or, narrower still, take narrow History's columns (History mode, above). Compare keeps its 44 px rail, scaled with the interface size, and right file list while the preview takes the remainder. Right-panel resizing remains available in either mode. Keep dragged pane sizes in the running workspace even when a page temporarily hides those panes. Tiling window managers such as Hyprland ignore the 1,000 × 680 window minimum, so a half-screen tile is often 600–800 px wide: below an 800 px repository width, where navigation and the history table (520 px) no longer fit beside the narrowest inspector (280 px), History shows the rail (these widths, and the column and inspector minimums below, are those of the default interface size and scale with it, as the rail does) without changing the saved navigation choice, the rail's navigation button is disabled with a tooltip asking for a wider window whether navigation was shown or hidden, Toggle Sidebar leaves the choice as it is, with a polite announcement and no visible notice that branches and worktrees are unavailable until the window widens, the command palette lists it as unavailable for the same reason, and the content column accepts 240 px beside the rail. Where the rail, that column and the 280 px inspector no longer fit, as in a half tile of a display about 1,000 px wide at a fractional scale, the inspector yields to 240 px first and then the content column to 176 px, so the inspector stays whole down to about 460 px. In a window narrower still, or at a larger interface size, the column keeps its 176 px minimum, which holds History's compact paging, and the inspector takes exactly the width left beside it and cannot be resized there: at 18 pt in a 461 px window, a 61 px rail, a 244 px column and a 156 px inspector. Below an 800 px window width, scaled with the interface size, the header's Projects and profile buttons keep only their icons, tooltips and accessible names so it stays on one row down to a half tile of a 1,366 px display; narrower, the header and the Git action bar wrap onto a second row rather than clipping, or, in a window too short for those rows, take their compact rows. History's scope toolbar stays one row at its 40 px height: in a History column narrower than its labelled row needs, measured from the interface font at the interface size (padding, gaps, the branch icon, the count, the labelled Columns, Latest, Previous and Older, and the scope name cut to its first character and ellipsis), Columns, Latest, Previous and Older keep only their icons and accessible names as 28 px compact controls, and their tooltips lead with those names ("Older · Continue the captured history…"). Latest shows an up arrow, since the newest rows are at the top, and Previous and Older show up and down chevrons, a quarter rem apart; the scope name and count give way first, measured the same way. The count comes first, whole or not at all, and the name truncates beside it only where at least its first character and ellipsis fit, so as the column narrows the name leaves, then the count, and neither shows a clipped or lone ellipsis; the branch icon's tooltip names the scope and count. This keeps paging inside even the narrowest column, 176 px at the default interface size and scaled with it, where the name and count leave entirely. Where the labelled row fits, the toolbar is unchanged. History a quiet read has found and the list does not show (after a Fetch from Changes, say, or below History's top, where the list keeps its rows) is cued in this toolbar, never in a row of its own: a full-width "1 new commit" bar above History once pushed the page down 35 px. The cue's words ("1 new commit", "History updated") follow the count in the primary text at the count's size, whole or not at all; the labelled row is measured with them, so the controls become icons before the words go, and in the compact row they come before the count and the name. Latest, which reads current local tips and ends the cue, is its action: while the cue lasts a 5 px accent dot sits 1 px inside its top right corner, clear of its label and at least 2 px clear of its up arrow's ink (the 6 px dot 2 px inside the corner stood 0.9 px from it at 18 pt), and its name and tooltip lead with the cue ("Latest · 1 new commit"), so in a half tile, where the toolbar keeps only its controls, the dot still shows it and assistive technology hears it. The cue is announced once, politely, in the same words as History scrolls. It ends once History shows the rows it announces: a quiet read that History follows at its top, as the reads after a Pull or a Push from History do, installs them there and ends it, as Latest and Refresh, which capture current tips, do; it once stayed through Pull and Push with the new commit already the top row. A control's tooltip wider than the window wraps its text inside it with equal margins on both sides instead of running off an edge; an element's tooltip, such as a file row's or the review caption's, wraps to the same width, but GPUI places it from the pointer rather than centring it on its trigger, so its two margins can differ.

### Working Changes

Separate staged and unstaged groups, with whole-file Stage/Unstage actions and clearly named all-files actions. A file with both kinds of edits appears in both groups; selection determines which comparison is shown. Keep a persistent “Create a commit” composer below the file groups, with the current branch, staged count, distinct Title and optional multiline Description fields, and full-width “Commit N files” action. The title counter gently flags summaries beyond 72 characters without rejecting them. It counts the title as committed: what an input method is still composing in the field is not yet text, so the count changes as the composition is committed, when Pinyin's letters become one character (`workspace::GitTurtle::title_length`; it read “6/72 suggested” while fcitx5 composed “U+4e2d”, QA-92). Preserve title and description formatting in Git, persist each canonical worktree’s draft across restarts, and keep it on failure. Clear only the draft that matches a successful commit. Identify missing Git identity near the composer. Unified text previews place hunk actions and changed-line selection beside the relevant lines, with a temporary selected-lines action at the bottom. Unsupported partial changes retain whole-file actions. Conflicted files show both named sides and an optional base, with a focused manual-resolution editor, complete-side choices and an external-editor handoff. A pending-operation banner provides explicit Continue, Abort and Stop-and-keep-files actions; ordinary commit creation resumes after the operation ends.

Fit the composer to the space remaining beneath repository controls and feedback. Keep a usable file list and the commit button visible; scroll extra composer fields, guidance, and errors inside their own area, and where the body cannot hold the list and the composer whole, scroll the two together (below). The composer prefers the room its rows take at the interface size (the title, the Description section and any guidance, such as “Set your commit identity”), within the list's reserve. The Description section (its label, field and the hint below it) is always drawn whole and never leaves for room: where the composer is shorter than it prefers, the description's field gives up whole lines, down to one, then the file list gives up the rows one line needs, down to its floor, then the composer's heading leaves (below), and where even that leaves too little for one line, as in a 461 × 490 window at either size, the list and the composer scroll together as one area in the app's own view. In that form nothing is pinned: the heading has already left, and Commit, at the composer's foot, is reached by scrolling or by Tab. There Tab and Shift+Tab reveal each stop whole by the least scroll, a gap clear of the body's edges: the list, the title with its label, the Description section with its label and hint, and Commit, when enabled, down to the composer's foot, so no part of “Description · optional” shows cut at the body's edge or the commit action. Commit, disabled, stays out of the Tab order, as disabled controls do on both platforms; its accessible name and tooltip give the reason (“Stage files to commit”, “Write a commit title”), and its drawn label does where it fits; with nothing staged the hint revealed with the description says so too (“Stage files to include in your commit”). The hint is the field's accessible description, so a screen reader reads it with the field, and its own line is hidden from assistive technology, so walking past it does not read the words again (decided 2026-11-09). The scrolling form keeps the Description section in the Tab order (decision, 2026-11-07: the section once left the Tab order there, and with nothing staged Commit was drawn below the window, so Tab went from the title to Main Menu). When the list's rows change in number while focus is in the list, the scrolling body comes back to its top, showing the list and its selected file; with focus anywhere else, on a composer stop, in the filter, on another control or on nothing, a refresh keeps the body's offset, since a redraw without a focus change scrolls nothing (decided 2026-11-07: returned to its top, a watcher's refresh moved Commit from under a pointer that had scrolled to it). A composer stop holding focus stays whole as the content around it moves: where staging a file gives up the list's Unstaged heading, the rows change in number, or the composer's own rows grow, as the draft-save note and its Retry come or the long title's note grows, the stop is revealed again by the least scroll, as a resize reveals it, never left where the change cut it (decided 2026-11-07: the title's label was cut at the body's top as a refresh staged a file). Where “Description · optional” does not share its line, “· optional” leaves and the field's accessible name keeps it (“Commit description, optional”). The composer's heading (“Commit to ‹branch›” beside the staged count) is never squeezed out or cut (an inspector that cannot keep it, below, drops it whole), and a short composer keeps the title's label and one line of its field between the heading and the commit action, a scaled 4 px from the heading, from each other and from the action. A taller composer keeps its own 0.5 rem rhythm the same way, from its branch line through the title, the Description section and any guidance to the commit action. The file list keeps its heading and first file row whole whatever else gives way (its rows through the first file: the Staged heading, the Unstaged one too while nothing is staged, and a directory's label when grouped), so its focus ring always surrounds a list that shows a file. Where its rows overflow it, the list is a whole number of rows tall, so the row at its edge is never cut through its text, as the Unstaged heading was in a 640 × 680 window: a taller composer takes the remainder, and with its share whatever its rows leave over, into its description's field, which grows past its preference to take it, since the field is the one row that reads better taller and the action stays at the composer's foot (at the default size in a 1,480 × 980 window the composer's floor once left the hint and the action 32 px apart, where the rhythm gives 6.5 px); a short one is never taller than its rows, so what its share leaves over goes back to the list in whole rows and the rest, less than a row, lies between the list's last row and the composer, never between the last field and the commit action (where up to 16.5 px once gathered, at 18 pt in a 640 × 680 window). No control above the list is cut, and none scrolls: where the inspector is too short for the filter, the selection controls and the Changes header whole beside that floor and the composer's least height (its rows with one line of its description), predicted before layout from the inspector's height and the interface font, the filter, Actions and Refresh share one row, Actions keeping only its more-actions icon and its name, and Directories and the selection's Stage and Unstage, with their counts, lead the Actions menu. Refresh joins them where the filter would keep less than a scaled 80 px beside it, and the selection line and the Changes count leave (the compact header's Changes badge and the Staged and Unstaged headings keep the counts). The filter's placeholder, "Filter working paths…", fits its field in whole words, since a shorter whole word reads better than a cut one: where it does not fit whole it reads the short "Filter", as in that row at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window, where it once read "Filte…", and only a field too narrow even for that ends it with an ellipsis at a grapheme-cluster boundary, as the composer's placeholders do, rather than cutting a glyph. Whatever its placeholder shows, the field keeps its whole name, “Filter working paths”, as the History inspector's changed-file filter keeps “Filter changed paths” while its placeholder fits the same way (“Filter” in the 156 px inspector at 18 pt, where it once showed “Filter changed” cut at the field's edge). Where the inspector cannot keep the composer's heading beside the list's floor and the composer's rows with one line of its description, in that row's form, the heading leaves as well, before anything scrolls, its branch staying in the commit action's tooltip and the action bar's branch picker and its count in the Staged heading and the commit action, so the title's label leads the composer (decided 2026-11-07: kept there, the heading scrolled with the composer at the default size in a 461 × 490 window). With two files staged, a 461 × 980 window at 18 pt takes the one row with the heading; a 461 × 490 window at either size and a 640 × 680 one at 18 pt take it without the heading and scroll the list and the composer together; a 640 × 680 window at the default size keeps every row and does not scroll. The filter, the selection controls, the Changes header and the composer's heading, fields and commit action share one leading inset. Stage and Unstage stay together, wrapping below Directories as a pair; in an inspector too narrow for the pair labelled, such as the 156 px one at 18 pt in a 461 px window, Directories, Stage and Unstage keep only their icons (the folder, plus and minus artwork) on one row as 28 px controls, with their accessible names (“Directories”, “Stage 0”, “Unstage 2”) and tooltips that lead with those names; the line below still counts the selection. The Changes header keeps Actions and Refresh at its trailing edge, labelled “Actions” with its caret in the same 28 px compact control as Refresh beside it, centred on the header's line (it was once the toolkit's 1.5 rem small Button, 19.5 px at the default size): where its labelled row does not fit, measured from the interface font, Actions keeps only its more-actions icon (three dots, `more.svg`, centred without a caret, which alone read as a disclosure arrow) as a 28 px control in the frame Refresh takes beside it, a button named “Working changes and recovery actions” that Enter or a click opens, its tooltip leading with “Actions”, “Refreshing…” moves into Refresh's tooltip, and then the header's icon leaves, then “Changes” and its count together, each whole: alone, the count would only repeat the Changes tab or toggle above it. The Staged and Unstaged headings fit the same way, together, so both keep one form: where its label does not fit, Unstage all or Stage all keeps only its minus or plus as a 28 px control named “Unstage all” or “Stage all”, its tooltip leading with that name; then the heading's glyph leaves, then the group's name, the glyph returning beside the count to tell the groups apart and the count naming “Staged · N” or “Unstaged · N” in its tooltip. At 18 pt in a 461 px window each heading reads its glyph, its count and the icon action, inside the list. A file row keeps its name whole, at least a short one such as “README.md”, content before decoration: where the name could not keep that beside them, every row's status word leaves, the path's tooltip naming the status; then the 26 px status tile gives way to its bare 16 px glyph, so the Stage or Unstage control can stay; then the control leaves, its action still first in the row's menu, which leads with Stage or Unstage at every width (decided 2026-11-08: one responsive order, so a row's menu reads the same however wide the window is), and the tile returns where it fits without it; then the bare glyph comes a `gap_1` from the name (4.5 px at 18 pt) rather than `gap_2`; and last the glyph leaves, only where not even that keeps the name whole, the status staying in the tooltip and the row's accessible name. Status outranks location: the folder below the name is drawn only beside the tile or the glyph, so it leaves with the glyph's last step, never after it, the path's tooltip and the row's accessible name keeping it; it lies beneath the name and gives it no room (decided 2026-11-07: beside the short body's scroll cue at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window the rows once read their names and folders with no status at all). At 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window, where “README.md” does not fit beside the tile beside the short body's scroll cue, the rows read the bare glyph, name and folder; in a 430 × 490 one, where it does not fit beside the glyph either, they read the name alone. The short composer's heading never cuts its verb: where it cannot keep “Commit to” and the branch's first character and ellipsis beside its icon and staged badge, the icon leaves, then the badge, whose count stays in the heading's tooltip and in the Staged group's heading row, so at 18 pt in a 461 px window tall enough to keep it, it spans the inspector, truncating only the branch. The composer's other texts fit its width too: where “Title” and the whole counter do not share their line, the counter drops “suggested” (“0/72”), keeping the word in its tooltip and accessible name (“0/72 suggested”); the title's placeholder reads the short “Summarize” where “Summarize the change” does not fit, as in the 156 px inspector at 18 pt, rather than “Summarize t…”, and “Summary” where not even that fits, as beside the short body's scroll cue at 18 pt in a 430 × 490 window, rather than “Summari…”; the Description section's hint wraps inside the column, as the composer's height is predicted, rather than running past it on one line, as it did at 18 pt in a 461 px window; the description's placeholder ends with an ellipsis at a grapheme-cluster boundary inside its field rather than clipping a glyph, as the title's short form does in a field too narrow for it, and where not even one character fits either shows nothing; and where its label does not fit beside its icon, the commit action reads “Commit”, keeping that label as its accessible name (“Write a commit title”) and leading its tooltip with it; in the narrowest form, where not even “Commit” fits beside its icon, as beside the short body's scroll cue at 18 pt in a 430 × 490 window (43.5 px for the word's 64.8), Commit keeps its word, whole, and drops its icon, keeping the same accessible name and tooltip (decided 2026-11-07: a lone glyph is less clear than the word for a full-width primary action, and the composer heading's icon already leaves before its text). Resizing the window or changing density keeps the selected working file in view, including after returning from Settings.

Git actions show the current branch, remote, and destination branch. Switching and creating branches are explicit actions; clicking a branch in the navigator continues to browse only. Keep the familiar Pull label and state its fast-forward-only behavior and exact source/destination in the tooltip. Push targets the shown branch without force. A branch's or a remote branch's dialog says why each action it refuses is unavailable, and the way to it, in that action's tooltip and its accessible description, as **New theme…** does at its bound: a branch another worktree has checked out "Checked out in another worktree. Rename it from that worktree." (Change upstream… and Remove upstream… alike), its Delete branch… the reason its note gives ("Checked out in …. Switch that worktree to another branch before deleting.", or the commits it would lose), Remove upstream… with none set "No upstream is set. Change upstream… sets one." before any other reason, since no worktree has one to remove, and a remote branch's Merge into current… and Rebase current onto this… while HEAD is detached "HEAD is detached. Switch to a branch first." (decided 2026-11-05 by the coordinator, by the rule that a control says why it is unavailable: those actions once dimmed with nothing to say why). Disable duplicate writes while an operation is running, show its outcome close to its origin, and refresh local state afterward. A timeout or missing result must explain that the effect may have occurred and require inspection before a deliberate retry.

Conflict resolution identifies the current branch and incoming operation side, including rebase's changed side meanings. Show an absent side explicitly for delete/modify conflicts. Retain manual text when selecting another conflict, changing views, or receiving an unrelated local refresh; a changed conflict identity requires reinspection before applying a resolution. Saving a manual result and staging a resolved file are deliberate actions. Where the window is too short for the conflict review, its body scrolls vertically as one, the versions keeping the larger of 100 px and their sides' header and message and the result draft its whole lines, rather than leave its actions below the window's foot (decided 2026-11-05: in a 461 × 490 half tile Save and stage result… lay below the window at 13 pt and at 18 pt, out of reach; 2026-11-09: a fixed 100 px left a side's message for no unresolved blocks, a binary file or a missing side wrapped under the footer in a 461 px window). The result draft prefers eight whole lines of code and gives way to the room the review's body has: where the body cannot show those eight lines whole with the focus ring's room, the draft takes the whole lines it does show, never fewer than three, below which the body scrolls and shows it by its top, so a focused draft is drawn whole with its ring; where they fit it keeps its eight (decided 2026-11-07: in a 461 × 490 half tile the window's tab strip, header, Git actions, merge banner and status bar leave the body about 145 px, and the eight-line draft, 162 px at 13 pt with 12 pt code and 234 px at 18 pt with 18 pt code, was cut at its foot with its ring wherever it took focus). While it overflows it keeps a gutter of the 16 px track and the focus ring's room at its right, where its always-visible scrollbar runs clear of every control and ring, and along a track shorter than 56 px it fades the edges it continues past instead, as the scrolling lists do (`tags::ScrollCue`); Tab or Shift+Tab onto any of its controls, a version's text or the result draft scrolls it by the least that shows that control whole with its ring, in the frame that first draws the focus (`focus_reveal::FocusReveal`), and so does focus the review moves into the result draft on the person's action, clicked or keyed: Next or Previous, Edit result, Restore saved draft and opening the file from its row (decided 2026-11-05: revealed only for the keyboard, a click on Next left the draft focused below the fold at 13 pt, about 15 px of it in view). The first frame that lays out an overflowing review lays it out with that gutter, and the first frame that draws the draft focused by such an action draws it whole with its ring where it stays, whether or not an earlier frame drew it (decided 2026-11-07: opening the file in a 461 × 490 half tile drew the draft at the body's top offset, below its foot, and the next frame, taking the gutter, rewrapped the controls above it and moved it down past the foot again, before a third revealed it). Where everything fits, nothing scrolls, it keeps no gutter and nothing moves. The block status keeps its height through a block review: "Updating conflict blocks…" stands in one cell with the status it replaces, unseen and unannounced, so the result draft does not move as the review begins, or lands the status it began from, and a chosen block's headers say "Updating conflict blocks…" during a review (decided 2026-11-09: at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 half tile its one line replaced the blocks' two, and the result draft moved 27 px each way). The review's saved-draft status stands beside Copy saved text and Restore saved draft where its line holds it, else on a line of its own, ending in an ellipsis where even that cannot hold it, its whole text its tooltip and accessible name. The banner of a paused operation across the repository page follows the one responsive order: its title and detail ("Merge in progress · main", "other · 1 unresolved file") are what it says, so Review files, Complete merge (Continue for a rebase, cherry-pick or revert), Abort… and Keep files… keep their labels only where both lines are whole beside them, and otherwise are their icons (the Changes page, a check, the reset arrow and an exit mark), each named by its label with it as its tooltip; an action that does not cancel or clear never takes the close mark, which the app keeps for those, so Keep files…, which ends the operation and leaves the working files as they are, takes an exit mark, an arrow leaving an open frame (`leave.svg`), unlike the page glyphs and Abort's reset arrow (decided 2026-11-07: it took the close mark, which read as cancelling the banner, then the modified file, the folded page Review files draws and the Modified status's glyph); the title then ends in an ellipsis down to its least, the operation's phrase whole ("Merge in progress…"), and below that the actions wrap beneath it as one group, each whole, on a line of their own whose first icon stands on the title's edge, its ghost frame its icon inset before it, as wrapped ghost Buttons do ("One left edge"; at the banner's padding they stood under the warning icon, an edge of their own) (decided 2026-11-07: labelled at any width, at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 half tile they ran past the window's edge, Keep files… drawn as "Ke", and at 13 pt there they left the title "Merge i…"). If stash restoration leaves conflicts, the saved stash remains available and resolution returns to ordinary Changes; do not invent a stash Continue or Abort operation.

### Pausing and recovering work

Keep recovery commands beside their targets: the Changes inspector's Actions menu holds stash save/browse and current-tip actions; selected commits expose an Actions menu and right-click commands. Avoid a universal Undo control. Each consequential action names the captured repository, branch, commit or stash, affected paths, and expected history or index changes before confirmation. If those inputs change, require a fresh plan instead of redirecting the old action.

Named stash creation offers an explicit Include untracked files choice. The stash inspector separates saved staged, unstaged, and untracked files and loads content only for an explicitly selected file. Stashes opens with focus on its stash list, its ring whole in the first frame, and closing it returns focus to the control that had it; with nothing saved, Refresh takes focus as the read lands, its name carrying the empty state's words, since the empty list holds nothing to reach and is no Tab stop. The window's Refresh key and the dialog's Refresh control, one action with one behaviour, read the saved stashes again from anywhere in the dialog: the selected stash stays selected by its commit, wherever the list renumbered it, with its inspection, focus and any Review restore… being prepared left as they were; the page shown stays the page shown unless the selected stash has moved to another page, which the list then shows, its row kept in view where it was in view before the read; a person who scrolled away from it is not pulled back. Only a stash truly gone clears them. Each press says its result once, politely, as the read lands: “3 saved stashes, stash@{1} selected”, on a later page “Saved stashes 41 to 80, stash@{50} selected” or, where that page holds one stash, “Saved stash 41, stash@{40} selected”, or “Saved stash no longer listed”; a read no press asked for says nothing. Its bounded, paged stash list and file list support keyboard navigation; the stash list and the saved files are each one Tab stop, moved through with the arrow keys, drawing the keyboard ring whole on their own edge. Stacked beneath the stash list, where the detail can be about 60 px tall (18 pt in a 461 × 490 window), the saved files are no taller than the detail shows with their ring, their rows scrolling inside them, so Tab reveals them whole. While their rows overflow them, the saved files show the always-visible scrollbar in a gutter at their right, as the detail around them does, where they are tall enough for a thumb that moves, and otherwise fade the edges their rows continue past; rows that fit keep the whole width (eleven saved files once had only a row cut at the box's foot to say more lay below). A stashed image's sides offer the open control Compare's image captions offer, by the same rule: in each side's caption, only where the captured bytes establish what is handed over (System preview on macOS; on Linux Open in default app for a raster image), labelled where its label fits beside the side's name and otherwise its icon, both sides in one form and on one line so their names and images stay level, with the short line saying why a click opened nothing beneath that caption, its whole answer in its tooltip and name. The form and the line come from Compare's own choice (`image_compare::open_place`): the narrower room either side's name leaves, both captions counted whether or not their side offers a control, so an added or deleted image's one control stands where a changed image's two do, on the names' line or the line beneath them (decided 2026-11-02, move nothing already drawn: counted from the offering sides alone, an added image's After control could keep a form or a line that a changed image's two had left). Stashes opens from Working Changes' Actions menu, from the palette's Browse stashes…, on Linux from the Main Menu's Browse stashes…, which follows Compare revisions… among the repository's inspectors, and on macOS from the menu bar's View ▸ Browse Stashes…, which follows Compare Revisions… there; each waits for a write and for the reload after it, the drawn menus stating the write's label at the row's end ("Refreshing…") with "available when … finishes" in the item's name, the menu bar's item, which has no room of its own at its trailing edge, after its title ("Browse Stashes… · Fetching…", the title VoiceOver reads), and the palette its reason (decided 2026-11-02: the Main Menu, and later the menu bar, offered no way to Stashes). The Main Menu, open as a write starts or ends or as the reload after it lands, builds its rows again in place, as the Actions menu does, so a row's wait never outlives its write and an item the write refused is offered once it has landed, the keyboard's cursor kept on its item, or resting there while that item waits, the rest said once (decided 2026-11-02: built only as it opened, Browse stashes… went on saying "Fetching…" after the fetch had ended). With that row the Main Menu no longer fits whole at 13 pt in the narrowest window (461 × 490), so there it keeps whole rows and scrolls behind its always-visible scrollbar, as it already did at 18 pt (coordinator's decision, 2026-11-02: a path to Stashes is worth one row past the fold in the smallest window; dropping another inspector to keep the menu whole would remove a way in). The keyboard reveals it in the detail with its ring, as it does a stashed document's. One rule holds for every captured side, as Compare's images and Stashes' documents already had it: the same bytes offer the same action wherever they are shown, so a stashed image need not be found in Compare to open it (Stashes once drew a stashed image's sides with no open control). Its heading counts what it holds ("Saved work · 2", or the range shown where Previous or More page through more, a page of one naming that stash, "Saved work · stash 41"); stacked above the detail in a narrow window it keeps one row, and while more are saved it shows its scrollbar rather than leave the next stash unseen beneath the pager. Restoration offers an explicit Restore staged state checkbox; explain whether Git will attempt the original index boundary. Restoration always keeps the stash, including on failure or conflict. Review restore… keeps Stashes open, its stash selected, until Git has prepared the restoration and read whether an integration is paused; the review then opens in its place. The selection belongs to its stash, by its stash commit, never by its `stash@{n}`, which renumbers as a stash is saved above it, from a terminal or the Actions menu, or dropped below it: Refresh keeps that stash selected where it now stands and reads its saved changes again, leaving focus where it was unless it lay on the stash's inspection, which hands it to the stash list (below), and selects nothing once the stash is gone; it once cleared the selection, and each row's accessibility node passed to the stash that moved into its place. Focus on the stash's inspection, which no frame draws while the list is read and none draws again once the stash is gone (the saved files, a group, a text mode, the comparison and whatever it draws, such as a model's canvas or timeline, Drop…, Review restore… or Details…), moves as Refresh, Previous or More clears it to the stash list, the nearest control of the same list still drawn: on the stash's row, which the list scrolls into view as it lands, or on the list alone once nothing is selected; focus anywhere else stays where it was (decision, 2026-11-06: a focus whose control stops being drawn never stays on it; focus once stayed on the undrawn saved files, and with the stash gone no key acted). A stash chosen while the list is read, from the rows it still draws, is read at once and leaves Refresh's answer to land: the stash stays selected by its commit where the answer puts it, its saved changes read again where the answer renumbered it, and nothing is selected once the answer no longer lists it (the click's read once replaced the list's, so the answer never landed). While Git prepares it, Review restore… says so in place, where the window's progress label lies beneath the dialog: its name says it is preparing at once, and once the preparation looks busy it reads "Preparing…", dimmed with the busy look, keeping focus, its ring and its own width, so Drop… and the footer beside it never move. It answers only what Stashes still shows: choosing another stash or page, the other Restore staged state, or closing Stashes before Git answers cancels the preparation (choosing the stash shown again changes nothing and reads nothing: whether it is still saved is checked again by Review restore… and Drop… themselves), so nothing opens or is said for the earlier choice and Review restore… can be pressed again at once. A refusal, such as a merge still paused, takes the place of the footer note beside Review restore…, announced once (one that another stash replaces before it shows is never said), and once a read finds the pause ended the note says so; a refusal never closes Stashes. Review restore…, pressed from the keyboard, keeps focus, whole with its ring, as the refusal appears: stacked beneath the list, the refusal, or the note that its pause has ended, keeps two lines, or one where the detail cannot also hold Review restore… whole (18 pt in a 461 × 490 window), its whole text in its tooltip and name, and the detail scrolls by the least that shows both, Review restore… first. A refusal or failure answering a press is also shown so while focus stays wherever the press left it: a click focuses no button, so focus stays where it was, such as on the saved files, and the answer would lie below the fold with nothing in view saying anything. Once the keyboard has moved focus onto another of the detail's controls while Git prepared, as on a large stash, the answer is heard rather than shown, as the end of its pause is, so the control the keyboard reached stays whole in view. The end of its pause, which no press asked for, scrolls only while Review restore… or nothing in the detail holds focus: with focus on another of its controls, such as the saved files, a file's text or a document's open control (which the keyboard scrolls into the detail's view with its ring, as it does each control of a document's or a model's own header: its open controls, a PDF's page arrows and page number, and a model's camera, views and clip controls, each shown whole in the detail where its header has scrolled it, so Tab onto any of them in a 461 × 490 window at 18 pt brings it into view; only the open controls once were), it leaves the detail where it is, heard rather than shown. Beside the list the note keeps two lines too. Any other answer, such as Git itself failing to prepare the restoration, whether its report runs to many lines or one, gives the note only the report's first line that says anything, kept to those lines, and Details… beside it opens the whole report, with Copy details, above Stashes; a report that says nothing gives "Git could not prepare this restoration." alone; the paused refusal, whose end replaces it, keeps its whole text in its tooltip and name. Drop is a separate confirmation that removes the saved entry without changing current working files.

Failed restoration should say whether conflicts were found and whether the reviewed stash is still saved, using fresh local evidence. If either check is unavailable, state that uncertainty. Direct conflict recovery to Changes and staging; keep Git's complete diagnostics in Details without suggesting a stash Continue/Abort action or automatically retrying.

Amend opens distinct Title and Description fields initialized from the current commit. Unedited messages retain their exact bytes; edited messages use the same conventional separator and formatting preservation as ordinary commits. Show the currently staged path count separately from all paths in the replacement commit and retain edited fields after failure. Locally known remote-tracking reachability prompts a history-rewrite warning. Undo last local commit uses a soft reset: index and working files stay intact and the undone commit's changes remain staged against its parent. Restrict it to the safe local-tip case supported by the backend. Revert and cherry-pick identify the selected commit; merge commits require an explicit mainline-parent choice. Their conflicts use the existing operation banner and resolution flow.

A recovery action refused while an integration is paused names the paused operation and the banner's two ways to end it: "A rebase is paused. Continue or abort it before another recovery action.", and likewise for a cherry-pick or a revert, with "Complete or abort it" for a merge, whose banner offers Complete merge; conflicts left with no operation to continue, as a conflicted stash restoration leaves them, say "Conflicts remain in this working copy. Resolve them before another recovery action." The core's plans and the window's own refusal share these words (`gitturtle_core::paused_recovery_refusal`). One sentence once refused for every pause alike ("Finish the current operation and resolve conflicts…"), which said neither what had paused nor which control ends it. Once a status read finds that pause ended, the muted line that replaces the refusal names the operation and the form's own button that tries again: "The merge has ended. Choose Review stash to continue.", with Review amend in the Amend form and Review restore… in Stashes; it once said "The paused operation has ended. Review again to continue." whatever had paused, naming no button. A refusal for conflicts alone stays, since an integration's end does not resolve them.

### Status strip

Use the bottom strip for local refresh state, loaded/visible history count, selection context, and concise keyboard hints. The hints name the page's keys; under a modal dialog or sheet, whose backdrop the page's keys do not reach, they name the keys of the dialog control holding focus where it has its own (the command palette's, Quick Open's and Keyboard Shortcuts' searches, the rebase sequence, and the GitHub panel's changed files, patch lines and outbound confirmation, `shortcuts::DialogKeys`), and otherwise the strip shows none. They name Escape by the dialog's own button it stands for, and a picker's inline help says Escape with that verb, so the strip, the footer and the dialog say one verb: Cancel in the command palette and Quick Open (Esc Cancel, Escape cancels), which leave without acting, Close in Keyboard Shortcuts, a read-only sheet, and the rebase dialog (Esc Close, Escape closes), and Back to repository in the GitHub panel (Esc Back to repository) (`DialogKeys::dismiss`, `DialogKeys::escape_help`). The strip gives way in the [one responsive order](#one-responsive-order), decided before layout from the interface font at the interface size (`GitTurtle::fit_status_bar`). First the local refresh warning, “Some changes need Refresh”, and its Refresh stay whole. Then a write's outcome, feedback in place, stays whole in the status line while it is fresh, for its 1.5 s; otherwise the status line keeps at least its ellipsis, so it is never cut, and in the room the warning and the hints leave it draws its whole parts, those “ · ” separates (“1 changed file”, “content read 12.3 ms”, “captured snapshot”), that fit: they leave whole, the last first, so a measured timing reads whole or leaves with the parts after it, and only a first part too long for the room ends in an ellipsis (`status_parts_end`). The line's parts are so the first to give way, then its first part to its ellipsis, and only then the hints below. At 18 pt in a 461 × 490 half tile Compare's whole hints, “Esc Back to history · ↑↓ Files”, keep their room, and “1 changed file · content read 12.3 ms” was cut mid-number; the line reads “1 changed file” there, and the whole line at 18 pt in 1,000 × 680. A part from user text, such as a path or a project's name, stays one part whatever it holds: a status joined from parts, as a cloned repository's “Repository cloned”, its project's name, its path and “open its repository tab or folder”, gives way only where they end (`views::StatusParts`), so a path holding “ · ” reads whole or leaves whole and a narrow line still reads “Repository cloned” whole; split at each separator, “Repository cloned · demo · /srv/clones/a · b · …” read “Repository cloned · demo · /srv/clones/a”, a whole path, and another one. The line draws only one line of its text, and the strip never draws a second (decided 2026-11-07, `views::first_status_line`): line breaks a status ends in are trimmed, so it reads whole, and those it begins with are skipped, so its first line with text is drawn; a line break inside its first part, as in a marked tab's reason with its folder on the line after it, Git's report, or a path that holds one, ends the line there in an ellipsis, so a path cut at its break never reads as another, whole path; in a status joined from known parts (`views::StatusParts`), one inside a later part ends the line with the part before it, as that part would give way, and a status whose parts are not known, as a read's error naming a repository at “/srv/a · b”, takes the break as falling in its first part, since a “ · ” in it can lie inside the path. Drawn whole, the marked tab's reason laid out as two lines centred in the 26 px strip, the first above its top edge, where the page painted over it. A line that drops a part or a line or ends in an ellipsis keeps its whole text as its accessible value and shows it whole in the app's tooltip on hover, after the shared tooltip delay, and a line drawn whole has none: the coordinator's decision of 2026-11-01, the rule of the dialogs' error lines, which cap what they draw and keep the whole report in the accessible value, so a person who reads the shortened line can still read all of it, and nothing drawn moves. The tooltip shows at most the text's first twelve lines within 1 KiB, as the error lines' does (`tags::error_tooltip`), since a read's error can carry Git's report; whether the line is shortened is decided as each frame lays it out, and the tooltip shows the latest frame's text and answer, so a status change or a resize during the hover's delay is answered for the line drawn last (`views::StatusLaidOut`). A tooltip already shown keeps its text until the pointer leaves the line, even if the status changes, as on macOS and as the error lines keep theirs through a resize, and the next hover shows the new text (the coordinator's decision of 2026-11-01). In a repository frame where a running write's status can come to the strip, where the widest busy label's barest form would have no room in a header with the widest contents at the window's width and text size ([Shared repository header](#shared-repository-header)), and only there, the line also keeps the room of the Cancel that status brings, whether or not a write runs: a write whose status the header has no room for takes the line's place, reading its progress in the accent, ending in an ellipsis where short, with Cancel as a 20 px icon after it, and nothing else in the strip moves. Where the header holds every write's status the hints and Details… keep that room. Then the hints keep their whole form, else their keys alone (“Ctrl+F · ↑↓ · Enter”), else fewer keys, each dropping the last but Escape in the order drawn, and otherwise leave (`shortcuts::KeyHints::giving_way`): the way out is the last hint to go, the coordinator's decision of 2026-11-01 under the owner's rule that the way out is never refused, so Keyboard Shortcuts gives way from “↑↓ · Esc” to “Esc”, Settings from “Tab · Esc” to “Esc” and the command palette from “↑↓ · Enter · Esc” to “↑↓ · Esc”, each once keeping the leading keys and losing Escape first, though its earlier description kept it longest; a dialog's spare hints, Keyboard Shortcuts' page and end keys, follow the whole form only where the status line keeps its whole text beside them (`shortcuts::KeyHints::spare`), so through a write whose status the header draws they stay, and they leave where a running write's status takes the line's place (they once left through every write, though a write in the header leaves the line its text: move nothing already drawn). Details… keeps its label unless the labelled warning would leave the status line, beside any room it keeps for Cancel, less than it keeps with every hint gone, where it becomes its icon, named and with its tooltip Details…. At 18 pt in a 461 × 490 half tile the whole hints took 277.5 px and cut “Up to date with origin · nothing new” to “Up to date with ori…”; with the watcher unable to start, as when the host's inotify instances run out, the warning pushed them past the window's edge, cut mid-glyph at 18 pt in windows up to 821 px wide; and there, beside the warning, Details… is its icon, no hint is drawn and a fresh outcome ends in an ellipsis, since the warning outranks it. Show elapsed timing only when measured. No permanent green badge should imply an unmeasured performance guarantee. Keep this strip visually quieter than all reading surfaces. The strip is window chrome, like the tab strip: under a modal it stays in place, dimmed and inert under the backdrop like the rest of the window, which assistive technology does not reach while the modal is open, and a modal panel's edge never crosses it. Nor does a menu: every dropdown and right-click menu keeps its drawn edge, the ring the kit paints just outside its fill, at least 8 px above the strip's top edge, and one opened at a low row or below a low button that needs more room than lies below moves up instead, a button's above its button and a row's wholly above its row, as it moves in from the window's sides (the kit's popup placement keeps the room `native_accessibility::set_overlay_room` sets; a right-click menu on History's lowest row at 13 pt in a 461 × 490 window ran 19.5 px over the strip, and then the kit's clamp moved it up over that row). A modal whose height is capped by the window ends its panel's lower edge at least 16 px above the strip's top edge at every interface text size, inside the window's client-decoration frame, and content that grows inside the panel takes its room from the panel's scrolling body rather than from the window; the custom theme editor follows this rule.

## Selection, focus, and feedback

- Selected row: semantic selected background, 2 px accent leading marker, primary text. Where the comparison's text shows its keyboard ring, in Compare or a Working preview, or the rendered Markdown or Blame's lines in its place show theirs, the inspector's selected row beside it, a changed file, a Working row or a File History revision, leaves its marker to the ring and keeps its selected fill, so one accent stroke marks where focus is and the selection stays in view; the marker returns when the ring leaves the text, and stays where the pane draws no ring though its editor keeps focus, as over an error or an empty patch (`GitTurtle::shown_text`, `GitTurtle::text_ring_shows`, `GitTurtle::selected_row_marker`). (Decided 2026-11-01: at 13 pt in a 461 px window the ring's right edge and the row's marker, a few pixels apart across the inspector's edge, read as one doubled stroke; focus is the one thing the accent outline marks, and the fill alone keeps the selection.) Hover: semantic hover background; selected rows retain their tint with a subtle accent blend. Selected controls preserve their surface with gentle hover feedback. A selected node has a contrasting ring. Selection remains visible after focus moves to another pane.
- Keyboard focus: a focused Button draws a 2 px ring of full accent 1 px outside its edge, 3:1 against the surface beneath in every state of the shared helper. A Button that fades itself fades the ring too: a loading Button fades to 0.8. A focused text field, checkbox or Switch draws the same ring outside its edge (a field's own 1 px edge takes the accent too, as a bordered Button's does), 3:1 against the canvas, the panel and `subtle` in every built-in palette with More contrast off and on, the ring the palette's accent (`appearance::tests::every_palette_draws_kit_focus_rings_at_three_to_one`); fields and checkboxes drew the toolkit's ring, 3 px of accent at half opacity, which read under 3:1 in 16 of the 20 palettes (Porcelain 2.27:1 on its canvas). Other controls and panes use a visible 1 px accent outline or inset outline. History's commit list, the changed-file list, Working Changes' list, the navigator, the project pane, the commit message's text, Blame's lines, File history's revisions and the rendered Markdown, in Split the active side's blocks below its heading, or the whole side where its heading leaves the blocks no room, draw theirs on their own 1 px edge (`FocusRing::edge`), whole on all four sides with no divider painted over it, History's around the part of the list its viewport shows, so where the columns scroll sideways, as narrow History's do, no side lies outside the window: a key press shows it on the list holding focus, and it stays while the pointer moves until a pointer press, after which a click-focused list stays unmarked until the next key (`focus_ring::FocusRing`). Every pane that draws its own ring asks the same ring rather than GPUI's `focus_visible`, which a pointer move turns off: the discard and forced worktree removal details, the rewrite review's series, the GitHub panel's file list, patch and conversations, the Settings theme cards, and Compare's text, the GitHub panel's Source and the rewritten-series review's inspected file preview, whose read-only editors draw no edge of their own: the pane, or in Split the focused side's text below its heading, and in the review's Review messages the focused message's text below its heading, takes a 1 px accent outline on its own edge, laid over the lines so none moves (`FocusRing::edge`; the review's preview turns its own 1 px border to the accent); a pane that keys scroll or move is a Tab stop. The GitHub panel's outbound confirmation is one such stop: a region that scrolls its destination, with every full identity it names, and the text Send sends, a group named for what it confirms, which takes the outline on the edge of the ring's room around it, inside the confirmation's resting border, and scrolls by Up, Down, Page Up, Page Down, Home and End. It is as tall as what it holds, up to the room the body leaves beside Send and Keep editing, which stay below it in view where the body can hold them, and never less than two lines, and the guidance sentence above Send moves into it after the destination where it would leave the region less. Where the body cannot hold them, the region keeps its two lines and shows from its ring's top, and the buttons are reached by scrolling or by Tab: at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 half tile the ring, the two lines and the buttons take 156 px and the reveal keeps 20 px at either edge, so a failure of three or more lines on the top line leaves too little.
