Where the guidance stands and how tall the region may be are decided before layout in the frame being drawn, from the panel's width and the interface font, the guidance's lines wrapped at that width, Send and Keep editing on one row or two by their measured labels, as the compact chrome's form is chosen below, and, while a conversation is open, the height of the top line above the body from what it says and its busy row's label, which wraps beside a whole Cancel, the row as tall as the taller of its lines and the Button, and what the last frame found the body short of its room, so no frame, a new panel's first confirmation or the first after a resize that rewraps the guidance or the buttons, draws the guidance where the next frame moves it from, and the body's reveal of the focused region is decided from that frame's own layout, before its content prepaints, so the first frame and each step of a live drag draw Send and Keep editing whole in the body's view wherever the body can hold them, while a resize or text-size change leaves a body the person wheeled away from the confirmation where it is (decided 2026-11-09: revealed after paint, they were cut or outside the view for a frame, a short comment whose body's foot fell inside the Buttons' 28 px band drawing them cut; decided 2026-11-07: from the last frame's layout, a new panel's first long confirmation at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 half tile drew its guidance below the region for one frame, and a long reply's, the window made only narrower, 1,480 to 461 px at 13 pt, laid its region out 612 px tall for one frame where it settles at 573, sized for the guidance's fewer, wider lines; decided 2026-11-08: from the height the last frame drew the top line, a notice on it rewrapped by that resize laid a reply's region out 39 px too tall, then 39 px too short). A change of the top line's height while the region holds focus, above the body or among the content, asks for the region's reveal in that frame, and the body moves in the next; the reveal covers the focused region and not a focused Send or Keep editing, and it does not undo manual scrolling among the content (decided 2026-11-08: with no conversation open, the busy row Refresh PRs drew among the content pushed Send past the body's foot, 63 px with the row wrapped, and nothing revealed it). Each edge its text continues past fades into the dialog's surface, the scroll cue's edge fade, at any length and with no scrollbar, inside the ring's room, so no fade covers the ring: it is not a list, so it is the one exception to the scrollbar thumb rule below, and with no gutter its text keeps its width from its first frame (decided 2026-11-07: cut at its foot with no cue, nothing said the guidance and the text Send sends lay below; shown whole as the stop, the confirmation stood 446 px tall at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 half tile, where the body is about 265 px, and no reveal could draw its ring whole). A list that draws its designed empty state, as a clean Working Changes does, stays a Tab stop and draws its ring there, so the Tab order stays the same whatever the list holds and a screen reader hears the empty state where the list is (decided 2026-11-07). Every page and dialog keeps focus visible at every Tab stop, at 13 and 18 pt: Tab never leaves focus on nothing, on the window's root or on an element the frame does not draw, and the stop it lands on draws its ring whole on all four sides, inside every clip around it, while the pointer moves (`focus_ring::sweep`, which names each stop still awaiting its fix). Focus the keyboard placed in a scrolling area stays in view as the area changes size: a resize or text-size change that cuts the focused control the area showed whole scrolls it whole again in the next frame, whether or not the pointer has moved since, as it does to drag the window's edge (shrunk from 461 × 640 to 461 × 490 at 18 pt, Stashes once left Review restore… cut at its detail's foot), and through every step of a drag: each resize that cuts it reveals it again, even one laid out before the frame that shows the last step's reveal, which once counted the control as cut and left it cut for the rest of the drag, while a control the person scrolled out of view stays out of view and a scroll of an area around it moves an inner area whole, revealing nothing in it (`focus_reveal`). A control taller than the area's view, which the keyboard reveals by its top edge, counts as shown while that edge is in view with the focus ring's room on either side of it: a resize after which it would fit whole but is cut scrolls it whole, one that cuts its top edge scrolls that edge back into view, and any other scrolls nothing; it once never counted as shown, so a resize that made room for it left it cut. Projects opens with focus on its selected Open, Clone or Create tab, its ring drawn in the first frame. Focus stays on Projects or Settings while a read of the working state lands behind it, such as a Refresh a review held back or the read after a write that ends there; it once moved to the hidden Changes list, and Back returns it to the list. A passive read never moves focus: a quiet refresh that finds the selected working file conflicted opens its conflict view without taking focus, which stays on the list's row, or in that row's open keyboard menu, which stays open; the view's result draft takes focus only when the person asks for it, by choosing the file, Edit result, a block step or Restore saved draft. It once took focus as the view opened, out of the list, closing the menu. Settings opens with focus on Follow system appearance, or, while the desktop's Omarchy theme makes that switch unavailable, on the selected theme's card, its ring drawn and the page scrolled to show it in the first frames whether a key or a click opened it, and every time it opens, as at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 half tile, where the switch lies below the page's fold; it opened with focus on the window's root, which showed none, and once, reopened by its shortcut after Back onto the same card, at its top with the Omarchy card below the fold. The GitHub panel, opened or reopened by a click on the header's Pull requests as by a key, draws the ring of the focus it lands on until the next pointer press: reopened by a click, it gave Files' patch back with no ring. Whatever a reopen or an opening confirmation focuses is whole in the first frame it paints, its reveal decided from that frame's layout before the body's content prepaints: the held control, a PR row among them, and the section's first stop a reopen falls to (decided 2026-11-09: a row held as the panel closed, its title cut by the body's foot, painted cut until a later frame's reveal landed). A reveal among the content runs only where the region or its actions were in the body's view in the last frame, and none undoes manual scrolling. Back to the repository returns focus to the control it left with its ring drawn, whether a key or “Back to repository” pressed with the pointer went back; where the window's root held focus as the page was left, after a click on an empty spot of the window, which focuses the root, focus goes to the shown mode's own list instead, as it does where File history, Blame or a dialog closes after such a click: it went back to the root, which shows no focus, and Down moved nothing. File history or a revision inspection opened from a row's menu, by a right-click or Shift-F10, gives focus back as it closes to the list the menu returns focus to, never to the menu, which has gone with its item (closed by Escape, File history once left focus on the gone menu, where no key, Escape and the command palette's shortcut among them, reached anything until a click; the window's root, which a click on an empty spot focuses, takes the window's shortcuts as every control does). A dialog opens with focus on its first field, whose ring the first frame draws whole, at 13 pt in a full window and 18 pt in a 461 × 490 half tile: the Reflog's filter, the worktree manager's filter (in Create, its branch field) and Tags' filter, where the worktree manager and Tags once opened with focus on the dialog's shell, which draws nothing. Where the field lies below the fold of a scrolling body, as the Reflog's filter does below its explanation at 18 pt in the half tile, the body scrolls by the least amount that shows the field and its ring, as the dialog opens and as a read that adds a line above it lands, and not at all where the field and its ring are whole already, as the GitHub panel reopening onto its destination field at 18 pt in the half tile once scrolled that field to the body's top, its banner out of view. That panel, reopening onto its destination field with no PR open, shows its body from the top whatever offset it closed at, since the field is its first part: closed scrolled to its end at 13 pt in the half tile, it reopened 93 px down, the field just inside the body's edge and the banner above it hidden, because the field alone was whole; reopening onto a Button that held focus as it closed, such as Refresh PRs or Connect, keeps the offset it closed at, as an open PR does, beside what reopening focuses; switching the worktree manager between Manage and Create moves focus held by one form's first field to the other's. A reply that takes away the control holding focus does not strand it either: when a read ends while its loading row's Cancel holds focus, in the Reflog, the worktree manager or the GitHub panel, focus moves first to the dialog's first field below the row (the Reflog's filter, the manager's filter or, in Create, its branch field, the panel's shown section), or, for a Reflog entry's read, whose row stands in the details below the entry list, to that entry, the list's stop above it, and for its recovery review's, whose row stands below Review recovery branch…, to that button, which draws its ring in the frame that drops the row, and Escape still closes the dialog (`focus_ring::LoadingCancel`). Nor does a read that takes controls away as it starts: the Reflog's Read log takes away the entries and the selected entry's details until the log lands, so pressed with the pointer, which moves no focus, while the keyboard is on an entry or on those details, it moves focus to the filter; focus stayed on an entry no frame drew, where keys and Escape reached nothing. The navigation's divider is the sidebar's own right edge, and the project pane's divider is the pane's own right edge, drawn there rather than over the page's first pixel, where the navigator draws its ring's left side; and Working Changes' list stands 1 px in from the inspector's divider, as the commit inspector's list stands inside that inspector's own edge. All controls require accessible labels; color or tooltip is insufficient as the only label.
- A text field draws its caret in the frame that gives it focus, by a key, a click or a repository tab shown again with the field left focused, and first blinks it off one blink interval later, as macOS shows a focused field's insertion point at once; a key, an edit, a caret move or a selection, typed, by a bound action such as undo or paste, by the pointer or by an input method, shows the caret at once and holds it shown one whole blink interval after the last of them, a full visible phase before it first blinks off, as GTK and AppKit hold an insertion point that has just moved, and a field given text while unfocused stays still, drawing nothing until it is focused; a field starts blinking only once a drawn frame has given it focus, so one the app focuses and leaves again before any frame stays still too, while a field that holds focus and is emptied, by its clear button or the project hub's clear, shows its caret at once. The kit toggled the caret as focus was reported, after that frame, so a tab left in a field drew a second frame that only showed or hid its caret, and a field focused by the app hid its caret at once; with the caret kept shown for the next focus, a warm tab's return, which gives each of its fields its text, once set its unfocused search field blinking, redrawing the window twice a second while idle, and a field the app focused started blinking as focus was asked for, so one left before a frame, never blurred, blinked and redrew the window until it was next focused and left; the kit's pause after a key or a move lasted 300 ms, so a caret typed or moved hid again before it had stood shown for one phase, and it paused for an edit only a caret already shown and for no selection or composition, so undo or paste from a caret hidden in its blink, or Shift with an arrow, drew no caret until the blink came round (decided 2026-11-02 under one frame per input, the whole-phase hold 2026-11-06; `vendor/gpui-base`, `BlinkCursor::start`, `BlinkCursor::pause` and `InputState::focus`).
- File status uses distinct icons and explicit words. Small status words use primary text on normal, selected, and hovered rows; semantic color remains on the icons so text keeps at least 4.5:1 contrast in every theme.
- Input response is immediate. In History, commit selection updates the right heading before changed-file work starts. A file action enters Compare immediately and sets the path heading before preview work starts. Load uncached metadata, changed-file lists, diffs, highlighting, and images in separate stages. Returning to History uses retained state and must not wait on an active preview request.
- Copy feedback: every explicit copy action confirms itself where it happened. For 1.5 s the activated copy button reads **Copied** after a check icon, or, icon-only, shows the check with the tooltip "Copied", which the click leaves up while the pointer stays on the button: a tooltip already shown turns to Copied, and one still waiting out its delay shows Copied when it appears (the kit hid a tooltip on mouse down until the pointer left and came back); then it reverts, and a repeated click restarts the time. One button reads Copied at a time, and Copied belongs to what it copied rather than to its place: another commit selected within the 1.5 s shows its own Hash and Copy message at rest, as another file or side shows its copy actions, while returning to the copied commit, path or side within the time shows Copied again. The button keeps its bounds and its accessible name in both states: a labelled copy button is always as wide as the wider of its own content and the copied content, and both start at its leading inset, so the check takes the icon's place and the narrower content's spare width trails it rather than opening a gap before the label. Every copy button leads with an icon for what it copies, so **Copied** starts where the label did; a label alone gained a check where no icon was and moved 22 px. A copy of a hash, a commit's or an object's ID, leads with the hash glyph (`hash.svg`, a #): the inspector's Hash, Blame's commit hashes, the Reflog's Copy commit ID and Tags' Copy object ID. Every other copy, Copy message and the text actions (Copy details, Copy saved text, Copy exact text, the GitHub panel's copy actions), leads with the copy icon. Coordinator's decision, 2026-10-30, one glyph per meaning: Hash drew Copy message's copy icon beside it, so the two read as the same action twice. Hash's content, the narrower, leaves that spare width at rest. Assistive technology hears one polite "Copied ‹what›" naming the content, such as "Copied commit hash", a fresh announcement for each copy. A focused list's Copy (a History, changed-file, working or revision row, or a Blame line) is announced with no button to mark. Copying selected text with the edit command, in an editor or the commit message, stays silent, as native text fields are. Nothing animates, so reduced motion needs nothing more. Every copy goes through `copy_feedback`, which other actions that confirm in place share, such as Open in default app and System preview reading **Opened** (see Documents, media and exact source); at most one control reads Copied or Opened at a time.
- Git feedback in place: a Git action confirms on the control or row the person touched, as Copied does, once Git reports success; the status bar keeps the record, not the confirmation. A working file's Stage confirms on that file's row where it arrives, its control in Staged showing the check with the tooltip **Staged** (Unstage, **Unstaged**, on its row in Unstaged); Stage all and Unstage all read **Staged** and **Unstaged** after the check in their label's place, or as icons show the check with that tooltip; Fetch, Pull and Push read **Fetched**, **Pulled** and **Pushed**; Targets' Create and Switch read **Created** and **Switched**; and a switch chosen from the branch picker's menu trades the picker's branch icon for the check, its tooltip saying Switched, once the picker names that branch, its label and width unchanged, as a tracking branch created with **Switch to the new branch** does once the picker names it, its tooltip saying **Created** ("Created · feature"). Each lasts 1.5 s in the control's own bounds with its accessible name unchanged, in its resting label colour even where the write leaves it unavailable, as Stage all is once its group is empty: it stays inert and takes the disabled colour when the confirmation ends, because the disabled colour (3.19:1 on Midnight's heading) read the outcome as unavailable. Each is announced once, politely, with the outcome ("Fetched origin", "Staged notes.txt"), and is recorded in the status line, while the window-wide notice stays down so nothing is announced twice. The status line keeps a write's outcome for those 1.5 s, a Commit's too, over what the reads after the write report, then reads what they reported, such as Changes' working summary: the refresh that follows a Stage once replaced "Staged notes.txt" with "3 working changes · 2 staged" while the row still read Staged. Where the re-sorted list leaves the heading whose Stage all or Unstage all confirms outside its view, as Stage all's own heading went below the foot of a long list once every file joined Staged above it, the list moves in one frame the least that shows that heading whole, so the confirmation is seen on the control that was pressed; it moves only so far as the selected file stays in view, and where the two cannot both be seen the selected file keeps its place while the outcome is still announced and in the status line. Fetch, Pull and Push keep the widths their icon and label give them, because those widths decide where the action bar wraps and where the compact chrome begins: labelled, the past tense alone takes the icon's and label's room, being narrower than both, and in the compact chrome each, an icon, takes the check and says its word in its tooltip. A Fetch or Pull that brought nothing new, which Git core decides from the refs before and after it ran, still reads Fetched or Pulled, so no word is wider than its control, while its announcement and the status line say what Git found: "Up to date with origin · nothing new" for a Fetch, which compares the whole remote, and "Up to date with origin/main · nothing new" for a Pull, naming the remote and branch it pulled. A failure or an uncertain result never reads as success and keeps the operation error. A tracking branch created without the switch leaves the picker naming the branch it named, so no control confirms it: its outcome ("Created feature · tracks origin/feature") is announced once and recorded in the status line, with the window-wide notice down. A quiet refresh, a write asked for elsewhere (the command palette, a review dialog's confirm other than a tracking branch's creation, a multiselection's Stage) and one that finishes for another tab confirm nothing on any control and keep the window-wide notice. One control reads a confirmation at a time, Copied and Opened included.
- Refusals in place: a refusal never replaces the content a person is reading. It says why where the person acted, in the bar, caption or control it came from, as an alert announced at once and again at each refusal, and the window's error, which takes the content area's place, is kept for failures that leave nothing to show. The tab strip's refusals, a ninth tab ("Switch to an open tab, or close one to open another; its commit drafts remain saved."), a switch past the 512 MiB retained allowance, closing a tab while its write runs, a group needing more tabs than remain and a pin past the full local library, read in a line beneath the strip, in the removed colour on its background with Dismiss beside it, until dismissed or a tab opens, closes or is shown (`GitTurtle::refuse_in_tab_strip`); a repository the Local workspaces dialog adds past the full library, by Create workspace or a group's Add current, reads in that dialog's own error line until an addition succeeds, and the dialog lists nothing as added; a ninth tab asked for from the Projects page reads in that page's feedback line, in the same words, and ends the Open the page waited for, so its actions are available again (`ProjectHub::refuse_open`), and closing a tab ends it, its cause gone as the strip's is; asked for from a recent row, the page moves only so far as that row stays in view, and where the row and the line cannot both be seen the page keeps its place, the row where it was, and the refusal is announced politely, while the line is out of view the refused project's row, wherever a search or a new recent list puts it and on no row while it is not listed, reads it in its location line in the removed colour kept readable on the row's own fills (`Palette::small_label`), in its own bounds, the whole text and the location it replaces in the row's tooltip, and the refusal in its accessible name. At the limit no folder picker opens, so nobody chooses a folder only to be told no tab can hold it: the Open and New Tab shortcuts, the menu, the command palette, the strip's + and the rail's folder refuse at once in the strip's line (`GitTurtle::refuse_picker_at_tab_limit`), and the Projects page's **Choose a repository…** in that page's feedback line (decided 2026-11-08). A hunk's or line's Stage or Unstage pressed while a write runs in another tab starts nothing, shows the busy look and keeps its controls available for the next press. (Decided 2026-11-05: set as the window's error, the close refused during a write and the eight-tab limit replaced the diff being read with "Preview unavailable", and a ninth tab from Projects left the page busy with nothing to say why.)
- A write looks busy only once the window has been busy a moment. Its busy look, the progress label and Cancel in the header or, where the header has no room for them, the status bar, the tab's "· working", the Changes header's "Refreshing…", the commit action reading "Working…" and the disabled look of every control the write and the reads after it make unavailable, waits 200 ms from the start of the window's busy spell, which lasts while a write runs, a status is read or Working Changes' rows are prepared (`workspace::BUSY_LOOK_DELAY`). A write pressed while the window is at rest starts the spell with its press, and the spell runs through the write's status read to its rows' landing: a write that lands sooner shows none of it, and a slower one shows it all when the 200 ms pass. A write pressed while the window is already busy, as while the rows of the filter's query or of a newer status are prepared, joins that spell, which began by the time those rows began to be prepared, so its look shows 200 ms after the spell began: sooner after its press, or at once where the spell already looks busy. Acceptance does not wait: those controls refuse a second write, or a click while the rows are prepared, at once, as they did when they were drawn disabled, and assistive technology hears them unavailable at once, so only their look waits; a menu opened during the delay, such as Changes' Actions, offers none of the items the write refuses. Through the delay those controls were announced enabled and the menu listed items that each refused. The look waits wherever it shows: History's Latest and View commit, Targets' fields (read-only at once), the branch picker and the Git profile picker (whose menus, opened in the delay, offer none of the items the write refuses and are built again in place once the write has ended and the quiet reload or status read it asks for has landed, as Changes' and a commit's Actions menus are, and the right-click menus of a navigator branch or worktree, a History commit, a working row and a project row, a project row's actions button and the menus Shift-F10 or the Menu key opens on those rows, which once read the write as they opened and kept their items so, the actions button's until it was opened again; built again as the write ended, before its reload, a menu offered items for the state the write had just changed, as a commit's Revert of a commit an Undo had taken away, or a working row's Delete untracked file… for a file a Stage had just added, as it did too when Refresh read the status again in place of the read the Stage had asked for, before either landed, while a quiet read the watcher asks for, as a file saved outside the app brings, refuses nothing, nor does a reload that waits for the window to return from Settings or to close Blame, File history or a revision inspection, which once left every such menu refused there with nothing to say why, while one that waits for the read a commit or row selection starts still refuses, which once let a commit's menu opened with Down, Up and Shift-F10 as an Undo's reload ran offer Revert of the commit the Undo had taken away; a project row's Open project waits for the write alone, as opening another project reads nothing the reload brings), diverged branches' Merge… and Rebase…, a paused merge or rebase's Continue, Abort… and Keep files…, History search's Continue and Restart, Compare's review options and change arrows, the Download LFS actions, the status bar's Refresh, the Settings identity's fields (read-only at once) and actions, with Save reading "Working…" only then, the Projects page's progress strip and project rows (announced unavailable at once, a click moving nothing), and the Activity, Worktrees and Reflog dialogs' actions too, as Tags' Create tag… and rows, Create tag's Review tag (announced unavailable by a name that says why in the busy spell's own reason, "Review tag, unavailable. Wait for the current Git operation to finish", and dimmed with that reason as its tooltip, "Wait for the current Git operation to finish", both naming a GitHub request's work where one runs, as every reason does below; they once named the current Git operation whatever ran), a tag's Delete local tag… and Push to…, the Stashes dialog's Drop… and Review restore…, with Review restore… reading "Preparing…" only then while its own preparation runs, and the rebase dialogs' Load commits, Review rebase…, Review last rewritten series… and Review Continue… (each pressed in the delay changes nothing, where Load commits once set the form's error and Review last rewritten series… closed the dialog first). Worktrees and the Reflog close before their own review submits, so a write they show is never their own; they once dimmed at once on the reasoning that it was. Tags, Stashes and the rebase dialogs, open as a write started, once refused those actions silently, announced and drawn available. Review tag, Create tag's own confirming action, stayed available through any write, and a press set the form's error ("The repository changed or another operation is running"). Its dialog draws its own footer for it, where the kit's footer draws its confirming action available through any write; the other dialogs whose confirming action is still the kit's, which the [writes contract](crates/app/docs/writes-and-persistence.md) lists, still look available through a write and answer a press with their form's error, and are to follow Review tag. A quick Stage once dimmed every row's control, Actions, Stage all and Commit for a frame and flashed "Refreshing…" after it, and later still drew the branch and profile pickers grey and Commit reading "Working…" in one frame. Switching pages is navigation, not a write: History, Working Changes and Settings open while a write runs, from their shortcuts, the header's toggles, the command palette and the menus alike, and Changes then shows the write's busy look on its own write controls and its rows as last read, which are read again as the write ends, whichever tab the write ran in (opened in a second tab during a write in the first, Changes once queued a read behind the write at each switch until the next was refused as busy and its list emptied); Primary + 2 and a click on "Changes · 1" once left History in place during a write while the toggle looked and was announced available, and the palette refused all three. A change a write or a review's preparation held back, such as a commit made in a terminal while a Fetch ran in another tab, shows as it ends, in History and Changes alike; the shown tab once kept what it last read until something else read it. A plan History asks for opens over History only: Changes opened while a recovery, rebase or branch action is still being prepared drops it, as Projects and Settings do, where Amend last commit's form once opened over the working list. Back in History before the preparation ends, the plan opens there, where it once stayed dropped and the action did nothing. A Refresh asked for while a review is prepared runs as the review ends, or as Settings, Projects or the form's Cancel cancels it, which once left it unrun; switching or closing the tab drops it, and the tab reads quietly when shown again, and History's Refresh, which reads the working state again with the history, drops it too. History's Refresh also drops a review History is still preparing (Tags, a recovery plan, a branch's actions or Edit local commits), as a switch drops one: nothing it brings opens, and the window stays busy under its label until its read ends, which then reads once. Refresh once cancelled such a plan without waiting, so each press during a slow read queued a status read behind it, until one was refused as busy and Changes lost its rows. Projects waits for the write, since each of its actions starts another operation: the header's Projects and History's Back to Projects are refused at once and, once dimmed, add why to their tooltips ("Open Projects · Wait for the current Git operation to finish · Primary + Shift + O", Projects ending with its shortcut as the History and Changes tabs end with theirs; it once named none), as the palette and the Linux Main Menu give that reason on its row. The reason follows the control's name and comes before its shortcut, which ends every tooltip that names one: "Back to Projects · Wait for the current Git operation to finish · Primary + [", which once read with the reason after the shortcut. A request of the GitHub panel that runs as the busy spell names itself instead, in the operation bar by its kind ("Connecting your GitHub account…", "Sending the captured review…", "Comparing the PR's changes locally…") and in every reason by what it waits for (`github_view::RequestKind::reason`): a request to GitHub "Wait for the GitHub request to finish"; Disconnect, which removes GitTurtle's stored authorization, "Wait for GitTurtle to disconnect from GitHub"; a PR's Changes, compared in the local repository, "Wait for the pull request comparison to finish"; and a new PR's templates, read from its base in the local repository, "Wait for the pull request templates to load". Connect once read "Loading your requested PR context…" and the reasons named "the current Git operation". The owner chose a true reason for each kind over one reason for all of them: "Wait for the GitHub request to finish" was said while the panel only compared, read templates or removed its own authorization, sending nothing to GitHub, and a reason that names work which is not running sends the person looking for it; a reason true of every kind ("Wait for the pull request work to finish") says less about what to wait for. Requests to GitHub keep naming GitHub, which is what they wait on. A review whose preparation a tab switch dropped keeps the window busy until its read ends, since that read still runs where every tab's reads run; in any tab but the one that asked for it, its busy label and reason say what runs and where, “Finishing another tab's review…” and “Wait for another tab's review to finish”, or “a closed tab's review” once that tab has closed, while the tab that asked keeps the review's own label (“Reviewing file changes…”). The next tab once gave the review's label as the reason its own actions were unavailable, naming work it never asked for, against the rule that a control says why it is unavailable. A tab that opens the closed tab's repository again also says “a closed tab's review”: the window forgets which tab asked as that tab closes, since the new tab never asked, though it once took the review's own label from the repository it shares. The labels are fixed strings, as every busy label is, so they name the tab's kind rather than the project; they run no longer than a GitHub request's label, and like any busy label they go only where they move nothing, the header's spacer or the status bar, so nothing moves as they show. While the only thing the window waits for is such a review's read, under any of those labels or the review's own, Projects stays available everywhere it is offered, the header's Projects, History's Back to Projects, File ▸ Projects…, the palette and the Linux Main Menu, as its shortcut always was, and opening it ends the wait (`GitTurtle::projects_refusal`). This was decided by the owner's rules that a control says why it is unavailable and that the way out is never refused: Projects, with Settings, is the way out of a read that never ends, and through the wait those places once looked and were announced unavailable, giving the wait's reason in the tooltip, while the shortcut opened Projects all along, so the reason given was not why. The commands the wait still refuses give its reason in the palette and the Linux Main Menu, and a write in progress still refuses Projects everywhere with the write's reason. A review still being prepared, not yet dropped, leaves Projects available in all those places too, and opening it ends the review as its shortcut does (`GitTurtle::projects_ends_busy`). (Decided 2026-11-01 by the coordinator: the button and its shortcut do the same thing; the header's Projects and History's Back to Projects once dimmed with the review's label as their reason while Projects' shortcut opened it and cancelled the review.) Behind a read the window no longer waits for, a status read asked for again, by Refresh or any other route, takes over the one already waiting rather than queue another, so each tab keeps one queued read at most; Projects, Back and Refresh pressed again and again during a read that never ends once queued one at each Refresh until the next was refused as busy and Changes lost its rows. A tooltip already shown follows the look under a resting pointer, gaining the reason as the button dims and losing it as the write ends; the kit kept the words it had when the pointer entered, so a pointer resting on Projects through a write read "Open Projects" beside the dimmed button, and the reason after it.
- Selecting is not choosing. Arrowing through a list selects, it does not activate, so focus stays in the list and the next arrow moves on through it, as in macOS lists; Enter, a click on the row and a view's own controls activate. A click or Enter that Working Changes' list refuses, while a write or its preparation runs or while the list's rows are prepared, chooses nothing: the selection and focus stay where they are, and the busy look, or the filter's error in the list, says why: where the press comes before the busy look's 200 ms delay has passed, as a write or a review's preparation starts or while a quiet refresh's rows are prepared, the refused press shows the look at once, a click, Enter, an arrow or Select All alike, and so does a press on Stage all, Unstage all, the selection's Stage and Unstage or Commit, which then dim and say why in the same frame (`GitTurtle::show_busy_look`, `refuse_busy_press`; decided 2026-11-01: a press on one of those controls in the delay was refused with nothing to show for it, unlike a press on the list). (Decided 2026-11-01 by the coordinator: the refusal and the look that says why come in the same frame; the delay keeps a quick write from dimming controls the person did not touch, and a refused press touched one. A press refused in the delay once changed nothing and showed nothing.) A press refused only for the filter's error, which the list states, shows none, even while a status read runs: the read refuses no press, and showing its look then dimmed what the person had not touched. An arrow, Enter or Select All on a list that draws no file, clean or matching no filter, has nothing to act on and refuses nothing, so it shows none either, where an arrow, and later Enter, once showed it at once during a write; on rows kept drawn while new ones are prepared it is refused and shows the look (`GitTurtle::working_files_drawn`). A refused arrow or Shift-arrow scrolls nothing either, so the list stays where the person left it, with the selection: it once scrolled to the row it would have selected while the selection stayed behind, out of view, and a refused Shift-arrow showed no look. While only the list's rows being prepared refuse them, with no write running, the controls that act on the files listed, Stage all and Unstage all, the selection's Stage and Unstage, and Commit, say why in place once they dim with the busy look: their accessible names, "Stage all, unavailable. Wait for the file list to refresh" for a newer status's rows and "Wait for the filter to finish" for the filter's query or grouping, the two causes a row's menu names "Refreshing…" and "Filtering…", and their tooltips, which add the reason after what they say ("Include all local changes in the next commit · Wait for the file list to refresh"), or give it alone where they say nothing at rest (`GitTurtle::rows_wait`). A control refused for a reason of its own that the rows' landing would not end, as Commit without a title, states no wait. (Decided 2026-11-01: a refused press during a quiet refresh's preparation showed only those controls dimmed, with nothing to say why. The header's "Refreshing…" follows a status read alone, and a line there would move the header on every passive refresh, so the reason goes where nothing moves, in the names and tooltips; a write gives none here, as its progress label and Cancel say why. Decided 2026-11-01 too, keeping focus where the person put it and one frame per input: the names wait for the look as the tooltips do. The local watcher's quiet refreshes prepare rows often and briefly, and a name given at once renamed a focused Commit "Commit 2 files, unavailable. …" and back on each one; a refresh shorter than the delay now changes no word a screen reader reads, and a press the list or one of them refuses shows the look, and the names with it, at once.) A refused click once still chose the selected row, so its conflict's draft took focus from the list. Command-click (Ctrl+click on Linux, as Select All is Ctrl+A there) and Shift-click edit the multiselection and only select, as the arrows do, leaving focus in the list: they once chose the file as a plain click does, its draft taking focus from the list being selected in, and Linux read Super for Command. A file chosen before its preview is read takes focus into its draft as the preview lands, once, and only while focus is still on the list: moved on meanwhile, to another row, the filter field or the row's menu, the person keeps focus where they put it, which the landing once took. Enter on a control inside the list, such as Clear filter or the selected row's Stage that Tab reaches from the list (the list's Tab order, below), is that control's, as Space is, and chooses no file: the list's Enter once took it from every control inside the list, so Enter on Clear filter did nothing and on a row's Stage focused the selected conflict's draft. A conflicted working file arrowed onto opens its conflict view with focus left on the list, and its result draft takes focus only as Enter or a click chooses the file: the arrow's selection once focused the draft, so the next Down moved the draft's caret instead of the selection. A passive read never moves focus: a quiet refresh, a write's status read after a Merge or Pull that stops on the selected file, or Refresh, opens its conflict view and leaves focus, and a row's open keyboard menu, where they are. Returning to the repository from Settings or Projects activates nothing either: a conflict that landed meanwhile opens with focus returning where it was, not passing through the draft for a frame.
- Never display an old commit's diff under a new commit's header. On selection, clear or explicitly mark the pending preview; late results for previous selections cannot replace the current one. Where clearing would move what is drawn, mark it: selecting another Reflog entry keeps the last entry's commit, message and changed files in place, faded to a loading Button's 0.8 behind the read's loading row, which stands over their first line, until the new entry's swap in within one frame; nothing acts on them meanwhile, and Cancel or a failed read clears them. Kept details are drawn and nothing more: no assistive technology reaches them and Tab passes over the kept message, so the last entry's commit is never read under the new entry's heading as its own; the read's loading row is what is reached in their place. Cleared at once, the details shrank to nothing for the read, and the list above them and everything below moved twice.
- Loading: a small local progress label while background work remains pending. Skeletons, if used, occupy the exact content area and remain static. Avoid whole-window loading overlays and decorative shimmer. Keep navigation interactive and cancel obsolete requests.
- Empty and edge states are designed screens: a list or pane with nothing to show, or a repository at an edge, says so in one deliberate line where the user looks, with its next action beside it as a real control where the product has one (`empty_state::EmptyState`). The line is the 12 px muted secondary text and wraps inside its room, never cut: in a pane it is centred, at most 480 px wide, 12 px in from the pane's sides and 4 px from its top and bottom, with the action 8 px below, and a state taller than its pane, where its host scrolls it, as the rewritten-series review's preview box does for a five-line failure at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window, scrolls inside the pane with the shared scroll cue rather than being cut, and while it overflows is a Tab stop that Up and Down scroll, ringing the pane's own 1 px edge; in a list it starts at the list's text inset, as wide as the list, with the action 8 px below; in the action bar it takes the counts' place. The action is the shared 28 px control on `subtle` with its icon and label, and it only opens an existing form or moves focus to an existing control: it never writes or contacts a server itself. Loading and failures keep their title and explanation, except in a column too narrow for them beside its controls: there, as History's search does where its controls keep only their icons (below), the state is one line, its title, and the explanation is its tooltip and accessible description, since in a half tile the wrapped explanation ran past the list. A line in a pane as short as Changes' list at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window (two lines) says only the state; the larger pane beside it adds what comes next. At 13 and 18 pt in a 461 px window each line and action lies whole inside its pane, and Tab reaches the action with its ring whole (`empty_state::tests`). An assistive technology reads each line where it is drawn, as static text named by its whole line, with its tooltip's explanation as its description (`native_accessibility::state_tests`). The states:
  - No commits yet: History says “No commits yet · Your first commit starts in Changes” with **Open Changes**, and Changes, with nothing to stage, “No files yet” in its list and “No files yet · Files you add appear here for the first commit” in the preview.
  - No working changes: Changes' list says “Working tree clean” and its preview “Working tree clean · Edits you make appear here”; a status that could not be read says “Changes unavailable”, Refresh being just above, and one being read, by Refresh or by the quiet read after a Commit or Fetch, “Reading the working tree…”.
  - A Changes path filter with no match says “No files match this filter” inside the list, in place of both groups' headings, with **Clear filter**, which empties the filter and moves to the list; the list keeps the room to show both whole, and nothing above it moves. Where the line would wrap, as at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 half tile, it says “No matches”, named by the whole line, and Clear filter is its icon, named and tooltipped by its label. The line once sat above the Changes header, pushing it 21 px, and at 18 pt in a half tile was not drawn at all.
  - The Git action bar, in the place of the ahead and behind counts: detached HEAD says “Detached at ‹short commit ID›” with **Create branch…**, which opens Targets on its branch field, whose Create makes a branch at HEAD and switches to it; no remote says “No remote” with **Add remote…**, the remote form, whose glyph is the remote glyph, a server, with a plus, as Create branch…'s is a branch with one, so no second cloud stands beside Fetch's; a branch with no commits says “No commits to push yet”; no upstream says “No upstream · Push sets one”, Push setting it. A detached HEAD outranks a missing remote, which outranks a branch with no commits. The line's tooltip explains the edge. The action joins the bar's actions, after Push and before Targets, so in a half tile it wraps with Targets rather than taking Push's row. As the bar gives way it becomes its icon, its label its name and tooltip, then the line's caption (“· Push sets one”) goes to the tooltip, each only where the richer form would cost the bar a row (`GitTurtle::edge_form`, measured as the chrome measures the bar). The compact bar, one row, never cuts a word of the line: it draws the line's shortest form whole where it fits beside the branch picker's whole label, and otherwise the line's glyph (the commit for a branch with no commits, the remote for no remote or no upstream, the server that was a cloud beside Fetch's), muted as the line is, with the whole line its accessible name and leading its tooltip (`GitTurtle::compact_edge_fits`); at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 half tile “No remote” read “No r…” and a detached HEAD's line was not drawn at all. A detached HEAD's picker draws the commit glyph in both bars, where the full bar's drew the branch glyph beside “Detached HEAD”. In the compact bar it names the commit, its short ID beside that glyph, in place of “Detached HEAD”, which ran under Fetch, so the line says only “Detached”; where even that would be cut, as at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 half tile, the picker says “Detached” beside its commit glyph and the line leaves, so the bar says the word with one commit glyph, where it drew a second commit glyph in the line's place and never the word (`RepositoryEdge::compact_branch`; coordinator's decision, 2026-10-30: one glyph per meaning). In both bars the picker's name and tooltip say “Detached HEAD at ‹short commit ID›”; where the picker takes the line's place, its tooltip carries the line's advice between that name and its own purpose, “Detached HEAD at ‹short commit ID› · Create a branch here to keep new commits on it · Switch branch and repository actions” (`RepositoryEdge::advice`), so the line leaves none of its explanation behind (coordinator's decision, 2026-11-01).
  - A History search with no match says “No commits match · searched ‹N commits› in ‹scope›” with **Clear search**, which restores the preceding selection context, as Escape does; a paused bounded search keeps its progress and Continue action. The search's controls give way in the responsive order, measured from the interface font: where the column cannot hold the summary's 160 px floor (scaled) beside the labelled Cancel, Continue and Restart, they keep only their icons (close, a chevron and the refresh arrows) as 28 px controls with their names and tooltips that lead with those names, and where it cannot hold the floor beside those icons either, as History's 177 px column of a 461 px half tile, the summary leaves, the scope toolbar keeping the match count, so the controls are one row (in a half tile their labels took three rows and left the list 2 px). There a running, paused or failed search with nothing to list says its title alone (“Searching repository history…”), its explanation in its tooltip and accessible description, while the status below the icons (“Searching…”, or a failure's message) stays drawn. With no match the list's line already says how much was searched and Cancel and Continue are disabled, so where the controls are icons their status leaves, and in that narrowest column, or a window short enough for the compact chrome, as any 490 px tall one at 18 pt, the whole row leaves while nothing matches, so the line and Clear search keep the list's whole height; editing the query searches again from the current tips, as Restart does.
  - A commit's changed files: an empty commit says “No file changes against this parent”, and a path filter with no match “No matches”, with **Clear path filter** as an icon beside the filter field, where the list, 74 px tall at 18 pt in a half tile, has room only for its line.
  - Tags with none say “No local tags yet · Create tag… names a commit” beneath Create tag…, the line alone, or “No tags match this filter” with the summary (“1 local tag · …”, the count agreeing with its noun) above it, the line starting at the dialog's text inset, where its filter's text starts (with no tag at all the summary once offered to inspect, delete or push one); in a repository with no commits, where a tag has nothing to name, “No commits to tag yet · Your first commit starts in Changes”, Create tag… unavailable with that reason on its tooltip and in its name; stashes “No saved stashes · Save one from the Actions menu in Changes”; the Reflog “No reflog records for this scope”, its tooltip giving the likely reasons. The Tags and Stashes dialogs keep within a 461 px window, measured each frame. Stacked in a narrow window, the Stashes dialog's saved-work list is as tall as its header, one row and its footer, scaled with the interface size, so it keeps a whole row and its empty line, which starts at the rows' text inset, lies inside it at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window, where the unscaled list was 35.6 px and the line ran past its bottom. The browser lies in the dialog's content rather than its scrolling body and gives up the height the dialog's cap takes, as Keyboard Shortcuts' list does, so the body never cuts its lines mid-line, as it cut the detail's prompt at 18 pt in that window. With no stash saved it draws no detail, and the list takes its room; with stashes, the detail's prompt, “Choose a stash to inspect its files.”, stands at its edge where its loading line does, one line beside the stacked list at 13 and 18 pt (the longer prompt it replaced left “files.” alone at 13 pt), and the detail scrolls, cueing the rest with its scrollbar, where the window leaves it less than a stash's inspection, a chosen file's comparison keeping about six lines of its own; until a file is chosen the comparison keeps only its prompt's height. Beside the list, Restore staged state, Drop…, Review restore… and their note are the detail's footer, whole on selection without scrolling and in place while the rest scrolls; at 18 pt in a 1000 × 680 window they lay below the fold until the user scrolled. Stacked below the list in a narrow window, where the detail is a few lines tall (about 67 px at 18 pt in 461 × 490), they scroll with the rest, revealed with their rings as the keyboard reaches them. There the note sits on its own line beneath the buttons, `gap_2` beneath them as beside the list, at every size, and the detail keeps a floor that holds Review restore… whole with its ring, that gap and one line of the note (it once sat `gap_1` beneath them in every narrow window, 3.5 px higher at 13 pt than beside the list and than before the floor, where only 18 pt needed the room; at 18 pt in 461 × 490 the list gives the 4.5 px more down to its one whole row: move nothing already drawn, and one gap beneath the buttons in both layouts), so a refusal shows beside the control pressed without anything else scrolling. Where the dialog's height cap leaves the detail less, as at 18 pt in 461 × 490, the room comes from empty space only, the list panel's spare height beneath its one whole row and the gap above the detail down to `gap_1`, so nothing a person reads is lost; the dialog's content never scrolls, keeping one scroll per pane, which keeps the focus reveal simple and moves nothing (coordinator's decision, 2026-10-30). A taller window or smaller size leaves both as they were, and a window too short even with that room lets the detail go below its floor, where it scrolls and fades as before rather than spill past the dialog's foot.
  - The worktree manager's list, where its filter matches no worktree, says “No worktrees match this filter”, muted and without a closing period as Tags' line is, starting at the list's text inset, where the rows' text starts (it was drawn in primary text and ended in a period).
  - The GitHub panel of a repository with no GitHub remote says “No GitHub remote · Name the repository to load its pull requests” with **Name repository**, which focuses the destination field and, where the panel's body has scrolled it away, brings it into view with its ring, from a click as from the keyboard; it moves to a name field, not a search, so it draws no glyph; with a destination it says “No pull requests shown · Refresh PRs loads the open ones”, Refresh PRs being just above. Opening the panel contacts nothing.

  Empty remote/worktree sections have concise labels rather than failure styling. An absent image side says “No image on this side” and, on a second line, why: “The file was added” or “The file was deleted” ([Text and image comparison](#text-and-image-comparison)).
- Errors stay close to their origin, with a plain explanation and retry when meaningful. A cancel the person asked for is no error: where a GitHub panel request can tell Cancel stopped it, as a PR's Changes or a new PR's templates read locally, the failure's place reads “Cancelled · ‹what did not happen›” in the muted notice colour, a status said politely rather than an alert, until the next request clears it, while a failure the request met before the cancel reached it still shows as that failure; the cancelled comparison once read as a failure telling the person to Fetch. Feedback answers on the control the person touched, so the line stands where its request was asked for, recorded as the request starts: while a reply is open in the GitHub panel, what was asked from the reply's composer, Review reply… and the reply's Send, answers in that composer, under the draft and above Review reply…, and only in the composer of the conversation it was asked for: Reply on another conversation leaves it on the panel's top line, never under the other conversation's draft, which no one reviewed; and everything else, Open captured local comparison, Connect, Disconnect, a conversation's Resolve or Reopen (a conversation has no line of its own), Refresh PRs or the templates, answers on the panel's top line, as with no reply open; a comparison cancelled from Overview once said “Cancelled” under a reply's draft the person had not touched (the owner delegated the call; decided 2026-10-30). In the composer the line moves nothing already drawn above it, where the line above the composer or under its header would push the draft down as it appeared. While a conversation is open, the panel's top line, its notice, the failure or “Cancelled” that answers there, refused input and the busy row with its Cancel, stands above the body on the panel's surface with a divider under it, rather than among the content under the toolbar, and as it appears, changes or clears the body's scroll moves by as much in the same frame: nothing already drawn in the conversation moves, the draft being written included, and nothing becomes unreachable, since what the line takes from the body's top is still the body's, reached by scrolling up (the owner delegated the call; decided 2026-10-30). As the conversation closes, the line moves back among the content in the same frame and the body's scroll moves by the room the line takes there, read from that frame's layout, less the height it stood above the body, so the conversation stays where it was, as it does when a reply opens (the rows once moved by that difference as a reply closed: the divider's pixel, or the whole text a capped line hid). Among the content, the line pushed the conversation down by its height as it appeared, and a comparison cancelled while a reply was being written moved the draft twice. The line stands above the body rather than over it with room added to the body's top: the picture is the same, and the body needs no copy of the line to size that room. Where the whole content fits the body there is nothing to scroll, and the conversation moves down by the line rather than lie under it; likewise, when the line clears with the body scrolled to its very top, there is no scroll to give back, and the conversation moves up by the line. Text taller than half the body's room scrolls within the line, so the conversation keeps the rest, and the line's scrollbar cues what lies beyond its edge, as a list's does; a long remedy once ended at the line's edge with no sign of more. Say why, where the person is looking: when Reply opens a conversation's composer, a notice from before that is not about that conversation (a reply sent or restored, or the conversation resolved or reopened, is) goes, since pinned above the body it would only keep room the composer needs, and an outcome that appears in the composer is scrolled into view within the body with the draft above it, focus staying where it is; a stale notice once stood above the body while a refused reply's failure, and the composer's actions, fell below the body's clip at 13 pt in a 461 × 490 window with nothing to show them (the owner delegated the call; decided 2026-10-30). A status whose text carries its remedy keeps it drawn: where it does not fit beside its controls it takes a line of its own below them and wraps there, rather than ending in an ellipsis, as Review's draft status does beside Review submission…. The draft status at the end of a PR's section tabs is the exception (coordinator's decision, 2026-11-01): it stays one line at the end of the tabs' last line, in the room they leave, elided in its middle where it is longer, as macOS elides a name, with its whole text in its tooltip and accessible name, because on a line of its own below the tabs it moved the conversation under them as Reply saved the drafts and said so (39 px in a 461 px window); only where the tabs' last line leaves it less than its two insets and an ellipsis, as long counts such as “Files · 300+” can at 18 pt, does it start a line of its own below them, whatever it says, so it is never drawn as nothing and its line stays as the status changes: there an empty status keeps that blank line, since a status that took its line only once it had text would move the rows below as Reply saved; move nothing already drawn outranks drawing the remedy whole there, and the remedy stays a hover or a screen reader away. Missing repository: show the path and Open repository without old repository labels under the new path. An unavailable blob/image remains in Compare with its file list and Back to history active. Oversized text/image: show size and a bounded preview or explicit unavailable message; do not freeze or silently truncate content.
- A form whose review is refused or cannot be prepared (Amend last commit…, Save changes to stash…, Rename branch…, Create tracking branch, Add remote and Edit remote) closes as the review is asked for and opens again with the reason in the warning colour beneath its fields. That line is an alert announced at once, as when it appears in the open form, and again each time the form opens with it, by a fresh node (`native_accessibility::refusal_line`), so the same reason asked for again, as a review refused while a merge stays paused, is heard again. The line was no live region, so a screen reader heard neither. The other forms' error lines, Create tag's, Ignore's, the profile form's, the worktree manager's, the LFS download's, Stashes' detail's and the interactive rebase form's and message editor's, are alerts too: each form sets its line only through one setter (`tags::set_error_line`), as an action fails or is refused, which has the window's alert say it once, at once, bounded, in the same frame that draws the line in place (one frame per input), and again only for a new answer, the same refusal pressed again included, never as frames draw the line again; their lines were no live regions, so a failure was heard only by moving onto it. Each of those lines but Stashes' detail's is drawn in at most three lines, the last ending in an ellipsis where the report needs more, with the whole report, bounded as the alert's is, as its accessible value, and, where the line is cut, a tooltip that shows its first lines (`tags::error_line`, since a tooltip has no height of its own to keep), cut or whole as the frame the pointer rests on laid the line out, its width, text size and report together, measured in the line's own font, so a hover after a one-step resize, or a text size or report changed during the hover's delay, answers for the line drawn. A tooltip shown while the pointer rests on the line stays as it was shown through a resize that cuts the line or makes it whole, and the next hover answers for the new cut (decided 2026-11-01: macOS keeps a shown tooltip until the pointer moves off, and nothing already drawn moves without an input): drawn whole, a long Git report, as a hook's or a remote's stderr, grew the form by each of its lines, past its fields and actions, while the alert and the accessible value still give all of it. Stashes' detail's read failure, as the recovery forms' refusal lines (Amend last commit…, Save changes to stash…), keeps its whole text in a line that scrolls within 130 px instead (`recovery::form_error`, `recovery::form_line`).
- Refresh means reread local Git state. Describe remote-tracking references as locally available; never imply refresh fetched from a server. A status timestamp should explicitly mean last local refresh.
- Local filesystem and Git metadata notifications, plus window activation, request quiet refreshes. Coalesce bursts and defer work behind foreground reads or writes. Keep selection, scroll, search, editor focus, and manual conflict drafts stable; leave unchanged previews intact and refresh changed mutable content in its existing editor. A missing scoped branch retains the displayed context with an explanation. Watcher errors leave explicit Refresh available. Never start network operations or repository writes automatically.
- Authentication, SSH, credential-helper, hook, and signing failures preserve Git's original diagnostic and add a relevant next step. Long details belong in a scrollable operation-details view. Do not silently retry a write whose result is uncertain.
- Every count shown or announced agrees with its noun, and a verb that follows it with the count ("1 commit", "2 commits", "1 path differs"), built by one helper (`counted` in the app; its counterparts in Git core and the preview crate's metadata, which cannot reach the app's). A count with a fixed plural once read "1 shortcuts", "1 matches" and "1 channels".
- Motion: only where it explains a change the user caused, and never under reduced motion (see Motion below).

## Motion

Motion explains a change the user caused; nothing else moves. Each of the app's own transitions lasts 120–180 ms on an ease-out curve, fast from its start and settling into its end, with no delay, spring, bounce or overshoot. It never holds back input, layout or a frame's content: the first frame after the change lays out the final state, and the transition only changes where one element is drawn on its way into that layout, ending exactly where the layout puts it. It draws only what is on screen and asks for frames only while it is drawn.

- **A row moving between Staged and Unstaged.** When a file's own Stage or Unstage, from its row's control or its menu, moves it to the other group, the row travels for 150 ms from where the user left it in the window to its new slot, drawn above the rows it passes, beneath the Staged and Unstaged headings, which are not rows, and inside the list's clip. Every other row, heading and count takes its new place at once, and the slot stays empty until the row lands; a heading stays whole and on top in every frame, so the row passes out of sight beneath it and comes out the other side. Staging the first row under the Unstaged heading once drew it over the whole heading, which had already taken the row's place, in the flight's first frame. Until the rows the write produces arrive, with a path filter set or not, the list shows its rows as they were, never empty and with no progress line opening above them, so nothing moves before the row does. Where the new slot lies outside the list's viewport the row does not travel, and a Stage or Unstage that fails, or leaves the row where it was, moves nothing. Stage all, Unstage all, staging the selected files, a refresh and changes made outside the app re-sort the list at once: many rows moving together explain nothing the eye can follow. The pointer that pressed the row's control, or a heading's Stage all or Unstage all, stays where it was, and the new rows put another row's control, or a heading's Stage all, under it: whatever arrives there shows no hover fill and no tooltip until the pointer moves, with or without reduced motion, since the person never moved to it, and the pressed control takes its own tooltip with it, shown or still waiting out its delay. A click there still acts on it, and a click on a row still travelling reaches what the list draws beneath it: the travelling row is a picture of the row, which shows no press and which assistive technology meets only where it lands, and its Unstage once took a double-click's second press and unstaged the file again. That second press, on a row's Stage or Unstage or a heading's Stage all or Unstage all the rows moved under the still pointer, does nothing, while a single click there still acts, so a double-click never writes a file the person did not aim at (decided 2026-11-08: a write nobody aimed at is worse than a lost press; it once staged the neighbouring file, and from a heading's Stage all every remaining file). Epsilon's Stage once lit up under the still pointer after gamma's Stage moved gamma away, its tooltip "Stage epsilon.txt" covering delta's row; then, with that fixed, gamma's own "Stage gamma.txt" appeared over epsilon's row a second after the press and stayed until the pointer moved.
- **The toolkit's entrances.** Dialogs and dropdown menus keep the toolkit's entrance: a dialog fades and slides in over 250 ms, a dropdown fades in as it drops 8 px over 150 ms.
- **Nothing else.** Selection, hover, focus rings, scrolling (no smooth or spring scrolling; a list that reveals a focused control lands in one frame), History's graph and the lists as they reflow, page and mode changes, loading (a local progress label, no shimmer) and copy feedback do not animate. Nor does the compact chrome switching at its breakpoint: the form is chosen before layout from the window's size so that no frame shows one form and then the other, and a transition there would either animate the workspace's height, laying History or Compare out again in every frame and moving the content being read, or fade the new form in, dimming its controls below their contrast rules and restarting at every crossing while the window is resized, and neither shows where a control went.
- **Reduced motion.** With the platform's reduced-motion preference (`App::reduce_motion`, which macOS supplies from its accessibility display settings and Linux takes from the desktop through the XDG settings portal, where any of its sources asking reduces it; the keys are in [native accessibility](docs/native-accessibility.md#display-preferences)), or with Settings' **Reduce motion** turned on, every transition is immediate: the first frame shows the final place, nothing departs and no frame is asked for, the toolkit's entrances finish in their first frame, and GIF playback and model clips pause (see Documents, media and exact source). Settings' Reduce motion **Off** keeps the transitions and lets GIFs and model clips play whatever the desktop asks; **Follow the desktop**, the default, takes the desktop's answer.

## Text and image comparison

Text opens in the user's active Diff / Split / Before / After mode, defaulting to unified Diff for a new session. Make added/deleted lines explicit with signs and background tints; keep hunk headers blue and visually separate. A changed line's tint, whole-line changes and changed lines alike, in Diff and on each side of Split, with More contrast off or on, spans the editor's whole visible row from where the line's text starts, blank lines included, at every horizontal scroll position, so a hunk's changed lines form one block with a straight right edge; unchanged lines, hunk headers and Split's filler rows draw none, the gutter keeps its own tint, and a changed word's tint and its rule stay on its text (decision, 2026-11-05: the tint marks the line, not its text; ending where the text ended, it left a ragged edge, and a blank changed line was tinted under its `+` or `-` alone in Diff and not at all in Split, which read as a text highlight). The `---`/`+++` file headers above the first hunk read as body text at medium weight, and the unified patch carries no syntax colors of its own, so its decorations decide every color: `added`, `removed` and `hunk`, and under More contrast the syntax roles fitted from them (see More contrast above). Preserve indentation and literal patch text for copying. Source views show line numbers. Unified patches show separate old/new line numbers without inserting them into the copied text. Split diffs align changed rows with clear Before/After headings and synchronized vertical scrolling. Blank alignment rows are excluded from copied source text. Preserve each mode’s selection and viewport when switching modes. Respect horizontal scrolling for unwrapped lines. Word wrap, if exposed, is a user control rather than an implicit width-dependent change. Highlighting may arrive after readable plain text, without reflow. Build only the currently displayed editor mode.

Group Diff / Split / Before / After as one compact mode control. Working previews identify Staged or Unstaged beside the path so two versions of the same file remain unambiguous. Partial-staging selection survives a view-mode switch, while a changed working snapshot clears stale line selections. Split scrolling follows either side, including its gutter and Find navigation, without a feedback loop between editors. File rows and comparison paths provide full-path tooltips; keyboard hints follow the current page and workspace mode.

Staging by line follows the diff text's caret, as the GitHub panel's line selection does, never a Tab stop per line, which a long diff would multiply into hundreds (decided 2026-11-07). While a Working diff shows its per-line checkboxes and its text holds focus, Space selects the changed line under the caret, or every changed line the text selection draws, for the bar's **Stage N lines** (**Unstage N lines** in the staged area): all of them where any is unselected, else it clears them all, as clicks on their checkboxes would. A selection ending at the start of a line selects nothing on that line and leaves it out, wherever the caret is, so Space acts on exactly the lines drawn selected (Shift + Up twice from a line's start once toggled that line too, and a forward selection ending there selected a line never drawn selected, for the next press to stage). A context line, a hunk's header or the file's header selects nothing, a held Space acts once (on Wayland and macOS, which mark a held key's repeats; under X11 every autorepeat is a fresh press), and Shift + Up / Down extend the text selection as in any text. The checkbox on the caret's line draws a 2 px mark in the ring's accent at its leading side, a mark rather than a ring, since focus stays in the text; it follows the caret and goes when the text loses focus. Each Space is announced in place, from the Diff view's own polite live region ("Added line 12 selected to stage", "3 lines no longer selected to stage"), while the bar below the text counts the selection in its action, the status bar names Space and the text's accessible description says what Space does there and, while lines are selected and the bar it names is drawn, that Tab reaches that action; neither names Space during a partial write, when it does nothing. That bar's action is the next Tab stop after the text, Shift + Tab going back, and **Clear** the one after it, and pressing either returns focus to the text, so it never rests on a control that goes. The action carries the count, **Stage 3 lines**, named by the words it shows, so speech input that says what it sees finds it, with "Stages the 3 selected lines" as its description: at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window the Diff view is 243 × 151 px, where a count beside a longer action and Clear ran the action past its edge, its ring cut, and wrapped below whole they took the text's room. Both are the shared 28 px compact controls. Where the action and Clear's label do not fit on one row, Clear gives way first, in the one responsive order, as the shared 28 px icon control (the close icon, named and tooltipped "Clear line selection"), decided from the interface font before layout; only a bar too narrow even for that wraps it below, whole (Clear once wrapped below, taking the text's room, before it became an icon). The bar is laid out across the width the view last drew at, so the frame of a resize that crosses one of those thresholds still shows the form before it, and the next frame, which that one asks for, settles it with no further input (the request was once ignored, and the old form stayed until something else drew). The bar never covers the caret's line: in the frame it comes, goes or changes height, a caret line the text showed stays whole in the text above it, scrolled by the least, as the text reveals a moved caret (in that half tile the bar once covered the text's last rows, the caret's among them, until the caret moved). Only at the file's end, where a bar taller than the room the text scrolls past its last row asks for more than the text, not yet laid out above it, allows, does the frame it comes or grows in stop short, and the next finishes the reveal with no further input (a bar wrapped to two rows, 112 px in that half tile against 75 px of that room, once left the last row under it). The scroll its arrival takes is decided as the view renders, so the checkboxes and the caret's mark stand on their rows in the bar's first frame, where they once lagged a frame behind it by up to the bar's height. A checkbox click toggles its line as before and moves the caret there, scrolling the text by the least that shows a half-cut line whole, as the text reveals a moved caret, and keeping its sideways scroll; a hunk's **Stage hunk** stays a pointer action whose lines the selection reaches. Space in the read-only text did nothing before (the editor refuses the space it would type), so it belongs to staging only while the per-line checkboxes show.

Find matches use a selection tint in place of the patch background while preserving source text colors; an accent underline identifies the active match. This treatment remains stable when the viewport changes in every text mode. Closing Find restores the underlying diff treatment without replacing the editor or losing its viewport. Opening Find is explicit; preparing its decoration handle must not create hidden inputs or steal focus. While Compare is open, the platform's Find shortcut opens Find on the comparison shown from anywhere in the window, the Changed files list, the inspector and the toolbar included, its field focused: the patch, Before or After, and in Split the side whose bar is open, else After (Before for a deleted file); where Compare shows no text (an image, PDF pages, a rendered document or diagram, a notice, Blame or an error), the key does nothing and never leaves Compare, because Back is the way out of Compare and a search key that navigates would lose the comparison (decisions, 2026-11-03: from the Changed files list it left Compare for History's search, and Find opened only once the text was clicked). In History it keeps its meaning, the focused list's search. Opening it, or returning to its open field, selects the field's whole query and shows it from its start, the caret there, so the caret lies inside the field in the first frame whatever the query's length; typing replaces the query, and since the selection is anchored at its end, Shift with an arrow moves its start, where a macOS field anchored at the start would move its end (not yet checked on a Mac) (decision, 2026-11-03: the selection ran from the start, its caret at the query's end, and a query wider than the field kept the view the field closed with, so in a narrower window, or taken from the editor's selection, the caret stood past the field's end). The find bar lies across the comparison's whole width, line numbers included, the unified patch's or, in Split, both sides', its controls at a pane's content edge (`appearance::PANE_INSET`), where Split's side headings start their text: in a 461 × 490 window at 13 pt it is about 177 px wide in either, where it stood right of the line numbers, 94 px wide in the unified patch and 46 on a Split side. It follows the one responsive order, its forms chosen from its own width in the frame it is drawn: its buttons are the app's compact 28 px controls, in lines of that height whatever their forms, its lines and padding on whole device pixels at every scale, so its padding is as deep beneath its last line as above its first. It keeps one line wherever the field's floor, eight digits of the field's text inside its padding and border (about 73 px at 13 pt and 94 px at 18 pt), and every control fit it, in their order: the field, Match case, the count, Previous and Next, and Close at the line's end. So in a 1,480 px window it is one line, 35 px tall at 13 pt and 49 px at 18 pt, no taller than the two lines of the kit's 20 and 24 px controls it drew before them (46.5 and 64 px).
