Its controls give up their labels to keep that line, which moves no code: Previous and Next together first, for the chevron pointing back and on, as macOS's find bars point; then Match case its checkbox for an "Aa" toggle; then Close its icon. Where even their icons do not fit one line beside the field's floor, every control keeps its icon and the bar wraps them whole: Close beside the field where it fits there, Match case starting the next line with the count, Previous and Next at that line's end, and a line that cannot hold the next control wraps it below, Previous and Next always together, so nothing is cut and every focus ring lies inside the bar; a bar narrower than the floor gives the field its whole line. At 461 × 490 that is two lines in the unified patch and in Split (66 px at 13 pt, 92.5 at 18), where the unified bar took four and three and a Split side's six. Each icon form keeps its accessible name and shows its label as its tooltip (`system_preview::HeaderControl`); Previous and Next name their keys as the platform does (`shortcuts::ENTER` and `shortcuts::shifted`, Return on a Mac). Match case is a check box in both forms, named Match case and checked while Find matches case, and keeps its focus as it changes form. Split's sides share one find bar, drawn across both in their headings' row: it reads as the unified patch's bar, at the same width in the same forms, and both sides' rows start level at its foot in every frame a resize draws. It shows the side it searches in every state: that side's half of its 1 px bottom rule is drawn in the accent colour, on the rule's own whole device rows at every scale, with a query, with no matches and at every width, and its empty field names the side, "Find in Before" or "Find in After", or the side's name alone, whole, where that does not fit the field, as at its floor, while assistive technology reads the whole form (`workspace::placeholder_fit`). It is one bar with one query: opening Find over the other side's text moves the bar there with its query and Match case, searching that side, and the first side's matches clear; Escape in either side's text closes it (`editor_find::shared_bar`). In any find bar, its own or Split's, Escape closes Find from its field and from each of its controls, Match case, Previous, Next and Close alike, focus returning to the text it searches and Compare staying shown (decision, 2026-11-03: on a control the key was Back, so Escape on Next left Compare for History). Tab from its last control goes to the text it searches, After's while it searches After, though Before's text follows the bar in the window's order, and Shift+Tab from that text returns to that control. While it is open, Compare's footer gives way wherever it would leave fewer than three whole rows of the comparison in view, as it gives way to an image's minimum, so three rows of the comparison stay readable beneath the bar: the footer's line stays in the accessible description of the comparison's group, named "Split comparison" while it carries it, and the footer comes back as Find closes or the window grows. (Decisions, 2026-11-03: a bar on each side, about 88 px wide at 13 pt in a 461 × 490 window, took four lines, 129 px at 13 pt and 179 at 18, taller than the 116.5 and 110 px between the side's heading and Compare's footer, so the footer cut its last line and no row of either side was in view; in a side's 68 px between its insets no forms of the field's floor and five controls fit two lines, and the sides' rows had to be kept level by laying each side's region out with the other side's bar. Across both sides in the headings' row it takes two lines and leaves three whole rows at 13 pt; at 18 pt its two lines, 92.5 px of the 151.5 the headings and the text share, left two whole rows and the top of a third with Compare's footer drawn, and the bar does not fold further, since folding Close, Match case or the count, or shrinking the field below its floor, hides controls; without the footer, which gives way there, it leaves four whole rows. With a query typed and the headings hidden nothing on screen said which side the bar searched, and "Find in Before" was cut where the bar had just become one line, its field at its floor, 73 and 94 px wide at 13 and 18 pt, where the placeholder needs about 114 and 151; the marker is the least layout change that shows the side in every state, and the headings stay hidden so the bar keeps one row and the rows stay level. The bar moved to the other side kept that side's own last query, so a search typed over Before was lost.) (Decision, 2026-11-02: at 461 × 490 and 13 pt the unified bar drew its field 24 px wide beside Close and cut "Match case"; the controls grew from the kit's 20 and 24 px ones to the compact 28 px ones, so a change of form never moves the code; labels give way before a second line, because a second line moves the code below it while a label moves only its control. Its "Aa" form was a toggle button, which AT-SPI reported pressed where the checkbox was checked, and Split padded the other side a frame after the bar's height changed, so the sides' rows stood a line apart in the first frame of such a resize.)

Image comparison defaults to side-by-side Before/After on a subtle checkerboard. Show dimensions and byte sizes beside the labels, with a side whose file is absent saying so in the image's place: “No image on this side”, and on a second line, muted like the first, why, “The file was added” on Before and “The file was deleted” on After, which holds in any comparison, not only a commit's (“No image on this side” alone left the cause unsaid, and with the footer's “Added” given way nothing on screen said the file was added; `image_compare::absent_side`). Fit is the default; 100%, zoom in/out, and fit controls are small and familiar. Link zoom and pan across both images. Overlay and Wipe share the same source coordinates and linked pan/zoom, with keyboard controls for opacity and divider position. In a column narrower than the labelled modes or zooms, measured from the interface font, each group keeps one row as a single control that reads the current choice with a caret (“Side by side”, “Fit”), named “Comparison mode: Side by side” and “Zoom: Fit”, whose menu lists the choices with the current one checked, as the text modes do; the transparent mode menu gives its inset back to the toolbar's padding, so its label starts where the captions' names do, and so does the zoom menu where it leads a row, beneath modes it does not fit beside or as a source image's first group, keeping its widest choice's width and the room its group takes in the row (decided 2026-11-02, one left edge: at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window “Fit” started 8 px further in than “Side by side” and “Before” above it). Each side's caption gives its name a line as tall as the scaled 28 px control, then the side's dimensions beneath, and keeps to its top, so both sides' names and dimensions line up whether or not a side offers a control. Both sides' open controls take one form and one place, chosen from the narrower room a side's name leaves its control, both captions counted whether or not their side offers one, so an added or deleted image's one control stands where a changed image's two do: labelled where the label fits beside the name, otherwise the icon, and on the name's line wherever the name, its gap and the scaled 28 px icon fit; otherwise both stand as their icons in the comparison's toolbar, after the zooms at the trailing edge of the zoom menu's row, Before's first as the halves are ordered, each named for its side; on Linux its tooltip names the side as its accessible name does (“Open in default app: Before, a read-only copy as a PNG image”), and on macOS it keeps the icon's own “Quick Look · …” tooltip, as in its caption, to match macOS's controls, so one side's control never wraps alone (at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window Before's once took a line of its own, 48 px of the image's room, while After's stayed beside its name). The toolbar counts them among its groups, and its zoom menu's row holds them down to the column's minimum. (Decided 2026-11-01: on the line beneath both names, where they went next, they took the image 48 px at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window, leaving it 94 of its 133 px minimum, and over the image they would cover content; the toolbar's row moves nothing over content and keeps the image its minimum.) (Decided 2026-11-02: chosen from its own caption alone, where the shorter name leaves more room, an added image's After control stayed beside its name at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window while the zoom menu stood alone on a row beneath the mode menu, its widest choice leaving it 1.7 px too wide to share their row. Keeping Fit on the mode row would need a zoom menu narrower than its choices, and choosing 100% would then move the captions and the image down a row; a changed image's two controls already stand on the zooms' row, so the one control joins them there and nothing a changed image draws moves. So at 18 pt in the narrowest window the zoom menu keeps its widest choice's width even where that wraps Fit onto a row of its own beneath the modes, 48 px of the image's room: a Fit that shared the modes' row only until a zoom was chosen would move what is already drawn on that choice, and the row it takes holds the sides' open controls.) The zoom menu takes that row of its own only where the modes (their menu or labelled), the gap and the zoom menu, as wide as they are drawn, do not fit the toolbar; where they fit, the two are one run the layout never breaks, so a fraction of a pixel by which the layout rounds the column never wraps it (decided 2026-11-02, move nothing already drawn: leading within a pixel of fitting, it took a row of its own at 13 pt in a 461 × 490 window, where in the bundled interface font the 157.25 px row fits the toolbar's 157.5 px, and moved the captions and the image down 34.5 px). The run and the room are measured on the display's device pixels as layout draws them, the toolbar's padding and gap, the mode menu's inset and each menu's parts rounded as layout rounds them, and a menu is as wide as it is drawn reading its widest choice at every choice, so the run never ends in the toolbar's padding (summed unrounded, at 13 pt and scale 1.2 the run fit a room it was drawn 0.83 px past, and at 18 pt and scale 2 the mode menu drew “Side by side” half a pixel wider than “Wipe”). Each side's control tracks one focus handle wherever it stands, so a resize that moves it keeps focus on it. The dimensions stay only where the image keeps its usable minimum, a scaled 96 px, beneath the captions, Compare's footer and the notes on a line each, or where giving way would free no room, as beside a reason as tall as they are (overlaid at 18 pt in a 1,480 × 490 window After's once gave way beside Before's one-line reason and left the captions as tall); otherwise both sides' dimensions give way to it, as labels give way before content, their words staying in the side name's accessible name and tooltip, and they do not count a click's band, so the captions never change with a click's answer: in a 461 × 490 window the image had 83 px at 13 pt and 60 at 18 pt beneath them. A side without an image is not a dimension: why it has none (“No image on this side” and its cause, or why its image could not be shown) stays whatever the room. Side by side and across a source image's room it stands where its image would be, centered in its half or across that room, on at most two lines, or the one that room holds, each ending between words (where an absent side's two lines cannot both be drawn whole, each sentence on one line, at any size, it draws one short line that carries both, “No image: file added” or “No image: file deleted”, on one line where it fits and otherwise broken after its colon, “No image:” then its reason (decided 2026-11-04: wrapped where the line ran out, it read “No image: file / added” at 13 and 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window), and only where even that does not fit ending in an ellipsis (“No image…” in a room of one line that does not hold it whole), its tooltip and its read line keeping both sentences: say why in the fewest words, since the cause is the point of the second line, and at 461 × 490 the two lines once lost it, “No image on / this side / The file…”; decided 2026-11-02: where the room held them they still broke at 13 pt into four ragged lines, “No image on / this side / The file was / added”, so a sentence that would wrap takes the short line too), the last ending in an ellipsis after the most whole words that fit beside it where words are left out, or, where the second line's first word fits a line alone but not beside it, the first line ending in it instead, its last words dropped until it fits, so the text takes one line, with the whole line in its tooltip and accessible name, at every text size and width (decided 2026-11-04: clamped to the lines the room held and wrapped where a line ran out, a decode failure took six lines at 13 pt in a 461 × 490 window, broken inside its words, “JP2-compati / ble”, with no tooltip, and three at 18 pt; words stay whole wherever they can: a word wider than the room, which no line holds whole even alone, is cut at a character after the whole lines before it, alone on the last line before its ellipsis, and ends the text there (“Error: / /very/lo…”, not “Error…”, since in an error that word is usually the path or hash that matters), and any other word is cut only where no line drawn holds a whole word beside the ellipsis; the ellipsis once followed part of a word the second line held whole, “compatibl…”); there it is static text read by its whole line, so the side's name reads only the name, and where that room holds no line the name keeps its words, in its accessible name and tooltip. In Overlay and Wipe, where the side has no room of its own, it stands beneath its name: whole beside the dimensions, and on one line ending in an ellipsis where they give way (an absent side keeps one line whatever the dimensions, so the caption row never grows for it: its two lines joined by “ · ” where that line holds them, and otherwise its short line, both sentences in the name's tooltip; its two lines once made Before's caption a line taller beside the dimensions, leaving the image a line less; a reason of two lines is joined by “ · ” on that one line too, as it was before the absent side's rule, which once left it drawn unjoined), its words in the name's accessible name and, where that line cuts them, its tooltip (a decode error of about a hundred characters once wrapped to five lines at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window, took the image's room and ran past the footer and the preview's clip). Given way with the dimensions, an added image's Before said nothing on screen at 13 pt in a 461 × 490 window. Where the notes have given way, the image's area carries their words named for the images it draws (“After image” beside an added image's Before), and where it draws none, as a source image that could not be shown, it is a group named “Source details” rather than an image. That node holds only the images: a side's line saying why it has none lies over it as its sibling, so a screen reader, which presents an image by its name alone, still reads the line. Where neither the image nor a click's band is drawn, as where the room beneath the toolbar is shorter than the captions, the captions carry the words, named “Comparison details”, so the words that gave way always reach a drawn node; the captions and the band are groups in every layout, so the lines they hold keep their place in the accessibility tree as the room changes. The scale notice (“First image/frame · 100% = … of source size”) and the interaction guidance follow the image, each wrapping to a second line where it needs one and the image keeps its usable minimum, a scaled 96 px, beneath both, and otherwise one line each, ending in an ellipsis with the whole note in its tooltip and accessible name where it does not fit (at 13 pt in a 640 × 680 window each once read one line ending in an ellipsis above an image 270 px tall); at 461 × 490 they had pushed the captions under the preview's clip. Where even one line each would leave the image less than its minimum in the room beneath the captions, less any band saying why a click opened nothing, the notes leave the image their room, their words staying in its accessible description (or that of the band or captions where the image is not drawn), as labels give way before content: in a 461 × 490 window the image had 27 px at 13 pt and none at 18 pt. Compare's footer beneath the comparison, the file's status, modes and object IDs, is a label line too: it gives way where the image, beneath the captions, any band at its two-line cap and the notes on a line each, would keep less than its minimum with the footer drawn. Its room goes to what is drawn beneath the captions, the image or a click's band, or, where the captions alone do not fit above it, to the captions, which name the sides and hold their open controls; its words stay in the accessible description of the image, of the band where the image is not drawn, or of the captions where neither is (where giving way drew no image it once stayed, and ran past the preview's foot beneath captions the clip cut). The labels there give way in one order, a label whose words survive where it stood (a note's second line or the dimensions, in a tooltip) before one whose words survive only in an accessible description, and of those Compare's footer before the image's notes: the notes' second lines, then the dimensions, then the footer, then the notes, then the image's minimum. At 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window the toolbar and the sides' names left the image about 109 px above the footer's 33, under its scaled 133 px minimum, and the image now takes the footer's room. Every choice is made in the frame being drawn: the toolbars' groups (the path's actions, the modes and the zooms) from the column's width there, and what lies beneath them from the room beneath the toolbar, its width as well as its height (the band's lines, the open controls' form and the notes' second lines among them), the captions measured there with and without their dimensions, so a resize or a click's answer never draws a frame with the choice of the frame before (`in_frame::InFrame`); chosen from the column as last laid out, the toolbars drew their old groups for a frame after the window narrowed, the labelled modes 233 px wide in a 240 px column at 13 pt. The modes lead the image toolbar and the zooms follow them, at one place in every mode; overlaid or wiped, the adjustment keeps the trailing edge, and where its labelled line ("After opacity · 50%") does not fit the toolbar it keeps only its value, its words in its tooltip and its buttons' names, and is read by assistive technology as its whole line (it once read only "50%"), and wraps rather than run past the column, as it once did at 13 pt in a 461 px window. At a zoom other than Fit the pan's controls follow the zooms on their row and never take a row of their own, since chrome gives way before the captions and the image beneath it: a form takes its place beside the zooms only where the modes, the zooms, it and any adjustment fit one row, counted at their widest, so each form changes at one width and a wider toolbar is never less labelled. They are four arrows beside labelled zooms, otherwise one Pan menu that reads "Pan" with a caret and offers Pan left, Pan right, Pan up and Pan down, and narrower still, the zoom menu offers those directions after the zooms and is named "Zoom and pan: 100%"; a direction the images cannot move in, as Pan left and Pan up at their start, is unavailable. The zooms keep their labels only where the Pan menu fits beside them on that row, at Fit too, so choosing a zoom never changes their form (switching between Side by side and Overlay or Wipe can, where the labelled zooms fit beside the modes but not beside the adjustment too, since the adjustment counts only where it is drawn: counted side by side as well, it would take the labelled zooms from toolbars wide enough to hold them there), and the zoom menu, the mode menu and the adjustment's line are as wide as their widest choice or amount ("100%" once took the modes' row from a "Fit" that had shared it); the toolbar so keeps the rows Fit draws, at every amount: the captions and their open controls stay where Fit draws them (at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window a row of arrows once put the captions 48 px lower, Before's control under the clip and Compare's footer past the preview's foot). Preserve aspect ratio, avoid upscaling by default, and keep checkerboard contrast below the artwork. SVG is a static image; never execute embedded content. A Git LFS pointer without available local content must be identified as such rather than presented as a broken image.

The transparency grid uses the current palette's canvas and subtle surfaces, including Braden, and image metadata truncates within its own pane. Missing LFS text or image content offers a separate reviewed download that names the selected object, source and known size. Its row says why on one line beside its buttons, and only there: wherever the sentence would not fit beside them, with one button or two, decided in the frame from the measured widths of the sentence and the buttons, it is not drawn, the row gives the comparison the sentence's height, and the sentence moves to each button's tooltip and accessible description, as it does beside a failing side's line (decision, 2026-11-09: wrapped inside the pane at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window it took three lines above one button and left the comparison 4.5 px, a row offering one download standing taller than a row of two stacked buttons, and above two stacked buttons it pushed Compare's footer under the status bar). Beside a Download row alone the comparison keeps at least one whole row in Diff, Before and After, and Split its headings whole and at least one row, giving way in one order: the sentence first, then Compare's footer, whose words stay in the comparison's accessible description; where the room still cannot hold Split's headings and a whole row, Split draws its headings whole and gives its text what remains. Keep transfer progress/cancellation visible and explain tooling, credential, unavailable-object and integrity errors. Browsing remains passive; decoding starts only after verified local content is available. See [LFS preview semantics](docs/lfs-previews.md).

### Documents, media and exact source

The [file-preview support matrix](docs/file-previews.md) is the source of truth for detection, real rendering, source/metadata fallbacks, limits and platform requirements across History, revision comparison, Quick Open and Working Changes. Extend these entry points through the same captured Before/After identity model.

BMP/TIFF/ICO use the image comparison surface; TIFF and animations remain a single selected image/first frame. AVIF, HEIF/HEIC and JPEG 2000 use compatible macOS codecs and show an explicit platform/decoder limitation elsewhere, but Linux (x86-64 and AArch64) renders AVIF and JPEG 2000 through GitTurtle's confined helper and draws them as macOS does, the caption saying what it shows without "macOS codec" ("AVIF · first image", "AVIF · first frame of an animation", "· crop not applied", "· HDR not tone-mapped", "· color profile not applied"); a side it cannot show, HEIF/HEIC among them, says why in the image's place in the app's fixed words and still offers Open in default app ([content contract](crates/app/docs/content-and-layout.md#avif-and-jpeg-2000-on-linux)). PDF uses independently labeled Before/After page controls, total counts and the visible first-eight-page cap; Quick Open gives its single source the available width. PDF comparison is visual and has no text-staging actions. Linux renders the same page view as macOS (through GitTurtle's confined helper on x86-64 and AArch64) and draws nothing differently: a page that fails says why in the page's own room, read as static text, in the app's own words (“The PDF renderer could not start.”, “The PDF page took longer than 10 seconds and was stopped.”, “The PDF renderer stopped on this page.”, “The PDF renderer returned an unreadable result.” or the document's error, as macOS words it), because a person should learn why a page is missing where they look for it. A later page offers Retry page and a failed first page none, as on macOS; nothing retries by itself, since a hostile page would only burn the CPU again. Read page text acts only for a page whose text result arrived, so it is never an action that opens nothing; until then it keeps its place in the header, disabled, its tooltip and accessible name saying why in the one sentence the page's room shows until the page arrives, “Preparing the page…” (decision, 2026-11-02: one sentence for one wait, whether or not the request has reached the render lane; the room said “Page preparation pending…” and then, after any redraw, “Preparing selected page…”, while Read page text said “Preparing the page…”), and where the page failed Retry page takes that same place at the same width, so nothing already drawn moves while a page prepares (decision, 2026-11-02: drawn only once the text arrived, it left its place while the next page prepared, so at 13 pt in a 1,480 × 973 window Next moved about 75 px left with the link under the pointer, and a second click on Next unlinked the pages). A reply that places a requested page outside the document reads as unreadable, never with the helper's count, which would contradict the page count already shown beside it. A side with no pages says why as static text too, an absent side's “No file on this side” or a document's error, such as a password-protected PDF's sentence: where the other side has pages, the page view draws it beneath its own header and says why in its room, wrapped within the side on the lines that room holds (decision, 2026-11-03: the message's frame took no height there, so in a 461 × 490 window an added PDF's absent Before read “No file on…” on one line with about 280 px free beneath it), and only where neither side has pages are both drawn on the generic document surface, which says the same (drawn there as plain text, neither reached an assistive technology). Fit, −, + and the zoom level are one run in a side's header that moves to the next line whole rather than break, as the image comparison's mode and zoom menus are, wrapping within only where a line of the header cannot hold it, and the level keeps the width of its widest reading, 400%, so a zoom never moves what follows it (at 1,480 × 973 a side with no link once ended its first line with Fit and began the next with − + 100%). The page number likewise keeps the width of its widest reading for the count in the form it takes, labelled or short, and the labelled link the wider of “Independent pages” and “Linked pages”, so a page turn or a click on the link moves nothing after them (drawn at the reading it showed, the page number grew with “Page 10 / 10” after “Page 1 / 10”, and in a proportional font with any page whose digits are wider, and moved the link and the zooms on a page turn, and “Linked pages” narrowed the link and moved the zooms on each click). The page number's dialog opens with the current page selected in its field, as a native page field does, so the number typed replaces it (with the caret before it, typing 9 on page 4 read 94). A side is headed by what it holds, never by its partner's format: a side that holds a PDF reads “Before · PDF” where a line of its header holds it, and one that holds none, an absent side above all, reads its name and then its own format as the caption the generic document surface heads every side with (“Before” and “Absent”), so an absent side reads its absence alike beside an added PDF, whose pages the page view draws, and beside a locked one, which leaves both sides to that surface (decision, 2026-11-02: every side of the page view read “· PDF”, so an absent Before read “Before · PDF”, its partner's format, beside an added PDF and “Before” and “Absent” beside a locked one). Where a line does not hold that, a heading reads the side's name alone, its format giving way first as a caption does, ending in an ellipsis where even that does not fit (laid out whole, it was cut at the side's edge, “Before · PD”, at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window). Both sides of a comparison give their kind way together, both reading it or neither (`pdf_view::pair_headings`; decision, 2026-11-03: each side dropping it alone, two paged PDFs in 461 × 490 were headed “Before” beside “After · PDF”, the wider “Before · PDF” not fitting its line). Keep white document pages separate from themed chrome and checkerboards.

Media, Office/OpenDocument, archives and fonts present useful bounded metadata with an explicit system-preview action for supported captured bytes. Label metadata as metadata. Preserve literal source access for Markdown, HTML, CSV and ordinary code; repository HTML/scripts/macros are not executed. Where a comparison draws Copy source once on each side, in a document's body or a model's header, its accessible name leads with the side, "Before: Copy source" and "After: Copy source" (or Copy pointer for an LFS pointer's text), as each side's heading leads what it shows, so a screen reader listing the two, or speech input naming one, tells them apart; a side shown alone keeps the plain label. An SVG's two copies of literal source share the comparison's row of captured actions rather than each side's caption, so their drawn labels must say their sides too: each is drawn and named by the same helper (`system_preview::side_control_name`, through `image_compare::literal_source_copy`), "Before: Copy source" and "After: Copy source", so the words drawn are the words named and speech input that says what it sees finds the control; they once read "Copy Before source", the side's prefix spelt apart from every other side's control. The model's once read "Copy exact After model source", holding neither the side first nor the label shown, and the document's two were both "Copy source". Decoded UTF-16 is identified as decoded text and cannot become a Git patch. SVG retains literal source copying even when rendering is refused. External preview opens a controlled captured-byte copy, never a substitute working file; missing sides and errors stay visible. A Git LFS side whose object is over the 32 MiB preview limit or damaged, or whose local store cannot be read, stays its pointer and says which, and offers no download, which could not help, while the other side keeps its preview (decision, 2026-11-09: one side's object failing once failed the whole preview, though the other side was readable). On the source tabs and Split it says so on one warning line above the comparison, two failing sides sharing that line; Rendered and Diagrams say it in the side's own place and draw no line, which said it twice. The comparison keeps three whole rows beneath the line and the line gives way first, down to its one line; Split, which holds its sides' headings as well, then lets Compare's footer give way rather than run past the pane's foot, its words kept in the comparison's accessible description (decision, 2026-11-09: three rows and the footer did not both fit at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window, and the status bar covered the footer's foot); where a Download row stands there too, it keeps its height, its sentence moves to the button's tooltip and accessible description, the line gives way, and the comparison keeps one whole row (Split gives way in the one order it follows beside the line alone, the line first, to its one line, then Compare's footer, whose words stay in the comparison's accessible description, its headings and as many whole rows as fit, at least one, staying; only where the room cannot hold its headings and one row does it draw what the room holds, its headings whole first; decision, 2026-11-09: with no floor there at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window the line kept its three lines and left Split a strip 9 device pixels tall, no heading's words drawn, while beside the line alone it gave way line first; the modes menu leads to Unified), the line scrolling inside its own height where it no longer fits, a Tab stop that Up and Down scroll while it overflows, as an over-tall state does (Empty and edge states) (decision, 2026-11-09: the line took about 68 px at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window for one side and about 130 px for two, of the 110 px between a side's heading and Compare's footer). A side that stays its pointer reads as what it is: in Rendered its heading and names say Git LFS pointer rather than Markdown, its text carries no source line, and Linked blocks is not offered beside it, having nothing to link; an image side offers Copy pointer and nothing to open. On Linux the action reads **Open in default app** and sits with the side it opens: after the side's name in a document's, diagram's or model's side header and in an image side's caption, above the image's dimensions, so a changed image shows each side's control by its own side and adds no row of its own, and the control stays on the caption's first line wherever the name leaves it room. A document's or diagram's two side headers share one row, which gives both the taller one's height, the heading's line as tall as the scaled 28 px control and each header's lines kept to its top, so Before and After align whether or not a side offers the control or wraps it below its name, as in a 461 × 490 window, where Before's header once ended 10 px above After's. It appears only for what the bytes establish: a raster image or PDF wherever one is shown, and plain text only where a view presents a file's captured text as a document, which is the Diagrams view of a Mermaid file or of Markdown with Mermaid diagrams (the file's text) and a text 3D model's header (such as OBJ, ASCII STL or STEP), never a source diff, the source tabs or Markdown's Rendered view. It names in its tooltip the type the default application receives. Its accessible name takes one form on every surface: the visible label first, so speech input finds the control by what it shows (WCAG 2.5.3), then the side, which the label leaves to the heading beside it, then that type ("Open in default app: After, a read-only copy as a PDF document", "Open in default app: Before, a read-only copy as a PNG image"). macOS names its **System preview** control in the same form, ending with what the surface shows ("System preview: After, a read-only copy of the captured PDF"), where it once led with the side ("After: open captured PDF in System preview"). On both platforms, below the width where its label fits beside the side's name or dimensions, the control draws its icon alone in the scaled 28 px square, its label leading its tooltip (on Linux "Open in default app: a read-only copy as a PDF document") and its accessible name, so the content beside it keeps its place and the action becomes an icon rather than being cut at its column's edge; its focus ring keeps its room inside the column. On both platforms the control answers where the click happened, as a copy button does, never in the window-wide notice: once something opened it reads **Opened** after a check for 1.5 s in its own bounds and "Opened a read-only copy of the captured bytes." is announced politely, naming no application because the portal can let the person choose one; when nothing opened, a line beneath the control's row says why, a failure in the warning color fitted to the page's surfaces and announced at once by its whole answer ("Could not open After. …"), or, when the portal reports the request cancelled, the muted "Nothing opened.", which is not a failure: the portal answers alike whether the person closed the application chooser or closed one that offered no application ("No Apps available"), so the line names no cause. Beneath an image side's caption the line takes a band of its own in that side's half, whose panel surface and rule span that half alone (they once spanned both halves while the text wrapped in After's), so neither caption nor either side's name moves (a two-line refusal inside the caption once grew the captions by 42.5 px and dropped the other side's name 20.5 px) and the image's notes, then the image, give way below it, the band covering neither (laid over the image, a refusal in a 461 × 490 window once covered both notes and ran past the column). There, as in a document's, diagram's or model's header, the line is short and leads with its reason, its whole answer staying in its tooltip, its accessible name and the announcement. The line is written from the failure's category, which the launcher and the private copy's store return with its sentence, never from words found in the sentence: "Seen as an SVG document, so not opened" where the desktop would type the copy as something else, "No default app for image/png" where the copy's type has no default application, "Over 32 MiB, so not opened" where the captured file exceeds the copy's size limit, and "Could not open this copy" for any other failure, to which a category without a line of its own also falls back; macOS's Quick Look reports every failure in that last form. It shows every line where the image keeps its usable minimum beneath the band and otherwise two, ending in an ellipsis, so the image stays in view, and it always ends at a whole line inside the room the captions leave, giving up its padding first where that room is shorter: in a 461 × 490 window the band once took the whole room, showing only "Could not / open After." at 18 pt over a hidden image, and a 100 px cap cut its last line at 640 × 680. Where the band leaves the image less than a pixel, the image is not drawn and the band reaches the column's foot with no rule of its own, Compare's footer having given its room to the band and its words to the band's accessible description (a 1 × 0.5 px sliver of the image once lay between the band's rule and the footer's, at 18 pt the two made one 2 px rule, and a footer that gave way there once left its words on the undrawn image alone). In a document's, diagram's or model's header the short line is the header's last line, beneath all of its controls, a model's camera, views and clip controls among them. A side with a header of its own, a 3D model's or the PDF page view's, keeps that header and its line inside the preview whatever the room, in one order, content first: the rule beneath the header keeps its pixel and the line its one whole line where a click answered, the page or model its usable minimum wherever that leaves the header its floor, one control whole with the larger of its focus ring's room and the header's own padding above and below, so a header of one row shows whole and unscrolled there, and the header the rest (the floor was once a control's height alone, which cut the ring of the control Tab moved onto in a side as short as the rewrite review's 100 px file box or the Stashes dialog's 120 px floor, and then counted the ring's room and the padding together, which took a PDF page about 6 px more than it needed at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window). Where a model's controls are taller than that room, as a narrow side's wrap at 13 and 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window and at 18 pt in 640 × 680, they scroll there rather than running past the preview's foot, cued while they overflow as every scrolling list is (see Keyboard contract): at the region's 36–44 px floor, where the toolkit's 48 px thumb filled its track, with no scrollbar and a soft fade at each edge its controls continue past, and Tab scrolls the control it moves onto into view with its ring; they once took the line out of view, and then the page's or model's whole room. The page view's header does not scroll in a narrow side: a side too narrow for its whole header takes a compact form instead, so its controls stay in view wherever its room holds one. It has three forms, which a side takes in one order as it narrows, never returning to a wider one: the whole header, where each of its parts, every control labelled, fits a line of the header to itself and they wrap into at most three lines; a compact form, only where its five controls fit one line: Previous, the page number and Next, a zoom menu reading the zoom, which holds Fit page width, Zoom out and Zoom in, as the image comparison's toolbar folds its zooms, and a "more" menu, its "more" icon, which holds Read page text or Retry page, Linked pages (checked while linked) and the open control, as the model's views menu folds its views; and otherwise the narrowest, Previous, the page number, Next and a "more" menu that holds the zooms too, first, beneath "Zoom · 150%", then the page actions, its tooltip and accessible name reading the zoom ("More page actions, zoom 150%"). Compact only where those five fit one line, so each form, wherever a side's width allows it, is allowed at every wider width too, and the forms change in one order as a side narrows; at 18 pt a 1,000 × 680 window's sides take the narrowest form, on two lines. Previous, the page number and Next are one run, which moves to a line of its own whole, so the arrows are never split, and the page number takes the widest reading, labelled, short or its icon, that keeps the run on one line, giving way in that order within each form (the narrowest form, which folds the zoom menu away, may give it back its label). The form is chosen before layout from the side's drawn width, the room its page's minimum leaves the header, the text size and the display's scale alone, on device pixels (`pdf_view::header_layout`): the form its width allows where that fits the room, and otherwise the first narrower one that does, the compact form only where its run still fits one line, so a short room never brings a compact header that wraps, and a side never changes form between frames at one size, nor as a page prepares, turns or the pages link. Both sides of a comparison take one form, the narrower of the two sides' own, each laid out within its own room in that form or narrower (`pdf_view::pair_layouts`), so their headings lie level and both show the same controls wherever the person looks across: each side choosing alone, a Before of 3 pages took the compact form while an After of 111, whose short page number is wider, took the narrowest, After's heading 3.5 px higher at scale 2 (3.33 px at 1.2), in 234–260 px sides at 13 pt, and the full form beside the compact one at 292–304 px. In that form the pair also draws the page number in one reading, the narrower of the two sides' own, labelled only where both sides' labelled readings keep their runs on one line, short only where both short ones do, and otherwise its icon on both, and reads both headings' kind or neither (see Documents, media and exact source); both headers wrap on one line width, the narrower side's as layout rounds each side's frame to device pixels, and every control is measured as layout places it there, its insets and gaps each on a device pixel and its label rounded up to one, rather than summed unrounded (`pdf_view::pair_line`, `placed_button_width`), so the two sides draw the same controls on as many lines and their pages lie level at a fractional scale as at a whole one (decision, 2026-11-03: at scale 1.2, as Hyprland gives a panel it cannot set to 1.25, sums of unrounded widths said a line held what layout wrapped, so at 13 pt in 314 px sides After's full header took a fourth line beside Before's three and “Independent pages” was cut on both; each side chose its page number alone, so in 156.5 px sides Before drew “1/3” and its run wrapped while After drew its icon on one line, the pages 38 device pixels apart; and in a comparison an odd number of device pixels wide After's frame is a device pixel wider than Before's, where an identical run held one line beside Before's two). The narrowest form's lines lie a quarter rem apart and from the header's top and rule rather than half a rem, which still keeps the ring of a control on its first or last line inside the header: at half a rem its heading and two lines of controls took 110 px at 13 pt in the 88 px sides of a 461 × 490 window, more than the 102 px the page's minimum leaves the header, and 152 px at 18 pt in 640 × 680, more than its 144 px. A side without pages beside it takes the same quarter rem above its heading, from its partner's header as the pair lays it out for that partner's own room, so the two headings lie level there too: in half a rem an absent side's heading lay 3.25 px below its partner's. Each item is named as the control it stands for, and Read page text, while its page is prepared, is unavailable with "Preparing the page…" stated beside it, the menu keys resting on it as on every item that states why. Where a resize changes the form, focus stays where the person put it: a folded control's focus, Fit's, Zoom out's, Zoom in's, the zoom menu's or a page action's, passes to the menu that holds it, and back to the control as its form returns. At 13 pt the narrowest header fits the 461 × 490 window's 102 px of room in 96 px, and at 18 pt in 640 × 680 its 144 px in 135 px; at 1,000 × 680 and 1,480 × 980 at 13 pt the header is whole (110 px and 75.5 px tall at scale 2). A header that scrolled at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window kept the controls out of view out of reach of assistive technology until scrolled, laid Read page text's accessible centre over the page and cut its messages. Only where the room the page's minimum leaves the header is shorter than even the narrowest form does it scroll, as a model's header scrolls, so no control is lost there: at 18 pt in Compare in a 461 × 490 window (its narrowest form 222 px tall in 91 px of room at scale 2) and in the Stashes dialog's and the rewrite review's small file boxes (about 100–120 px), where one line of a narrowest header, 34 px at 13 pt, fits whole beneath the header's floor but two do not. A line saying why a click opened nothing keeps its place above the page's minimum, so the header may scroll while that line shows, where without it the header fits. An assistive technology's own focus or click reaching a control the scrolling header has clipped is not yet revealed; that is a follow-up. The message a page view side shows where its page would be (a wait, a failure, a locked or absent side) shows the whole lines its room holds, the last ending in an ellipsis where it must be cut, and is read whole. Each control lies across the side whole with its ring too: a labelled control wider than a line of the header to itself draws its icon, its whole label leading its tooltip and its accessible name the one it has labelled (Copy source its copy icon, the model's Linked or Independent its link icon, the views menu the "more" icon, Reset and Edges their reset and edges icons, the clip menu its clip icon, the chosen clip's name leading its tooltip), and the page number its short reading ("1/3", "Page 1 / 3" in its tooltip) and, where that does not fit either, its page icon. A model's header counts each line less the gutter its scrollbar takes whether or not the header scrolls, so no form changes with the scroll it causes. The page view's header decides before layout whether it scrolls, from its room, and only then leaves the gutter on its lines, so the gutter never changes with the scroll it causes and a header that fits keeps none (counted on none of its lines, the compact zoom menu was cut by the gutter at 13 pt in 461 × 490; counted on all, Previous and Next took lines of their own in 88–123 px sides, 213.5 px at 13 pt and 297 px at 18 pt, taller than any room); its heading is drawn no wider than its line, where as wide as its header it was once drawn whole into the gutter at 13 pt in 58–63 px sides. One rule, the side header's control helper (`system_preview::HeaderControl`), draws every one of them. Each form is chosen from the control's widest reading, a page number's with each of the count's digits the widest digit, so a toggle, a view, a clip or a page turn never changes it: in an 88 px side at 13 pt in a 461 × 490 window the labelled forms were cut at the side's edge, the clip menu was cut on a narrow side after the others had given way, and a page number measured at the count ("Page 111 / 111") could be narrower than another page's reading ("Page 108 / 111"). Each is named the same in every form, the side first, as the page arrows are: Retry page, as Read page text, names its side and page ("After: retry page 1"), and Edges and the page view's link name the state they show, as the model's link does ("Before: Solid shading. Show all triangle edges", "Before: Triangle edges shown. Switch to solid shading", "Before: Pages independent. Link pages to this page"), where Edges once read "Toggle all triangle edges" while it showed Solid and the page view's link "linked PDF page navigation" while its pages were independent. A model's details, a label, give way before the header's controls do (at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window they once left After's open control 10 px), and where they give way in part their own scroll area shrinks with them, so every line stays within its reach, with a visible scrollbar at their edge while their lines overflow and none where they fit, as the commit inspector's message has (clipped whole by the room left, they were once cut mid-line beyond it, and then, shrunk to a sliver, showed no sign that the rest scrolled), and none where they have given way to less than the toolkit's shortest thumb and its insets, an unscaled 56 px, too short a track for the thumb to move along (there the toolkit's thumb once ran out of its track against the scroll, or moved a few pixels at most, and a drag divided by the track's length less the thumb's, at most zero), where they fade the edge their lines continue past instead. Their room holds their padding and four of their lines at the interface size, so at the default text size a wide side shows the usual details whole with no scrollbar, as a triangle's in a 1,480 × 980 window at 13 pt (at 18 pt they may wrap past it and scroll, the details giving way before the content), and where they overflow it the scrollbar's thumb is shorter than its track and moves as they scroll (capped at an unscaled 48 px, the toolkit's shortest thumb, the thumb filled its track and never moved); the page view's position, read politely as it changes, lies outside the header's scrolling region, and a new file's header starts at its top. The page, diagram or model beneath keeps its usable minimum first, a scaled 96 px as an image keeps beneath its band, since content stays whole and labels go: the line shows every line it wraps to where the room beneath the header's controls holds them above that minimum, and otherwise the whole lines that room holds above it, the last ending in an ellipsis, so it always ends at a whole line. It always keeps one whole line, as the band beneath an image side's caption does, because a click answers where it happened: where that room holds none above the minimum, the line shows one and the content beneath gives way below its minimum for it, as an image does beneath its band, the line's padding beneath going first where the side is shorter than the line itself, and the whole answer stays in its tooltip, its accessible name and the announcement. It once drew none there, so a failed open changed nothing on screen in the rewrite review's file box (100 px), at the Stashes dialog's 120 px floor and in Compare at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window. The line and the content beneath it are laid out for the room the side has in the frame they are drawn (`in_frame::InFrame`), so neither the first frame after an answer nor one after a resize draws the line for another room. The header's line once showed the whole answer, scrolling inside a scaled 100 px that cut a line at 13 pt in a 640 × 680 window; it then took every line the clip left it, which at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window left a PDF page no height, and in a model's header, among the source actions, it pushed Fit, Reset and the views down. The line stays while that file is shown, goes with the next click on the control, and is gone once another file has been shown, even when the same preview returns. Do not count external viewing, container recognition or a generic unavailable notice as in-app rendering.

## Command palette

**Primary-Shift-P** and **View → Command Palette** open a native, focused command search; **Primary-P** remains Quick Open. Search names and useful synonyms from the bounded existing-workflow registry. Browse stashes… is one, named as the Working Changes Actions menu names it and available where that item is, on the repository page while no write runs and once the reload after one has landed, and otherwise saying why it waits, as the item does (decided 2026-11-02, so a menu's action is reachable from the palette by its name: only that menu opened Stashes, so a search for “Browse stashes” or “stash” matched nothing and Return did nothing). Rows show shortcuts and application/repository/selected-file scope; unavailable actions stay discoverable with a reason, their titles in regular weight and the disabled color where an available command's title is medium, so weight and color both mark them. Highlighting is inert. Return or a click revalidates the target and opens its existing workflow/review once; Escape/Cancel restores the preceding visible focus.

Use 54 px scaled rows, an adaptive one-to-seven-row result height, a compact empty-result explanation and a viewport cap at large text sizes. Opening the palette and every change to the query highlight the first command the context can run, or the first row when none can, with the list shown from its top; Up/Down moves selection one row at a time, unavailable rows included, and keeps it visible. Preserve query editing shortcuts, consume background navigation shortcuts and avoid opening over another active modal. The [command-palette contract](docs/command-palette.md) owns search bounds, availability and target validation.

## Keyboard contract

Use `Cmd` on macOS and `Ctrl` on Linux for the primary modifier. The [user guide](docs/user-guide.md#keyboard-controls) and native Keyboard Shortcuts help list implemented bindings. Keep this table, menus and action availability aligned with the current page; do not advertise unavailable actions in the application.

| Binding | Action |
| --- | --- |
| Primary + O | Open repository |
| Primary + Shift + O | Open Projects |
| Primary + P | Quick Open a tracked file in the shown repository worktree/revision scope; the file opens with focus on its source text, ring drawn, and the window's shortcuts work from there |
| Primary + Shift + P | Open the command palette |
| Primary + 1 | Return to retained History |
| Primary + Shift + C | Compare local revisions |
| Primary + Shift + A | Open local GitTurtle operation activity |
| Primary + 2 | Open Working Changes |
| Primary + , | Open Settings |
| Primary + F | Focus commit search in History; use editor find while reading text in Compare |
| Escape | Dismiss active transient UI first, then return through inspection/page context; at root History clear its query and focus History |
| Primary + [ | Return through inspection/page context; Compare/Changes return to History, root History returns to Projects |
| Up / Down | Select previous/next row in the focused list |
| Home / End | First/last loaded row in the focused list |
| Page Up / Page Down | Move through the focused list by viewport |
| Left / Right | Collapse/expand a focused navigation section |
| Return | Open the highlighted file in Compare from either history or the file list |
| Return in a dialog | Press the focused Button, once, as the key goes down, never on a held key's repeats (under X11 a held key arrives as fresh presses), since the ring marks the control the keyboard acts on; in a single-line field, on the dialog itself or on its default Button, run the default action, as the key goes down and never on a held key's repeats; a multi-line field takes a new line. A dialog an Enter press opened takes no Enter until that press comes up, so a held Enter never confirms what it opened, under X11 too (decisions, 2026-11-05: Enter on a focused body Button, or on Cancel, ran the default action and pressed nothing; held on a Button that opened a confirmation, Enter's next repeat ran its write) |
| Option/Alt + Up / Down in text | Previous/next change |
| Option/Alt + Up / Down in the rebase sequence | Reorder the selected commit |
| P / R / S / F / D in the rebase sequence | Pick / Reword / Squash / Fixup / Drop |
| Primary + Option/Alt + Up / Down in conflicts | Previous/next unresolved block |
| Primary + click / Shift + click in Working Changes | Toggle a file / extend the selection range |
| Shift + Up / Down; Primary + A in Working Changes | Extend the range; select visible file rows |
| Space in a Working diff's text | Select the changed line under the caret, or every changed line the text selection draws, for Stage N lines / Unstage N lines; again to clear them. Shift + Up / Down extend the text selection; Tab moves on to the action |
| Shift + F10, or Menu on Linux, in the navigator, the project list, History's commit list or a file list | Open the cursor row's menu below it, the one a right-click opens, on its first action; Escape returns to the tree or list |
| Tab / Shift + Tab | Traverse controls and panes in visual order; on an open menu they move nothing, and in an open popover they stay inside it, as in a dialog (below); in multi-line text fields (the commit and amended descriptions, the rebase message, the tag annotation, the remote form's URL and refspec lists, the GitHub composers) they move focus too and indent nothing; the manual conflict result, which holds code, keeps them for indenting |
| Primary + R | Refresh local repository state |
| Primary + C | Copy selected text; explicit copy buttons handle commit hash/path |
| Primary + B | Toggle expanded repository navigation in History |
| Primary + Shift + B | Show or hide the project list |
| Primary + ? | Open Keyboard Shortcuts (Shift+/ on a US keyboard) |

An open menu is one stop and keeps keyboard focus, as a dialog does (decided 2026-11-07): Tab and Shift + Tab leave focus where it is and move nothing, on every platform, as macOS menus take them, while Up and Down move through its rows. That holds for every menu the app opens: a button's (among them the branch picker's menu, the Git profile picker's, Changes' Actions, the tab strip's and the viewers'), a row's from a right-click or the keyboard, and a text field's drawn right-click menu. A popover with several stops cycles Tab and Shift + Tab through its own stops and wraps at both ends. The theme editor's colour picker has two: its swatch grid and its hex field (decided 2026-11-09). The grid is one stop that the arrow keys move inside, along a row or to the swatch above or below it, stopping at the grid's edges; the swatch focus rests on draws the focus ring whole, previews its colour in the hex field as hovering it does, is the one Tab comes back to, and is named by its colour for a screen reader (“Red 500, #EF4444”), and Enter or Space, as the key comes up, chooses it, once however long the key is held. Either is left by Escape, which gives focus back to its opener, by a choice, or by a click outside, which closes it. Focus never rests behind a drawn menu or popover: Tab from the branch picker's open menu once moved focus to Main Menu, the tab and its close button behind it while the menu stayed drawn, acting on controls the person could not see (`native_accessibility::bind_keys`, bound in the kit menu's own key context, deeper than the window's Tab; the colour picker's in `theme_editor::init`, in the kit popover's context inside the row's picker, as its popover stays drawn when focus leaves it).

A menu opened from the keyboard (Enter or Space on its button, Shift + F10 or the Menu key on a row, F10 for the Linux Main Menu) opens with its first item that can act under the keyboard's cursor, so Enter acts on it and Down moves on from it, as native menus open; a menu a pointer opened highlights nothing until the pointer or a key moves. Every menu stays above the status bar: it opens below its button or row, or at the pointer, and where the room down to 8 px above the bar is shorter than its items, it ends after the last whole row that room holds and scrolls the rest, never less than about four rows; a row's menu with less room than that, from the keyboard or a right-click, moves up instead. A button's menu with less room than that below its button opens above it, where the room up to the window's top margin holds them, and otherwise on the roomier side with the whole rows that side holds: it never covers its own button, so a second click lands on the button and closes it. A resized window, as a tiling compositor re-tiles it, closes a button's open menu, whose side and rows were chosen for the old size; opened again, it takes the side the new size gives it. Moved up only far enough to clear the bar, a button's menu at 461 × 490 once lay wholly over its button. A row's menu that moves up goes wholly above its row, the keyboard's cursor row or the row a right-click pressed, its drawn edge clear of the row and at least 8 px from the bar, rather than sliding up just far enough to clear the bar, which once left a keyboard's menu over the row it acts on and a right-click's over the row it was opened on (`native_accessibility::PointerMenu`, in place of the kit's context menu, which only moved it into the window). A right-click's menu starts at the pointer, below it or above the row, and a resized window closes it, as it closes a button's menu. A text field's or editor's right-click menu (the commit title's and description's, a filter's), which Linux draws in the window and macOS shows as the system's own, never covers the point it was opened at, as a macOS context menu opens above the pointer where the room below cannot hold it: it opens just below the pointer where its items fit below it, and otherwise the whole menu lies just above the pointer, its drawn edge (the 1 px ring outside its fill) on the pixel row next to the pointer's either way, never on it, at a fractional scale such as 1.25 too, and 8 px or more above the bar and inside the window's edges, the edge counted, not only the fill, capped or not (`gpui_base::Positioner::point`; a menu capped at a room shorter than its first row filled it to the window's 8 px margin and drew its edge 7 px from the top), as the app's row menus keep their edge clear of their row, so the pixel clicked stays in sight and the pointer never starts inside the menu; with its fill at the pointer, its edge lay on the row clicked, and a pointer on a whole pixel, as X11 reports one, lay inside the menu's top edge; moved up only far enough to clear the bar, it once covered the line a right-click near the commit title's foot at 461 × 490 had pressed, the text the menu's Cut or Copy would act on. Where neither side holds its items, as for an editor's eight-entry menu (Go to Definition and Show Code Actions above Cut, Copy, Paste and Select All) opened mid-window at 18 pt in a 461 × 330 window, it opens on the side with more room, its items capped at that room and scrolled past it with the scrollbar a capped menu shows, and Down scrolls each item it reaches into view; clamped into the window, it lay over the point clicked. A right-click's press focuses the field it lands on before the menu opens, as on macOS, so the menu's Cut, Copy and Paste act on that field, never on the list or control that held focus before, and focus the menu holds returns to that field when an item, Escape or a resized window closes it. A resized window closes it, as it closes the other menus, since its side and its cap were chosen for the size it opened in. Its Cut, Copy, Paste and Select All are announced as the plain menu items they are, as on macOS, where an item without a mark is one; Linux once announced each as an unchecked check box. However a row's right-click menu closes, by Escape, an item, a press outside it or a resize, or with its row, as a quiet refresh takes away the row of a file deleted outside the app, focus it holds returns to the list or tree it was opened from, whose keys reach it again, as does focus a press outside gave the window's root (on the status bar or a heading), as a press outside a keyboard's menu returns it; an item that moved focus on, to the dialog it opened, or a press on another list or control keeps it there. A dialog a menu's item opens gives focus back, on Escape or as it closes, to the control that opened the menu: a button's menu to its button, whether a key or the pointer opened it, or, where that button is no Tab stop, as a project row's menu button that shows only on hover is and can be drawn at opacity 0, to its row's tree; a right-click's menu to where focus was at the right press, since a right press moves no focus and a row with no handle cannot take it; and a keyboard's menu to its list or tree. The Linux Main Menu moves focus back to where it opened from before its item runs, so its dialogs return there. Landing there by a key, the ring is whole on arrival; where that control has gone, focus falls as it does for any dialog whose opener has gone (decided 2026-11-09; the kit's `PopupMenu::return_dialogs_to_opener`). The menu had gone by the time the dialog closed, so its closing left focus nowhere and no key reached anything until a click. An Enter still down as any dialog closes waits for its key-up before anything in the window takes it, as an Enter that opened a dialog does: held past the repeat delay, the Enter that confirmed a dialog pressed the Button its close gave focus back to, and under X11, whose repeats are not marked held, it reopened a dropdown and ran the item under its cursor (decided 2026-11-09; the kit's `Root::close_dialog` and `Root::close_all_dialogs`, through `gpui_base::dialog_closing`). A press on a heading once left focus on the root, where nothing showed it, and a menu that went with its row left nothing focused, so Down, Space and Shift-F10 no longer reached the list. A Working Changes row's menu, from a right-click or the keyboard, belongs to its row's file, not its place in the list: where a quiet refresh after a file is committed, staged away, reverted or deleted outside the app takes another row away, the menu stays open on its own row, drawn beside it where it now stands, and its items act on its file, built again where the file's status changed, as an untracked file added and edited outside the app offers Discard changes… where it offered Delete untracked file…; where the refresh takes its own row away, the menu closes and focus returns to the list as above, the cursor, where that row held it, on the row that took its place, or on the one before where it was the last, within the row's own group, Staged or Unstaged; only where that group is now empty does it cross the group's heading, to the first row of the group that followed, else the last row of the group before (decided by the coordinator: the group says what Stage or Unstage would do, so the keys go on among changes of the same kind, and a neighbour taken from all the rows put the cursor on an unstaged file just after the last staged one went), as the navigator's cursor goes where a branch deleted under its menu leaves the tree and the project list's already went; it once restarted at the top, so Down selected the second row (decided by the owner's rule to keep focus where the person put it). The same holds wherever the cursor's row leaves Working Changes while the list or a menu opened from it has focus, and only then: with focus elsewhere, as in the commit message, nothing is selected in its place. Where focus is on a control in a row that leaves, as a row's Stage after Enter or Space, focus returns to the list as the rows without that row land, so the frame that first draws them draws the list's ring. The cursor stays with its file: on that file, it goes with it to its new group, as a read that finds the selected file staged moves the selection, so Enter again writes nothing; where the file leaves Working Changes, it takes the row that took its place, as above; on another row, it stays there. Kept on the control while its row had left, focus went to that row's Stage when a refresh brought the row back, and Enter staged the file again. Where other files stay selected, as after Command- or Control-clicks, only the cursor moves and the selection keeps them, so Stage selected acts on the files the person picked; chosen as a click chooses, the neighbour once replaced them, and so it does, from either, when its row leaves the part of the list drawn. History's commit menu from the keyboard belongs to its commit in the same way: where a quiet reload brings commits above it, it stays open and focused beside the row that holds its commit now, and it closes, focus back on the list, only once History no longer lists the commit, a selected commit the reload took away counting as gone though its row stays retained for the inspector; kept to the place it opened at, it closed as soon as the rows moved. A changed file's keyboard menu in History belongs to its file, by path, the same way: where the rows change under it, as a slow path filter's answer or the commit's files landing again replace them, it stays open beside the row that holds the file now, and it closes, focus back on the list, once the list no longer holds the file or another commit's files are shown; kept to the place it opened at, it stayed beside whichever file moved into it. A row cut only under its list's 1 px edge opens its menu where it lies, scrolling nothing. Built again as a write starts or ends, either is built from its file as it then stands; a keyboard's was built from the file as it opened, which brought back Delete untracked file… for a file a refresh had found added and edited. While the rows of a newer status or of the filter's query are prepared, the rows still drawn are the previous ones and a click or key on them acts on nothing, so either menu waits for the new rows before it is built again, except as a write starts or ends, which builds it at once with every item refused, and a right-click's opened on the previous rows offers its items only once the new ones land; built again as a Stage's status read landed, before its rows, a right-click's offered Delete untracked file… for a file the read had found added, and one that waited as a write started kept offering what that write refused until the rows landed. Refused for that wait, each item states why at its row's end, as any item a row cannot take states why (below): while a write runs, once it looks busy (200 ms into the window's busy spell, above, which a write started while the rows are prepared joins, so not 200 ms from its press), the write, by the label it runs under (“Staging file…”), as Fetch, Pull and Push name it; before then, and otherwise, what the rows are for, “Filtering…” for the filter's query or grouping, as the list says “Filtering working paths…” until its first rows land, or “Refreshing…” for a newer status. A menu that states why is built again as that changes: as the write's busy look shows, and, though no write starts or ends, as a newer status that lands while the filter's rows are prepared has the filter prepare that status's rows instead, so it says “Refreshing…” from then on; it once kept saying “Filtering…” until the rows landed, and a right-click on rows being prepared named a write as it started, inside the 200 ms delay in which a write that lands sooner shows none of its busy look, which runs from the busy spell's start, not the write's press. Opened then, a right-click's menu once showed every item dim with nothing to say why, and later said “Refreshing…” for a filter's rows and through a write as well, though the Changes header says “Refreshing…” only while a status is read. A menu opened before the rows began to be prepared keeps the items it was built with until a write starts or the new rows land: each acts on its file by path, and Discard changes… and Delete untracked file… review the file as it then stands before Git runs, refusing one whose status changed. A keyboard's menu keeps focus while a status read selects its row again, and closes, focus on the list, when a read moves the selection off its row, as one that finds its file staged moves it to the Staged group; it once stayed open over the row the selection had left. Every row's menu, a right-click's in any list (History's commits, the navigator, changed files, Working Changes) and a keyboard's beside a Working Changes row, also closes, focus returning to its list, when its row moves where the menu, sized for the room where it opened, fits neither below it nor wholly above it, rather than lying clamped over its row; one whose row has not moved stays where it opened. A keyboard's menu opens with its row scrolled into view, the least it needs, rather than pinned to the list's edge beside no row. The menu keys stop on an item that states why it is unavailable or waits, as on an item that can act, so its reason is heard: Up, Down, Home and End rest the keyboard's cursor on it, drawn there, its accessible name saying why, as a Working row's “Ignore…, untracked files only”, the branch picker's ticked current branch, “main, current branch” (once passed over, heard by its check alone), or its “review, another worktree. Open that worktree from Worktrees…”, which adds the way to that branch, and Enter does nothing there until it can act, and then only for a fresh press, while the next key moves on; a disabled item that states no reason is passed over, and a menu the keyboard opens still starts on its first item that can act (the kit's `PopupMenuItem::reason_stated`, set wherever the app builds such an item, `native_accessibility::detail_row`). A menu acts on Enter, on Space in a button's menu and on the secondary Enter only as the key goes down, never on the repeats a held key sends, on any item: Enter held on an item that waited once ran it on the first repeat after the menu was built again with it able to act, and Enter or Space held on a button ran the first item of the menu it opened, so a write now always waits for a new press (decided by the coordinator, from the QA89F-A security re-review; the kit's `PopupMenu::confirm_key`; an X11 session, whose GPUI backend marks no key held, still acts on a repeat). A button opens its menu the same way, on a fresh Enter or Space only: the key that chose an item, still held once the menu has closed and returned focus to its button, reopens nothing, where it once reopened the menu for a flicker (decided by the coordinator, 2026-11-03; the base kit's popover trigger, `open_key`). A row's menu built again, a right-click's or a keyboard's, keeps the keyboard's cursor on the item the keys left it on, found by its label wherever it now stands, and never moves it to another item. Where that item can no longer act but states why it is unavailable or waits, as while a write refuses it or once a conflicted file's Discard changes… reads “After conflict resolution”, the cursor stays on it, drawn there, and Enter does nothing until the item can act again. Only where that item is gone, or can no longer act and states no reason, does the cursor rest on none, and Enter does nothing until the item can act again, when the cursor returns to it, or a key or the pointer moves it; from rest, Up and Down start from the item's place (the kit's `PopupMenu::rebuild`). As a menu built again leaves the cursor resting on an item that can no longer act, assistive technology hears once, politely, why, the item's accessible name, as “Open project, available when fetching remote finishes”: as the rest begins, not again however often the menu is built while it lasts, again for a later rest, and nothing for a cursor the keys put on such an item, which they report selected with its reason in its name, for a menu whose selection the pointer owns, which keeps no cursor to rest, or for an item that states no reason (`native_accessibility::announces_resting`, from the window's polite announcement node, through the kit's `CursorRests`). Decided by the owner (keep focus where the person put it; say why something is unavailable): the cursor moved to another item once ran an action nobody chose, and a rest with nothing said left Enter silently doing nothing; polite rather than an alert, because nothing failed and the person's own action stands. A keyboard's Working row menu whose file becomes conflicted closes instead: its opening selected the row, so the conflicted file's preview focuses its resolution draft, and focus stays there. Once the pointer has taken the selection, or moved off the row that holds the cursor, a menu built again selects nothing, its first item included; and a menu that moves or grows under a pointer resting on or beside it, as a row's menu follows its row when rows arrive above it, a menu opened above its button changes height, or History's commit menu gains Amend last commit… and Undo last local commit… as its commit becomes HEAD, lets no item that arrives under the pointer take the selection: the pointer's selection stays while its item stays under the pointer, as when the menu moves sideways or by less than a row, and goes once its item moves out from under it, while a keyboard's cursor stays on its item, which no item arriving under a still pointer takes. A menu built again under a pointer resting on it, while the keyboard does not own the cursor, lights no item until the pointer moves, even where its rows stay put: a capped menu scrolled to its end and built again shorter keeps its size while its rows move down a row, and the branch switcher, built again as a write starts or ends, once gave the branch that came under the pointer the selection, which Enter checked out; nothing tells an item that came under the pointer from one that stayed, and the rebuild has already cleared the pointer's selection, so an item lit there would be one Enter does not run. Either way the pointer selects nothing until a key places the cursor or the pointer moves, which selects the item under it: a keyboard's menu built again once put the cursor on its first item under a pointer that had moved over it, so on a narrow Working row, the keys on Discard changes… and the pointer over it, a status change outside the app left it on Stage, and Enter staged the file; and the same menu, the pointer resting on Discard changes…, moved down with its row as files that sort above it changed outside the app, Stage arrived under the pointer and took the selection, and Enter staged the file again. A right-click's menu built again once lost its highlight, and a keyboard's put its cursor back on its first item that can act, so Enter just after a write ended could choose another item than the one the keys had reached; moved instead to the nearest item that could act, the cursor rested on an action the person had not chosen, as Stage once a file became conflicted, where Enter marked the file resolved, or a project's Rename project… while a write refused Open project, where it stayed after the write. Kept by its place, a right-click's menu once passed to the row after its own, and a keyboard's stayed where it opened beside whichever row moved there, so a menu whose file had gone still offered to act on it; kept as it opened, a menu offered to delete a file that was no longer untracked.
