A click acts only on the item its press went down on as the menu was last built: where a rebuild comes between the press and its release, or between the frame the press was aimed at and the press, the release acts on nothing, and the person clicks again; it once ran whatever item the rebuild put where the press went down, as one that took away an item above put the next one there (the kit's `PopupMenu::on_click`). Decided by the coordinator (a label is not an item's identity): kept across a rebuild where an item of the same label stood at its place, a press on the Changes Actions menu's Undo last local commit… reviewed the Undo of the commit a commit outside the app had just made HEAD, and a press on one of two project groups named Archive could run the other; a dropped click costs one more click, while running another target than the one pressed costs a review of a commit the person never chose. One menu is open at a time: Shift-F10 or the Menu key in an open right-click menu, already the row's, opens no keyboard's menu beside it, where the keyboard's menu once opened and closed again in the frame after, both drawn for that frame. A row's menu from the keyboard follows writes and pauses as a right-click's does: built again in place, focus kept in it, when a write starts or ends, a merge, rebase, cherry-pick or revert pauses or ends, or, a commit's, its commit becomes or stops being HEAD, so a commit's Revert, Cherry-pick, Amend and Undo read unavailable while an integration is paused, and Enter there says why in the window's error, announced at once, and leaves the menu open, while a commit HEAD leaves as it stays at its row, as a `git reset` outside the app with another branch still on it, no longer offers Amend or Undo; a branch's, a worktree's, a project's or a working row's menu, from the keyboard or a right-click, reads nothing HEAD changes and is not built again as HEAD moves, so its cursor stays where the keys left it, where a commit or a `git reset` outside the app once sent a keyboard's back to its first item and took a right-click's highlight away; built only as it opened, it offered Revert under a merge paused meanwhile, and Enter on it closed the menu. The Changes Actions menu, whose Amend last commit… and Undo last local commit… act on HEAD itself, is built again as HEAD moves to another commit, as a `git reset` outside the app moves it, so Enter on Amend reviews HEAD as it then stands: built from the HEAD it opened on, it reviewed the commit HEAD had left, which the review refused (“Amend and Undo apply only to the displayed current tip”). Built again, it keeps its keyboard's cursor on its item, as a Working row's menu does; a changed file's menu, whose File history… no write or pause changes, is built only as it opens. It closes, focus back on its list or tree, once its row no longer holds what it was opened on at that place, as an Undo or an Amend takes a commit from History's row; built again for the row, it offered Revert of the commit an Undo had left behind, over the new HEAD. The navigator's and the project list's rows are keyed by what they show, a branch by its name, a worktree or a project by its path and a group by its own identity, and their keyboard cursor stays on that branch, worktree, group or project as rows arrive above it or leave, so their keyboard's menu stays open on it, drawn beside its row where it now stands, and closes only once it leaves the list, deleted or renamed; History's commit rows are keyed by their commit and its changed files by their paths, so a right-click's menu on one, as on a navigator or project row, moves with its row as commits or files arrive above it. Keyed by their place, the cursor passed to the branch a refresh moved into it, a keyboard's menu on a branch created above closed, and a right-click's menu stayed beside the place, over the row that moved in, which its items then acted on, against the rule to keep focus where the person put it; History's keyboard menu closed once commits arriving above moved its row, though the selection followed its commit. Enter there asked again while paused says why again, the window's error a fresh node each time. History's commit menu ends at the commit list's trailing inset rather than starting at the row's leading one, so it leaves the graph's lanes in view. Its rows keep the height and spacing they have where the menu fits, so the room cuts no row, and while rows lie past that fold the menu shows the always-visible scrollbar a scrolling list cues its overflow with, in a gutter beside its rows that its thumb never crosses; a menu whose rows fit draws none. A wheel that scrolls a menu's rows under a pointer that stays put lights none of them until the pointer moves, as nothing the Changes rows move under a still pointer lights; the row the pointer had lit once kept its fill beside the row that arrived under it. An item a row cannot take reads as its command with the condition it waits for apart from it, in secondary text at the row's end where a shortcut stands (“Ignore…” and “Untracked files only”, “Discard changes…” and “After conflict resolution”, any Working row's item and why it waits while its rows are prepared, as “Filtering…”), as the command palette states an unavailable command's reason; assistive technology hears both. Every menu that refuses items while a write runs says why by its cause, through one helper (`native_accessibility::waiting_item`, read from `GitTurtle::menu_wait`): a commit's, a branch's, a worktree's, a project's and a Working row's menu, the Changes Actions menu and the branch picker's and the Git profile picker's menus, from a right-click, the keyboard or a button (the pickers' once refused their items with nothing to say why; decided 2026-11-01 by the coordinator, by the rule that a control says why it is unavailable, with MN1's in-room reasons so neither menu widens). Each item the write refuses shows the write's own label at its row's end in secondary text, as the window's progress line shows it (“Fetching remote…”), or “Refreshing…” once the write has ended and the reload it asked for has yet to land, and its accessible name says the same in words, “Open project, available when fetching remote finishes”, the label read as what finishes and a review another tab left to finish named by that review (“available when another tab's review finishes”). Decided by the owner (say why something is unavailable): these menus once refused their items with nothing to say why, and only a Working row's menu named the write, while its rows were prepared. The reason shows from the write's start, not after the busy look's 200 ms, because the items refuse and dim at once; the delay keeps a quick write from dimming controls the person did not touch, and a dim item with nothing to say why is what the rule forbids. It names the write by its label, not by a fixed list of operation names, so every write and GitHub request is named the way the progress line names it and no list can fall behind them. The reason takes only the room its command leaves at the row's end, never more: it ends in an ellipsis where that room is short and is left out of a row where the room cannot hold its first word whole and an ellipsis (“Fetching…”), the accessible name saying it whole, since “Fet…” or “Che…” says nothing, and the menu is exactly as wide with it as without it, so its edges, its rows and their commands stay where they were drawn as the reason comes, changes and goes. Decided by the coordinator (move nothing already drawn; the command is what the person reads first; the full reason is always in the accessible name): drawn whole beside the command, as a condition is, a long reason (“Checking rewrite publication destination…”) cut a 461 px menu's commands at 18 pt to “Rev…” and “Undo l…”, and the menu changed width as the reason came and went, which moved a right-click menu's edge. A condition of the item's own (“Untracked files only”) still stands whole beside its command, as a shortcut does, since it does not come and go with a write. An item refused for a reason of its own as well, as Save changes to stash… with nothing to stash or a commit's items while a merge is paused, states no write, since the write's end would not make it available. A Working row's menu names what its items wait for the same way while its rows are prepared, “Delete untracked file…, available when filtering finishes”, where it was announced “…, filtering…”, so every waiting item reads alike; it once refused what a write refuses with nothing at the row's end while its rows stood. The Git profile picker's menu states the reasons of its own the same way (decided 2026-11-06 by the coordinator, by the rule that a refused control says why): its items wait, named in the room their command leaves, for a profile save (“Saving profile…”), New profile… for the saved profiles' first read (“Loading profiles…”) and a saved profile for the read of the repository it is applied to, by that read's label (“Reading local history…”); where a read fails, New profile… reads “Storage unavailable” beside it, Retry profile storage below it reading them again, and a saved profile “Repository unavailable”; and the open menu is built again as that reason changes, as for a write (`AppDropdown::follows_refusal`). Those items were dimmed with nothing to say why, and stayed refused until the menu was opened again. `native_accessibility::open_menu`, `AppMenu`, `detailed_item` and `waiting_item` are the shared implementation.

A menu keeps one gap from what it opened from (coordinator's decision, 2026-10-30): 4 px of page at 13 pt, scaled with the interface text (5.5 px at 18 pt), between its drawn edge, the 1 px ring outside its fill, and the button or row beside it, on whichever side it opens: a button's dropdown below or above its button, the Linux Main Menu below its button, and a row's menu below or above its row, from the keyboard, or from a right-click where it moves wholly above the row (`appearance::popup_gap`, which the app sets as the toolkit's `Theme::popup_gap`). A row's menu keeps that gap rounded out to the device pixel the same way at every scale: below its row its fill starts on the first device pixel at or after the row's foot, the gap and its edge, and wholly above it ends on the last at or before the row's top less them (`native_accessibility::RowMenu::origin`), so its edge stands the gap from the row or less than a device pixel further, never nearer: at 18 pt, whose gap is 5.54 px, a keyboard's menu above or below a changed file in History's inspector stands 6, 5.67, 6.2, 5.67 and 6 px from it at scales 1, 1.2, 1.25, 1.5 and 2 (2026-11-03: left where GPUI's rounding put it, that menu above the row drew its edge 4.83 px from it at 1.2, a device pixel nearer than the gap, and 5.4 and 5.5 px at 1.25 and 2, and below it 5.4, 5 and 5.5 px at 1.25, 1.5 and 2). A dropdown kept the toolkit's quarter rem to its fill, its edge 2 to 2.5 px from its button at 13 pt and 3.5 px at 18 pt, while a row's menu drew its edge on its row, so the two kinds of menu stood apart differently from what they act on. A menu opened at a point, a right-click's below the pointer and a text field's, starts at the pointer, as macOS's context menus do, and keeps only its edge off the pointer's pixel row, its drawn edge starting on the device pixel row below the pointer's at any scale: a right-click's menu puts its fill on the first device pixel at or after that row's foot and its edge (`gpui_kit::base::device_pixel_at_or_after` in `native_accessibility::RowAnchor::pointer`, and kept on that device pixel through GPUI's rounding of its overlay's offset), and a text field's lies on the same device pixel, or above the pointer ends on the device pixel at or before the pointer's row less its edge (`gpui_kit::base::point_row_clear_by`, which the toolkit's positioner places it on). A right-click's menu below the pointer once had its fill at the pointer and drew its edge on the pixel row above it, over the row clicked; rounded out to a whole pixel, as both menus' were, at 1.2 (Hyprland's scale on a panel that cannot take 1.25) and 1.25 its fill started part way into a device pixel that GPUI rounds back towards the pointer, so its edge covered the pointer's own device row, and at 2 it left an empty device row between them (2026-11-02, a text field's the same evening). Nothing a menu draws reaches what it opened from: its soft shadow, which falls 4 px with a 3 px blur and so darkens the page about 9 px below its fill, further than the gap, is cut where the button, the row or the pointer's pixel row begins (`gpui_kit::component::ClearOfOpener`), so a menu opened above its button or row, or above a text field's pointer, no longer shades the control it was opened from: it laid about 9 % black over the first pixels of a row whose menu lay 1 px above it, or of the pixel row a text field's menu above the pointer lies next to, and about 4 % over the top of a button with its dropdown above it, and at the 4 px gap it would still lay 2 %, visible in a light palette. A text field's menu cuts it exactly where the pointer's device pixel row begins, wherever its placement put the fill: cut at its ring, it stopped up to two device pixels short of the row above the pointer at scale 2, while below it at 1.5 it reached the row. Between the ring and that row, nothing at scale 1 and at most a pixel at scale 2, the shadow's 8 to 10 % shows and ends at the row, a step of 1.19:1 to 1.26:1 on Daylight's panel and canvas, under the border rule's 1.3:1 and beside the ring's 1.4:1, a dropdown's cut 4 px further down at 2.6 % about 1.06:1. Cutting the shadow, rather than widening the gap above or casting the shadow upward, keeps the one gap and the light from above that every other raised surface shows; the menu's fill and drawn edge are never cut. Three cases are left as they fall (security review, 2026-11-01): a menu clamped over what it opened from, in a window too short for it on either side, has no side to cut its shadow on and takes no mask; a popover anchored at its trigger's side takes none, and no app menu opens so; and GPUI rounds a mask out to whole device pixels, so where the gap ends part way into one, as at 18 pt, whose 5.5 px gap can end on a half pixel at scale 1, the shadow's faint tail can reach one device pixel into the button or row. Every menu keeps one margin from the window's sides (coordinator's decision, 2026-11-01): its drawn edge, not its fill, stands 8 px in from the side it is moved in from, since the edge is what the eye reads as the menu's side: a button's dropdown, a row's menu, the Linux Main Menu, a text field's menu, a submenu and a menu bar's menu alike (`gpui_kit::component::POPUP_WINDOW_MARGIN`, the popup hosts keeping the fill `popup_fill_margin`, 9 px, in). That edge stands 8 px in to the device pixel at every scale (decided 2026-11-02, so that every menu's side reads at one distance from the window's): a popup host, and the app for a row's menu, puts a fill it moves in from a side on the device pixel at or inside its 9 px, counted from the window's side, and the ring covers the device pixels from 8 px in, 16 device pixels from the side at scale 2, 12 at 1.5 and 10 at 1.25. A right-click's menu clamped exactly 9 px in, between device pixels at 1.25, drew its edge 7.2 px from the side. Moved by whole pixels from a host laid out half a pixel into one, beside the Main Menu's button at 13 pt or the Settings profile menu's at 18 pt, a fill lay half a pixel further in and its edge 8.5 px from the side, 17 device pixels at scale 2; the fill moves that half pixel back, and nothing inside the menu moves within it. Each also keeps its edge 8 px from the window's top and above the status bar, on the device pixel there too where it is moved down or up to that margin (2026-11-02: moved by whole pixels, a popup host's menu clamped to the top at 1.25 drew its edge 7.2 px from it); one too tall for the room between those two margins keeps the margin above the status bar and gives up only as much of the top margin as it needs, where it fits between the window's top and the status bar's margin, and one taller still, which crosses that margin wherever it lies, keeps the top margin (2026-11-03), but a submenu and a menu bar's menu, which no app menu has, keep only the window's margin at their foot, and a menu bar's menu is moved in from a side or the top only once it runs past the window, by GPUI's anchor, which rounds it to the nearest pixel, so its edge stands 7.5 to 8.5 px in. A submenu that fits on neither side of its menu covers its parent, moved in until its edge stands 8 px from the window's side, as a macOS submenu overlaps its menu where the screen cannot hold it beside it (coordinator's decision, 2026-11-01): the whole submenu stays inside the window and readable, rather than cut at the window's side or turned onto the side with less room. A dropdown's, a row menu's and the Main Menu's fill stood 8 px in and their edge 7 px, beside a text field's menu whose edge stood 8 px in, and a submenu kept its fill only 4 px in, and only once the corner it hangs from ran past the window.

The graph and file list must scroll the selected row into view during keyboard navigation, and again when the window or the text size changes the list's size or its rows' height, only as far as needed. A write's landing centres the cursor's row only where that row was in view before the rows landed and they moved it out of view, so it lands with context around it; where the person had scrolled the list away from the cursor's row, the list keeps its offset, so a pressed row that lands in that view keeps its confirmation in view, and one that lands outside it is still announced and in the status line; a heading whose Stage all or Unstage all confirms moves the list by the least that shows it, the cursor having no say (decided 2026-11-08: feedback stays in place, and the rule for the selected file covers a row the person could see; with the cursor on row 5, a Stage pressed at row 50 moved the list back to row 5). A change made outside the app that adds or takes away rows leaves such a list on the rows the person was reading, and brings a selected row they could see back into view only as far as needed (decided 2026-11-08: a passive read never moves a list to a cursor the person scrolled away from). A read from outside the app, Refresh's or the local watcher's, does so where the rows' number stays too: where the selected file's row was in view before the rows landed, the list moves only as far as shows it at its new row, to the nearest edge (decided 2026-11-09: one file leaving Unstaged for Staged keeps their number, and a watcher's read left the selected row out of view). Where the rows' number stays, the quiet read a write asks for keeps the list on the rows being read and only the selection follows; where their number changes, it goes as a change made outside the app does. Whichever read moves it, a write's own or one from outside, the selected file stays selected at its new row; in a multi-selection only the cursor's file follows. A read whose entries did not change never moves the Working list, whatever started it: Refresh still reports what it read, and a Fetch that found nothing lands as a quiet read, which never moved the list (decided 2026-11-09: a Refresh that found nothing centred a selected row the person had scrolled away from). When the window or the text size changes, History also shows the selected commit's lane: a window narrowed from 1,480 to 461 px wide keeps the selected row, its subject and its node in view, though narrow History's graph holds only the whole lanes its room fits, which follow the font and can be as few as one ([History mode](#history-mode)): the selection brings its lane among them. In every scrolling list whose rows or row controls take focus (among them the branch chooser, the remote manager, Tags, the tag inspector's Push to… list, the Reflog's entries, the worktree manager's branch choices and the Settings Your themes rows), and every scrolling dialog body that holds them (the Reflog's content, which shows the whole entry list while the list shows the entry, the branch chooser's content, which shows its whole list the same way, the worktree manager's content, which shows its worktree list the same way and the selected worktree's details below it, and in Create every field and action of its form, its branch choices shown the same way, the Stashes detail, and the Git profiles dialog's body and the profile form), when the keyboard moves focus (Tab, Shift+Tab or any other key) onto a control that lies wholly or partly outside the list, the list scrolls by the least amount that shows the whole control plus the focus ring's room (`appearance::button_ring_room`, 3 px at the default), to the nearest edge, with no centring and no animation, in the frame that first draws the focus, so no painted frame shows the control cut and reduced motion needs nothing. A pointer click, which leaves focus where it was, and a redraw without a focus change scroll nothing, so a list the user scrolled away from a focused row stays put. `focus_reveal::FocusReveal` is the shared implementation. The project hub's page uses it with its own rule: it reveals a focused form field 12 px clear of the page's edges, in the same frame, whether the keyboard or a click moved focus there. Virtualized lists (History, the file lists, the command palette, the project pane) keep one tab stop and move a selection with keys instead. So does the Reflog's entry list: its one stop is the entry the keyboard is on, else the selected entry, else the first, which draws the Button's whole ring; Up, Down, Home and End select the entry they reach and move focus onto it, Space and Enter select the entry as a click does, and Tab from the list reaches the selected entry's details (Copy commit ID) next, Shift+Tab the filter. Working Changes' list keeps one stop too (decided 2026-11-07), and Tab from it reaches what acts on the list rather than every row: the selected row's Stage or Unstage, where the row draws one, then each group heading's Stage all or Unstage all that is available, in the list's order, then the composer, and Shift+Tab walks back the same way. With a merge or rebase paused no composer stands after the list, so Tab from the last control wraps to the window's first stop at rest, and Shift+Tab from that stop walks back the same way. Every other row's control, where drawn, is out of the Tab order, still clickable and named for assistive technology. A press on one takes no focus, as no Button's does; one still holding focus after the selection has left its row counts as standing in the list, so Tab goes to the first stop and Shift+Tab to the list. When the selection moves the stop moves with it: with a row's or a heading's control holding focus, an arrow key selects the row it reaches and moves focus onto that row's control, as the Reflog's entries do, or back to the list where the rows draw no control or that row is still landing from its flight, so no key ever leaves focus on a control the frame does not draw; only the person's own scrolling can take it out of the frame, and the list's accent edge shows where focus is meanwhile. The control that takes focus is revealed as this section says, whole with its ring's room by the least scroll that shows its row, in the frame that first draws the focus, so a partly drawn row's control never takes focus with its ring cut and a control the list has scrolled out of view is drawn as it takes focus; where the list and the composer scroll together the body returns to its top, where the list stands (`GitTurtle::tab_working_row_stops`). Leaving the stops, by Tab from the last control on to the composer or by Shift+Tab from the first back onto the list, ends with the selected row brought back into view by the least scroll that shows it, where a heading's reveal took it out of view and the control holding focus is still drawn; a list the wheel has moved away from that control keeps its offset (decided 2026-11-08: the selection stays in view through the walk's end, and a reveal never undoes manual scrolling). Tab once met every drawn row's control in the rows' order, a press a row in a long list, and took a row half drawn at the list's foot with its ring cut. Focus belongs to the row's identity, never its place: a Reflog entry by its line (its reference, both object IDs, its time and its message), a line written twice in one second told apart by how many like it lie below it in the loaded log, which neither a line written above it nor the filter changes (counted by those above it, a look-alike the filter let in above the focused entry took its focus), a Tags row by its tag's name, so entries that come in above the focused one, as the filter empties, leave focus and its ring on the same entry, and the keys go on from there (keyed by place, focus passed to whichever entry moved into its place). A click on an entry takes the keyboard's place with it where focus was on another entry, so the arrows go on from the entry clicked, or on the selected commit's message, name or Review recovery branch…, which the selection takes away until its commit lands; focus in the filter stays there, and the commit's landing leaves the content where it is: it scrolled the content back up to the filter, so where the content had scrolled past the filter the list moved down from under the pointer. A Button takes no focus on a press, so the arrows went on from the entry the keyboard had left, and focus on the commit's message stayed on an editor no frame drew. Every entry was a stop, so from a selected entry of a 40-entry log Copy commit ID took 41 presses. A selected entry's read shows its loading line and Cancel in the details below the list, where its commit lands, and leaves Read log available, so selecting with the arrows never moves the list; the line stood above the list, which moved down 37.5 px (13 pt, 1000 × 680) until each read landed. The error such a read ends with, as for a commit pruned since Git logged it, stands there too, below the entry's details, rather than above the list, which it moved down with each failed read, and as it lands the content scrolls by the least amount that shows it, focus staying on the entry: at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 half tile it lay below the content's fold, and nothing in view said the read had failed. Where focus leaves the read's Cancel for the entry as the error lands, the content shows the entry, which draws its ring, instead, and focus stays there; the error, which can then lie below the fold, since at 18 pt in that half tile the entry and its error cannot both show, is announced politely, once, as it lands, from the window's announcement node rather than from its line, which assistive technology may not reach while it lies out of view, rather than leaving a failure nothing in view or in speech reported. Each error is revealed once, as it lands, so a later scroll, the user's or a key's, stays where it takes the content. The recovery review's loading line and error stand below Review recovery branch…, the button that asked for it, and leave Read log available: a branch name already taken shows “Branch '…' already exists. Choose a new recovery branch name.” there, the content scrolling by the least amount that shows it, and the review's landing no longer scrolls the content back to the filter. That error stood above the list, which it moved down, far from the button and out of view where the content had scrolled down to it. Held, the arrows select each entry they reach as they go but read none of them until they stop: the arrow's own release, or 150 ms without a repeat, reads the entry they rest on (another key's release, as of a key held since before the arrow, leaves the arrow repeating and reads nothing, where it started a read the arrow's next repeat cancelled), once any read still running, which their first repeat cancels, has answered, and the entries passed over are never read. A single press reads at once, cancelling a read still running, which its own then waits for. Every press queued a read on the browser's reader, whose queue holds eight, so a key held while a read was slow failed the presses after the eighth with “Background executor is busy…”; later, while the key repeated, each reply started the latest entry's read and the next repeat cancelled it, one Git process started and stopped per repeat. The project hub's recent projects are a `uniform_list` whose unrendered rows Tab cannot reach. The repository navigator and the project list pane share the Up/Down/Home/End, Left/Right, Enter/Space and Shift-F10 tree bindings. Search keystrokes should not trigger list navigation. Native text selection/copy behavior takes precedence in diff content. Escape in an editor find box closes that box before a subsequent Escape returns to History. Back to history preserves the history query; it is not a Clear search action. Primary + Q retains platform quit behavior.

A scrolling list of tab-stop rows shows the always-visible scrollbar that cues overflow in the review dialogs (Reflog's content and changed files, the discard and forced worktree removal reviews) and in the commit inspector's message. The branch chooser, the remote manager, Tags, the tag inspector's Push to… list, the Reflog's entries, the worktree manager's worktree list and branch choices, the Git profiles dialog's body and the profile form, the Stashes dialog's saved files and the Working inspector's short body, where its file list and the commit composer scroll together as one area, each keep, while their rows overflow, a gutter of the 16 px track plus the focus ring's 3 px of room at the right of their rows. In the Working body the track lies over the rows' trailing inset rather than beside it: the file list and the composer keep the body's whole width, the list's edge, its focus ring, running beneath the track 3 px outside the thumb the track paints 4 px in from its right, and each row in it, a file row, a section heading and each of the composer's, keeps the gutter less its inset at its right, so its content and its controls' rings end where the track begins (decided 2026-11-07: beside the whole gutter, “README.md” was drawn 76.5 px wide for its 89.7 at 18 pt in a 430 × 490 window). It fits what it holds, the file rows, the section headings, the title's counter, the description's note and Commit's label, to the width that leaves in the frame that keeps the gutter, so nothing in it is laid out too wide and cut at the track (at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window, a 156.5 px inspector, its track holds a thumb and the content of its rows ends 19 px short of its right); the file list draws no scrollbar of its own in its row floor, so the body's track is the only one beside it, and the floor fades at each edge its rows continue past, in the panel its rows lie on, as a list too short for a thumb fades (below), with none at an end it is scrolled to (decided 2026-11-07: its rows past the floor once scrolled into view with nothing to say they were there), and like those fades stops short of the ring of the row control holding focus, a file's Stage or Unstage or a heading's Stage all or Unstage all at the floor's edge (decided 2026-11-07: the foot's fade lay 3 px over a focused Unstage's ring there at 18 pt in a 640 × 680 window). In the dialogs' lists the track runs in that gutter inside the list, clear of the room the list keeps around its rows and of the dialog's side padding, where the dialog's own scrollbar runs, so its thumb follows the list's offset and covers no row and no focus ring; the Working body's track, which lies over its rows' trailing inset (above), paints no fill, so only its thumb lies over the rows, 3 px clear of every ring (decided 2026-11-07: filled with the canvas, it hid the list's ring's right side, the selected row's end and the composer's divider for its whole height). A list whose rows fit draws no track, and its rows keep their full width. The branch chooser, the worktree manager's content, the Git profiles dialog's body and the profile form lie in their dialog's content rather than its scrolling body, whose scroller lays what it holds out at full height: each takes what the dialog's cap leaves it above the footer and scrolls the rest with its own cue, the chooser's list no shorter than two whole rows (decided 2026-11-05: laid out at full height in a 461 × 490 half tile, the chooser's list ran below the dialog's fold, where a row Tab moved onto took focus unseen, and the profiles dialog's body and form ran past it with no cue; the worktree manager's content, sized from the window's height, ran 16 px past it at 18 pt in a 1480 × 800 window). Where the chooser's detail, its filter and its list's floor exceed what the cap leaves, as at 18 pt in a 461 × 400 window, its content scrolls as a whole too, cued by its own scrollbar in the dialog's side padding as the worktree manager's is, and a row Tab moves onto brings the whole list into view, so the row the list reveals stays whole (the dialog clipped the list's foot there, and that row with it). The GitHub panel's scrolling body cues its fold the same way: while what it holds runs below the fold, the track runs in that gutter at its content's right, inside the room the body keeps for its controls' rings (at 18 pt the body once hid what lay below its fold with no cue). Its content keeps the gutter whether or not it overflows, as a stable scrollbar gutter does, and a body that fits draws no track there: a PR's section tabs, opening a PR and a reply's composer change how much the body holds, and nothing already drawn moves when they do (kept only while the body overflowed, the gutter moved the content's right edge, its Buttons and the draft status 19 px between Overview and Files at 13 pt in a 1,480 × 980 window). The gutter is a column of the body, as a macOS view with always-shown scrollbars keeps one, so the dialog's footer, which aligns to the panel's edge, ends 19 px past the content's right (coordinator's decision, 2026-11-01). Edit local commits' body keeps no gutter: its scrollbar runs in the dialog's side padding, as the worktree manager's content's does, so the body keeps its full width and Load commits keeps its place in the form, never shifting sideways, as the commits it loaded make the body overflow (kept only while the body overflowed, a gutter moved Load commits 19 px to the left as they arrived, at 18 pt in a 461 × 490 window; kept always, it left the one permanent empty column of any dialog; decision, 2026-11-07). The top line that stands above the body beside an open conversation keeps the same right inset, its own scrollbar drawn in it, and its divider spans the panel, so a notice keeps its width and its wrapped lines as it moves there from among the content (at the panel's full width it was 19 px wider pinned and could re-wrap). Every such list, a document side's header controls and a model's details among them, draws no scrollbar where its track is shorter than the toolkit's shortest thumb and its insets, an unscaled 56 px, measured along the track the scrollbar lies on, the list less the room it keeps around its rows: there the thumb ran out of its track against the scroll or moved a few pixels at most, as in a side header's controls at their 36–44 px floor, where the 48 px thumb filled its track. It keeps the cue as a soft fade at each edge its rows continue past, the bottom's at its top, both part way down and the top's alone at its end, none where its rows fit: as macOS lets content dissolve beneath a window's edge, the rows there fade into the surface rather than end at a cut. Its rows keep the gutter while they overflow, as beside a scrollbar, so nothing it holds moves as the list grows or shrinks past that length: without it, a document header's controls wrapped wider and moved up a line when a click's answer shortened the header past it. Each fade is a band as wide as the list, opaque at the edge in the theme's background colour, the canvas that panes and dialogs are drawn on, and clear inward, a scaled 8 px tall, about the room a 28 px control keeps above and below its label with its ring's, so a control at the edge keeps its label clear in light and dark palettes alike, and never more than a quarter of the list, so its middle half reads unfaded (8 px at 13 pt and 10 px at 18 pt in a 40 px list). It is drawn over the rows and catches no pointer, so a wheel over it scrolls the list and a row under it keeps its hover and click. It is decided as the frame is painted, so it comes and goes in the frame that scrolls the list, resizes it or brings rows that overflow it, one frame per input, with no motion of its own; decided as the list rendered, rows that arrived drew no fade until something else drew the list again. The length is judged then too: a list grown long enough for a thumb draws no fade in the frame before its scrollbar comes (Tags, the branch chooser, the GitHub panel's body and the Reflog's entries once showed one for a frame as they opened), and one shrunk below it fades in the frame that shrinks it and draws no scrollbar there, for the eye or the pointer (its bar from the frame before once painted over the new fade for that frame, still catching a click). A fade never obscures a focus indicator: where the ring of the control holding focus, the ring's room outside its edge, reaches into a fade's band, as a control Tab reveals at an edge does, the fade at that edge ends where the ring begins, its gradient kept over the height left, or draws none where the ring reaches the edge, so the ring and the label inside it stay whole at 13 and 18 pt in light and dark palettes, and the fade returns whole in the frame focus leaves. The reveal keeps its ring's room alone rather than also a fade's height: at a side header's floor a 28 px control (about 39 px at 18 pt) cannot keep a fade's height clear of both edges, as in the model header's 40 px region at 13 pt, and a reveal that kept the fade clear at the edge it brought the control to left it within the other edge's fade, where the reveal's second pass in that frame scrolled it back, 3 px from the edge; the fade once lay over that control's ring. Focus is what the person is acting on, so its indicator wins over the cue for as long as it is there. A list whose track holds a thumb that moves draws its scrollbar as before and no fade, so nothing already drawn moves. A list too short for a thumb still scrolls under a wheel and as Tab reveals a control, and an assistive technology still finds the scroll view that clips its rows, with no scrollbar element left behind. `tags::ScrollCue` is the shared implementation.

Every scrollbar's thumb, the only cue that more content lies past a list's or a body's edge (`tags::ScrollCue`'s lists, the dialogs' and pickers' bodies and every other scrollbar the toolkit draws, one app-wide token, the toolkit's `scrollbar_thumb`), holds at least 2:1 against each surface a track lies on, the canvas, the panel, `subtle` and the raised surface the pickers' tracks rest on, in every shipped palette, light and dark, both as fitted and as drawn a channel step nearer that surface, the rounding a colour can take on its way to the screen: the palette's `border` where that already holds, and otherwise the least step toward the text side that holds it (`Palette::scrollbar_thumb`; decision, 2026-11-02: fitted to the token alone, Midnight's #444f5f held 2.01:1 on its raised panel and drew #434e5e, 1.98:1, on Keyboard Shortcuts' track). It stays well under secondary text's 4.5:1, which its hovered state takes, and keeps its place and size, so it still never covers a focus ring (decision, 2026-11-01: in `border` it stood 1.52:1 off Midnight's canvas, 1.39:1 off its panel and 1.28:1 off Braden's canvas, too faint for the one cue of more content). The one thumb drawn over rows rather than on a track, the Working inspector's short body's, whose track lies over the list's rows and paints no fill (`tags::ScrollCue::over_rows`), takes a token of its own, `Palette::over_rows_thumb`: it rests on the panel and on a hovered, selected and hovered selected row, where the app-wide thumb, fitted to the track's surfaces, missed 2:1 in every shipped palette (Midnight's `#45505F` stood 1.46:1 on its selected row, `#223B3B`). It is the least step from the app-wide thumb toward the text that holds at least 2:1 against all four, judged by WCAG's luminance ratio as fitted and as drawn a channel step nearer, never by an OKLab distance alone, which a saturated hue can meet at the row's own lightness, or, where no step toward the text holds, as where the text itself reads under 2:1 on a row, the least step toward white in a dark palette or black in a light one, as the app-wide thumb's is, never the text; it stays under secondary text's contrast there, which its hovered and dragged states take as every thumb's do (decision, 2026-11-07). The hovered selected row is a fourth surface beyond the panel, a hovered row and a selected row, and the strictest of the four, the one that decides the step in every shipped palette; the token is fitted to it on purpose, since the pointer that hovers a selected row rests beside the thumb. The section headings' `subtle` surface, which the track lies over too, needs no fit of its own: the app-wide thumb is already fitted to it, and the token, a step from that thumb away from the surfaces, holds 2:1 there in every shipped palette. Every other thumb keeps the app-wide token. ## Review checklist

Choose checks for the affected surface. History/comparison checks use merges, long paths, non-ASCII text, large commits, and relevant image/missing-content cases. Exercise commit → file → another file → Back, including a repository-wide search, a scrolled graph, file-history pages, and a rename boundary. Verify focus, Escape precedence, stale-preview rejection, pane resizing, chosen-column alignment, and clipping at regular/narrow widths in each affected theme. Check Split scrolling and Find from both sides and literal selection/copy across alignment blanks. Project, partial staging, commit, branch, recovery, and network tests use disposable repositories and local remotes; check exact destinations, stale plans, failures/conflicts, and unrelated staged/unstaged content. Settings checks include reopening, failed persistence, identity scope, and retained editor focus. External file/Git changes must refresh without disturbing the current interaction. Record the build and observed results separately; a screenshot is not performance evidence.

## Platform appearance and evidence

The [everyday feature semantics](docs/macos-features.md) describe attribution, source-aligned image overlay/wipe, system appearance, native menus and local editor/Finder handoff. [Tags and contextual ignore](docs/macos-git-actions.md) use captured review dialogs and the existing operation feedback. [Authentication](docs/authentication.md) preserves configured Git mechanisms with transient native prompts and cancellation. Projects uses its own single header; repository context returns through Back.

The [Liquid Glass prototype](docs/liquid-glass-investigation.md) exposed a pinned-renderer compositing limitation. Current navigation stays opaque in every built-in theme; no ordinary transparency is labelled native glass. The [earlier macOS milestone](docs/macos-milestone.md) retains its historical findings. The [active consistency milestone](docs/consistency-milestone.md) records the current native review, including presentation/focus corrections and remaining checks, against actual builds.

## Review and recovery controls

The current-branch menu and native View menu expose occasional comparison, Quick Open, activity, worktree and reflog workflows. Keep explicit Before → After labels and resolved commit identities visible. Since-branching comparisons identify the merge base; unavailable or ambiguous ancestry needs an actionable explanation. Quick Open presents a tracked source file without synthetic Added/Diff status and returns to its preceding inspection. Both modal searches accept keyboard input immediately, suppress duplicate acceptance, cancel obsolete reads and restore destination focus after closing.

Diff review controls remain together above text: whitespace suppression, bounded context, reset, and previous/next change. The context control leads with what it does and shows the unchanged lines each hunk has now, **More context (3 lines)**, stepping through 3, 12, 48 and 192 lines and disabled at the last; its accessible name says the same in words ("More context, 3 lines shown") and its tooltip names the step it takes. In a preview column narrower than their row needs as it first appears, at the interface text size and measured from the interface font (the five labelled buttons, "More context (3 lines)" among them, and the caption "Showing the original Git diff"), they collapse: Previous change and Next change keep only up and down arrows with their tooltips and accessible names, and Hide whitespace, More context and Reset review move into an Options menu with a caret, whose tooltip carries their explanations; Hide whitespace is a checked item, More context keeps the button's label and accessible name and increases it, and Reset keeps its disabled state. The caption follows the Options button only where its whole text fits there, measured from the interface font as the breakpoint is; in a narrower column it leaves the row and closes the Options menu as a label below a separator, so the row never shows a cut caption such as "Showing the orig…". Each caption says what the review shows ("Showing the original Git diff", "Review filter active · exact source retained", "Expanded context · partial staging unavailable", and "Review unavailable", in the modified captions' colour, while a failed review's error replaces the diff, since no diff is shown then), with the same words in the row, in the menu and as its accessible name, so at the menu's end it reads as state rather than as the title of an empty group. The collapsed controls take one row even in the narrowest column, 176 px at the default interface size and scaled with it, at 18 pt too; a column too narrow for them wraps them rather than clipping. The breakpoint uses those first labels, so choosing an option never switches between the two layouts. The caption takes a small button's text inset, given back by the control before it, so beside the buttons it does not move and on a line of its own its text starts where the buttons' labels do. Active review variants explicitly disable partial staging; code/source copying stays literal. Interface and code sizes have independent reset controls in Settings. Scale text, gutters and relevant rows together while preserving each pane's scroll and selected content.

Working Changes names the count and area of selected files. Command-toggle and Shift-range selection use visible paths. Filtering/grouping clears selection predictably; all-files actions are disabled while a path query is active. Optional directory labels help large lists without creating implicit selected subtrees.

Worktree creation/removal and recovery branch creation review their exact target and consequences. Distinguish worktree removal from branch deletion and report retained branches after partial checkout failures. Activity shows local operation outcomes and controlled concise explanations, with explicit next actions and no automatic retry. The Reflog dialog is 740 px wide and never wider than the window less 48 px, so a half tile keeps it whole. Its entries are list rows, not centred button labels: the dialog's text starts at one inset, the 11 px at which its scope and filter fields start their text: the title, the explanation and the other labels, each entry's text and the changed files. An entry's text ends in an ellipsis where the entry is too narrow, and only then carries a tooltip with the whole entry; its accessible name is always the whole entry; the selected entry keeps the selected surface and its toggled state. It opens with focus in its filter field, drawn with the field's accent edge and ring whole, and Escape returns focus to where it was opened. Tags and the worktree manager follow the same rule: their title, summary or labels, notices and each row's name start at their filter's 11 px text inset, a worktree row's inside its 1 px border, where the kit centred each row's name; Tags' filter fits its placeholder in whole words (“Filter tags…” at 18 pt in a half tile). The manager's list keeps two whole worktrees before the content scrolls, and cues the rest with its scrollbar, as its content does beside it. See [parallel work and reflog recovery](docs/parallel-work-recovery.md).

[Conflict blocks](docs/conflict-blocks.md) expose current/incoming/base meaning, unresolved counts, navigation, both-side choices and a manual result. **Save draft** leaves the index conflicted; **Save and stage result** requires a reviewed result without recognized unresolved markers. [Interactive rebase](docs/interactive-rebase.md) reviews an exclusive base and at most 100 linear commits, with native message editing and focused keyboard reorder/action controls. Its form opens with focus in the base field, drawn with the field's accent edge and ring whole, or, reopened on a sequence it already loaded, on that sequence, whose 1 px edge turns accent after a key, as History's lists do. Label the stable rebase base separately from the replayed original commit. Git message pauses and conflicts remain distinct, and the interface must explain unsupported histories and uncertain intermediate outcomes.

Unfinished conflict-result and rebase-message drafts use visible pending/error feedback and explicit restoration after their captured source identities are revalidated. Stale drafts keep recoverable text without blindly applying it. [Conflict drafts](docs/conflict-blocks.md) and [rebase messages](docs/interactive-rebase.md) specify the respective flows. The [rewritten-series review](docs/rewritten-series.md) shows original/replayed correspondence and ambiguity before a separate publication review. Publication names one remote branch and reviewed old/new OIDs with an exact lease; completing local rebase never publishes automatically.
